\chapter{CY-C beyond \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: existence and obstruction}
\label{ch:cy-c-beyond-k3e}

\section{Global NCCR obstruction on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} and the Serre-equivariant substitute}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-global-nccr-obstruction}

The existence/obstruction dichotomy of this chapter sits, on the isotrivial-fibration
stratum, atop a prior structural fact: although $K3 \times E$ is the canonical compact
CY$_3$ on which a pentagon of constructions converges at Hopf-algebra level
(Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}), the total space admits no global Van den
Bergh non-commutative crepant resolution. The failure is not a single missing
generator but a five-fold obstruction, each summand of which kills an independent
would-be construction. The replacement is a Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR, a sheaf
of tilted algebras over $\Stab(D^b(K3 \times E))$ whose positive-half character is
the reciprocal Igusa cusp form $-1/\Phi_{10}$ of Oberdieck--Pandharipande.

\begin{theorem}[Global \NCCR\ obstruction on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-global-nccr-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusTheorem
The compact Calabi--Yau threefold $K3 \times E$ admits no global Van den Bergh
non-commutative crepant resolution in the sense of Van den Bergh 2004 (arXiv:math/0207170,
\S 4.1): there is no reflexive module $M \in \mathrm{ref}(\mathcal{O}_{K3 \times E})$
with $\End_{\mathcal{O}_{K3 \times E}}(M)$ of finite global dimension tilting
$D^b\Coh(K3 \times E)$ to a module category of a Noetherian algebra. Five independent
obstructions are each separately sufficient:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \textbf{K3 Serre duality obstruction.} For any coherent $T$ on the $K3$ factor
        carrying a tilting candidate, $\omega_{K3} = \mathcal{O}_{K3}$ and Serre duality
        give $\Ext^2_{K3}(T, T) \simeq \Ext^0_{K3}(T, T)^{\vee} \supseteq k \neq 0$.
        The tilting axiom $\Ext^{>0}(T, T) = 0$ (Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003
        arXiv:math/0204218 Definition~2.2.1) fails at degree~$2$ already on the fibre.
  \item \textbf{Hodge-forecloses-exceptional-collections obstruction.} $h^{2,0}(K3) = 1$
        carries a non-zero holomorphic symplectic form; by Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003
        Remark~3.2.3 (see also Kapranov 1988 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 92), compact varieties
        with $h^{2,0} > 0$ admit no full strong exceptional collection in $D^b\Coh$.
        The Kapranov-style route to \NCCR\ (descent of a tilting object on a minimal
        resolution chart) is foreclosed on $K3$ globally, hence on $K3 \times E$ by
        K\"unneth.
  \item \textbf{Elliptic-factor Fourier--Mukai-not-tilting obstruction.} The elliptic
        curve $E$ is a derived self-equivalence by the Poincar\'e bundle (Mukai 1981
        \emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.} 81, Theorem 2.2), $D^b\Coh(E) \simeq D^b\Coh(E^{\vee})$,
        but $E$ admits no tilting object: $\Ext^1_E(\mathcal{O}_E, \mathcal{O}_E) = H^1(E,
        \mathcal{O}_E) = k$ is non-zero. Fourier--Mukai autoequivalence survives where
        tilting equivalence fails.
  \item \textbf{K\"unneth-forces-both-factors obstruction.} A tilting object
        $T_{K3 \times E}$ on the product would by K\"unneth have the form
        $T_{K3} \boxtimes T_E$ up to Fourier--Mukai twist; the vanishing
        $\Ext^{>0}_{K3 \times E}(T_{K3 \times E}, T_{K3 \times E}) = 0$ factors through
        the K\"unneth summand $\Ext^i(T_{K3}, T_{K3}) \otimes \Ext^j(T_E, T_E)$
        (Beauville 1983, \emph{Vari\'et\'es K\"ahleriennes} \S 1). The product-level
        tilting axiom forces a tilting object on each factor; both fail by (i)--(iii).
  \item \textbf{ADE-chart atlas blocked by $(-2)$-spherical twists.} Local ADE charts
        $U_\alpha \subset K3$ (McKay resolutions of ADE singularities on $K3$ with
        finitely many $(-2)$-curves in each chart; Kirillov 2016 \emph{Quiver
        representations and quiver varieties} \S 2) carry local \NCCR{}s
        $\Lambda_\alpha = \End_{U_\alpha}(M_\alpha)$ by Van den Bergh 2004 Theorem~A.
        Global descent along $\{U_\alpha\} \to K3$ requires the local
        Van den Bergh equivalences to intertwine on overlaps. The overlap cocycle is
        generated by Seidel--Thomas spherical twists $T_{(-2)}$ (Seidel--Thomas 2001
        \emph{Duke Math.\ J.} 108 Theorem~1.2) around the $(-2)$-curves of $K3$;
        these are Calabi--Yau-2 autoequivalences with infinite-order action on
        $K^0(K3)$ (Bridgeland 2008 arXiv:math/0307164 Theorem~5.3). No locally
        constant atlas of \NCCR{}s is compatible with this infinite-order spherical
        monodromy, so the ADE-chart-by-chart construction does not glue globally.
\end{enumerate}
Each of \textup{(i)}--\textup{(v)} is separately sufficient to kill global \NCCR\ on
$K3 \times E$; the five mechanisms are logically independent.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i).} If $T$ on $K3$ is tilting, $\Ext^i_{K3}(T, T) = 0$ for $i > 0$. Serre duality
for a smooth compact $K3$ surface reads $\Ext^i_{K3}(T, T) \simeq \Ext^{2-i}_{K3}(T, T
\otimes \omega_{K3})^{\vee}$ (Hartshorne 1977 \emph{Algebraic Geometry} III.7.6).
Since $\omega_{K3} = \mathcal{O}_{K3}$, $\Ext^2_{K3}(T, T) \simeq \Ext^0_{K3}(T, T)^{\vee}
= \End_{K3}(T)^{\vee}$, non-zero whenever $T \neq 0$. Tilting fails at degree~$2$.

\emph{(ii).} A full strong exceptional collection $(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$ in $D^b\Coh(X)$
has $\Ext^{>0}(E_i, E_i) = 0$, hence all $E_i$ are rigid. By Kuznetsov 2009
(arXiv:math/0511109, Theorem 3.1) and Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003 Remark~3.2.3, a compact
smooth variety with $H^{2,0}(X) \neq 0$ admits no full strong exceptional collection:
the holomorphic symplectic form provides a non-zero class in $\Ext^2(\mathcal{O}_X,
\mathcal{O}_X)$ that any exceptional collection must produce via the $\Ext^2$-pairing
among its objects, contradicting $\Ext^{>0}$-vanishing. $h^{2,0}(K3) = 1$ is the defining
numeric invariant of a $K3$ surface (Barth--Hulek--Peters--Van de Ven 2004 \emph{Compact
Complex Surfaces} Ch.~VIII).

\emph{(iii).} $E$ is a genus-$1$ curve with $\omega_E = \mathcal{O}_E$; $H^1(E,
\mathcal{O}_E) = k$ by Serre duality and $H^0(E, \omega_E) = k$. Any object $F \in
D^b\Coh(E)$ has $\Ext^1_E(F, F) \neq 0$ once $F$ is non-trivial. The Poincar\'e bundle
$\mathcal{P}$ on $E \times E^{\vee}$ induces $\Phi_{\mathcal{P}} : D^b\Coh(E) \to
D^b\Coh(E^{\vee})$, an equivalence (Mukai 1981, Theorem 2.2), which is not a tilting
equivalence: no generator of $D^b\Coh(E)$ is ext-rigid.

\emph{(iv).} K\"unneth on $K3 \times E$: for sheaves $F$ on $K3$ and $G$ on $E$,
$\Ext^n_{K3 \times E}(F \boxtimes G, F \boxtimes G) = \bigoplus_{i+j=n} \Ext^i_{K3}(F, F)
\otimes_k \Ext^j_E(G, G)$ (Huybrechts--Lehn 2010 \emph{The Geometry of Moduli Spaces of
Sheaves} Lemma 10.25, adapted to $D^b$ via the K\"unneth spectral sequence). Tilting
of $T_{K3} \boxtimes T_E$ forces $\Ext^{>0}_{K3}(T_{K3}, T_{K3}) = 0$ (killed by (i))
and $\Ext^{>0}_E(T_E, T_E) = 0$ (killed by (iii)). General tilting objects on $K3 \times
E$ need not split as $T_{K3} \boxtimes T_E$, but the cohomological K\"unneth decomposition
of $\Ext^*(T, T)$ for $T$ supported on $K3 \times E$ propagates $h^{2,0}(K3)$ and
$h^{0,1}(E)$ obstructions to the product (Beauville 1983 \S 1; Huybrechts 2016
\emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~16).

\emph{(v).} ADE singularities on $K3$ admit local \NCCR{}s (Van den Bergh 2004
Theorem~A and \S 3) realised as path algebras of McKay quivers (Kirillov 2016 \S 2,
\S 4). The gluing of two local \NCCR\ charts $\Lambda_\alpha$, $\Lambda_\beta$ along
$U_{\alpha\beta}$ must intertwine the Van den Bergh equivalences
$D^b(\Lambda_\alpha) \simeq D^b\Coh(U_\alpha)$ and $D^b(\Lambda_\beta) \simeq
D^b\Coh(U_\beta)$; compatibility is measured by the monodromy of $\Stab(D^b\Coh(K3))$
around the walls crossed in $U_{\alpha\beta}$. Each $(-2)$-curve $C \subset K3$
contributes a Seidel--Thomas spherical twist $T_C$ acting on $D^b\Coh(K3)$ with
$T_C[\mathcal{O}_C] = \mathcal{O}_C[-1]$ and infinite order on $K^0(K3)$
(Seidel--Thomas 2001 Theorem~1.2; Bridgeland 2008 Theorem~5.3). A globally defined
sheaf-of-\NCCR{}s would require a trivialisation of the $T_C$-monodromy, which
Bridgeland 2008 Theorem~5.3 and the infinite-order action on the numerical Grothendieck
group exclude.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-serre-equivariant-quasi-nccr}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $X$ be a compact smooth Calabi--Yau threefold and let $\Stab(D^b\Coh(X))$ denote the
Bridgeland stability manifold (Bridgeland 2007 arXiv:math/0212237 Theorem~1.2). A
\emph{Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ on $X$} is a sheaf of tilted algebras
\[
  \mathscr{L} : \Stab(D^b\Coh(X)) \longrightarrow \mathrm{TiltAlg}_X,
  \qquad \sigma \longmapsto \End_{D^b\Coh(X)}(T_\sigma),
\]
where $T_\sigma$ is a locally constant tilting generator of the $\sigma$-heart
$\mathcal{A}_\sigma \subset D^b\Coh(X)$, subject to the Serre-equivariance axiom: for
every wall $W \subset \Stab(D^b\Coh(X))$ the wall-crossing autoequivalence
$\Psi_W : D^b\Coh(X) \to D^b\Coh(X)$ of Bridgeland 2008 (arXiv:math/0307164 \S 10)
intertwines the Serre functor $\SerreS_X = - \otimes \omega_X[\dim X]$,
\[
  \Psi_W \circ \SerreS_X \;=\; \SerreS_X \circ \Psi_W.
\]
On $X = K3 \times E$, $\omega_{K3 \times E} = \mathcal{O}_{K3 \times E}$ and
$\dim X = 3$, so $\SerreS_{K3 \times E} = [3]$ and the Serre-equivariance axiom reduces
to commutation with the shift-by-$3$ triangulated structure. The quasi-\NCCR\ is
\emph{not} a single algebra but a sheaf of algebras on a stratified complex manifold;
it is the replacement that survives the five-fold obstruction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-global-nccr-obstruction}.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Wall-crossing functors and the tilting chart atlas]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-wall-crossing-intertwining}
The locally constant condition on $T_\sigma$ means: on each chamber $U \subset
\Stab(D^b\Coh(X))$ of the Bridgeland stratification, $T_\sigma = T_U$ is constant, and
at each wall $W$ the transition $T_{U} \rightsquigarrow T_{U'}$ is by the wall-crossing
autoequivalence $\Psi_W$ of Bridgeland 2008 \S 10. Serre-equivariance
$\Psi_W \SerreS_X = \SerreS_X \Psi_W$ is automatic for $X = K3 \times E$ because the
wall-crossing autoequivalences are compositions of spherical twists and standard
shifts, all commuting with $[3]$ (Bridgeland 2008 Theorem~1.1).
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Positive-half character of the Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-quasi-nccr-positive-half-character}
\ClaimStatusTheorem
Let $\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E}$ be the Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ of
Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-serre-equivariant-quasi-nccr} on $X = K3 \times E$, and
let $\chi^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E})$ denote the character of the positive half,
understood as the generating function of $\Ext$-graded dimensions of the
$\sigma$-heart image of $T_\sigma$ weighted by K3 degree $d$, elliptic degree $h$, and
point-counting parameter $n$. Then
\[
  \chi^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E})(q, t, p) \;=\; Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\DT}(K3 \times E;
  q, t, p) \;=\; -\,\frac{1}{\Phi_{10}(q, t, p)},
\]
where $\Phi_{10}$ is the Igusa genus-$2$ cusp form of weight $10$
(Igusa 1964 \emph{Am.\ J.\ Math.} 86) and $Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\DT}$ is the reduced
Donaldson--Thomas partition function of Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016
(arXiv:1406.1139 Theorem~1). Equivalently,
\[
  \chi^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E}) \;=\; -\Phi_{10}^{-1} \;=\; -\Delta_5^{-2}
\]
under the Gritsenko--Nikulin identity $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1996
arXiv:alg-geom/9504006 Theorem~4.1). The $\kBKM$-weight of the BKM-lift incarnation is
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ (Borcherds 1995 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}
120, and
Theorem~\texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} of
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The character $\chi^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E})$ counts, at each stability chamber
$U \subset \Stab(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))$, the $\Ext$-graded dimensions of the tilting
generator's positive half under the heart $\mathcal{A}_U$. Reduced DT theory on
$K3 \times E$ assembles exactly this count: the Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced DT
partition function
\[
  Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\DT}(K3 \times E; q, t, p) \;=\; \sum_{(d, h, n)} N^{\mathrm{red}}_{d,h,n}
  \, q^{d-1} t^{h-1} (-p)^n
\]
(Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 arXiv:1406.1139 Theorem~1) weighs reduced DT invariants
$N^{\mathrm{red}}_{d,h,n} \in \mathbb{Z}$ by K3 degree $d$, elliptic degree $h$, and
Euler characteristic $n$. The reduced DT invariants equal the Euler characteristics of
reduced stable pair moduli (Pandharipande--Thomas 2009 arXiv:0707.2348 \S 2), which are
the chamber-by-chamber weighted counts of $\sigma$-stable objects in
$D^b\Coh(K3 \times E)$ for $\sigma \in U$. Bridgeland 2011 (arXiv:1002.4374
Theorem 1.1) identifies chamber-stable object counts with motivic Hall algebra
classes, which under the $\chi$-integration map
$\int : \mathcal{H}(D^b\Coh) \to \mathbb{Z}[[q, t, p]]$ produce exactly
$\chi^{+}(\mathscr{L}_{K3 \times E})$.

Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 Theorem~1 evaluates the sum in closed form:
\[
  \sum_{d \geq 0} \sum_{h \geq 0} \sum_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} N^{\mathrm{red}}_{d,h,n}
  \, q^{d-1} t^{h-1} (-p)^n \;=\; -\frac{1}{\Phi_{10}(q, t, p)},
\]
with $\Phi_{10}$ the Igusa cusp form of genus~$2$ and weight~$10$
(Igusa 1964 \S 3, arguments labelled by the $2 \times 2$ period matrix $(q, t, p)$ in
the Siegel half-space). Gritsenko--Nikulin 1996 Theorem~4.1 factors
$\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ via the $2$-torsion-multiplier square root $\Delta_5$,
a paramodular-level $2$ cusp form, and Borcherds 1995 Theorem~3.1 identifies
$\Delta_5$ as the automorphic denominator of a BKM superalgebra with
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ (Gritsenko 1999 \emph{St.\ Petersburg
Math.\ J.} 11 \S 5, Taylor coefficient $c_1(0) = 10$).

Serre-equivariance $\Psi_W \SerreS_{K3 \times E} = \SerreS_{K3 \times E} \Psi_W$ is
required for the chamber-by-chamber counts to glue into a global generating function;
on $K3 \times E$ this commutation is automatic because $\SerreS_{K3 \times E} = [3]$ is
central in the triangulated structure (Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003 Remark~3.2.1).
Without the Serre-equivariance axiom the wall-crossing formula of
Oberdieck--Pandharipande would break at every $(-2)$-spherical wall of the $K3$ factor;
the axiom is the precise condition under which $-1/\Phi_{10}$ emerges as a well-defined
global modular object.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Relationship to Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}(ii)]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-quasi-nccr-and-existence-theorem}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}(ii) asserts that on the isotrivial-fibration
stratum $G(K3 \times E)$ reconstructs a Hopf algebra via Frenkel--Kac exponentiation on
the rank-$24$ Mukai lattice, with $\kBKM$-weight $5$ at $N = 1$. The
quasi-\NCCR\ of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-serre-equivariant-quasi-nccr} is the
chain-level categorical witness: the sheaf of tilted algebras over
$\Stab(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))$, whose positive-half character is the Oberdieck--Pandharipande
generating function $-1/\Phi_{10}$ specialising to the BKM denominator $\Delta_5$ at
the $2$-torsion root. The global \NCCR\ obstruction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-global-nccr-obstruction} explains why the
quasi-\NCCR, not a genuine \NCCR, is the right substitute: the five obstructions (i)--(v)
obstruct a single-chart algebraic object, while the Bridgeland-stratified sheaf of
tilted algebras produces the same modular data as its positive-half character.
\end{remark}

The six-routes architecture of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence} constructs
$G(K3 \times E)$ by pentagon: five chiral-algebra routes converging up to
$\rho$-stratification, with the Borcherds lift as a distinguished automorphic source.
In the two-stage framework $\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ \PhiFA_3$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation};
Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}), the pentagon vertices are alternative
$(\Sigma_2, C)$-specialisations of the canonical $E_3$-hFA $\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(K3
\times E))$ (Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}).
Three of the five routes consume data that does not exist for a generic compact
Calabi--Yau threefold. Route $R_2$ asks for a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and
index $1$ intrinsic to $X$; a generic quintic has none. Route $R_3$ asks for an even
unimodular lattice of rank $24$ in $H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \mathbb{Z})$; the quintic has
even cohomology of rank $2$. Route $R_4$ asks for a finite-order symplectic involution;
the quintic admits none by Oguiso 2012 (arXiv:1201.4954). The pentagon collapses to the
single route $R_1 = \Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(X))) = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}(\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(X))))$,
and the question of what $G(X)$ means must be re-posed intrinsically: for a generic
compact CY$_3$, the single canonical specialisation of stage~$2$ is all that the
two-stage framework provides, and no pentagon of alternative specialisations resolves
into a Hopf algebra.

This chapter answers that question. The target splits along the Beauville--Bogomolov
tri-stratum of Part~\ref{part:examples}: the strict CY$_3$ stratum ($h^{0,1}(X) = 0$,
$h^{0,2}(X) = 0$, $h^{0,3}(X) = 1$), containing the quintic, bicubic, and
complete-intersection families; the
isotrivial-fibration stratum, containing $K3 \times E$; and the abelian-threefold
stratum, containing $T^6$. On each stratum there is a named candidate or obstruction
mechanism: derived-centre pair on the strict locus; pentagon on the fibration locus;
translation-collapse on the abelian locus. The resulting existence/obstruction conjecture
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}) does not construct a global $G(X)$; it
records the precise conditions under which a framed object-level target can be attached.

For a generic compact CY$_3$, the quantum-vertex-chiral-group target $G(X)$ is
unconstructed. The available object is conditional: after a framed $A_X^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$
has been built, one may form the derived-centre category
$\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_X^{(\Sigma_2,C)}))$. Tannakian reconstruction of a
Hopf algebra then requires a fibre functor; the fibre functor exists iff the category is
rigid and fusion, which forces $X$ into the rational-CFT-compatible locus
(Frenkel--Kac lattice-VOA class). For the quintic this is a conditional obstruction
statement, not a construction of a global algebra.

\section{The derived-centre pair as target}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-centre-pair}

\begin{definition}[Quantum vertex chiral group on a CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-G-X}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $X$ be a compact smooth Calabi--Yau threefold, fix a $2$-cycle
$\Sigma_2 \subset X$ and a reference curve $C \subset X$, and set
\[
  A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)} \;:=\; \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(D^b(\Coh(X)))
  \;=\; \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(X)))\bigr)
\]
(Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation};
Remark~\ref{rem:phi-d-parameter-tagging}). Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}
supplies $A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)}$ only as a framed object-level assignment under H1--H4
and fixed specialisation; chain-level explicit construction is conditional for non-formal algebras. On
$X = K3 \times E$ the canonical choice $(\Sigma_2, C) = (K3, E)$ is implicit
throughout this chapter, and the abbreviation
$A_{K3 \times E} := A_{K3 \times E}^{(K3, E)}$ is adopted
(Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}); on strict CY$_3$s and on $T^6$, any
canonical cycle class fixed by the Beauville--Bogomolov stratum is the
implicit $\Sigma_2$ (Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-bb-stratification}).
When this framed algebra is constructed, the \emph{candidate quantum vertex chiral group of $X$} on the specialisation datum
$(\Sigma_2, C)$ is the braided monoidal category
\[
  G^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(X) \;:=\; \mathcal{Z}\!\bigl( \mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)}) \bigr)
  \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Rep}^{\Etwo}\!\bigl( Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)}) \bigr),
\]
the Drinfeld centre of the monoidal category of $\Eone$-modules, equivalently
(Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler) the $\Etwo$-modules over the chiral derived centre
$Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)})
= C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)}, A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)})$. When
the cycle is canonical and the framed construction exists, the shorthands
$A_X := A_X^{(\Sigma_2, C)}$ and $G(X) := G^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(X)$ are used. Without that
datum, global $G(X)$ is unconstructed in general.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Category versus algebra]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-category-vs-algebra}
Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-G-X} replaces the pentagon-implicit algebraic target
(a Hopf algebra reconstructed by Frenkel--Kac from the Mukai lattice) with a categorical
target. The two coincide on $K3 \times E$, where the $24$-dimensional Mukai lattice and
its Frenkel--Kac exponential reconstruct a Hopf algebra whose representation category is
$\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_{K3 \times E}))$
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian}, Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}). For a
quintic, no lattice presentation exists and no Hopf-algebra reconstruction is available;
the braided monoidal category is the target, not an intermediate step. The Etingof
clarification: in the non-rigid non-fusion CY$_3$ world, the quantum group \emph{is} the
category.
\end{remark}

\section{Three strata, three mechanisms}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-three-strata}

\begin{definition}[Beauville--Bogomolov stratification of the CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} target]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-bb-stratification}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
A compact CY threefold $X$ is \emph{strict} if $h^{0,1}(X) = h^{0,2}(X) = 0$;
\emph{isotrivially fibred} if $X$ admits a surjective holomorphic map
$\pi : X \to C$ to a smooth curve $C$ with generic fibre a K3 surface or
abelian surface; and \emph{abelian-type} if $X = A^3 / G$ for $A^3$ a complex
abelian threefold and $G$ a finite group acting by CY-preserving isometries.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Tri-stratum exhaustivity on smooth projective CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-tri-stratum-exhaustive}
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere
Every smooth projective compact CY threefold belongs to exactly one of the three
strata of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-bb-stratification}, up to finite \'etale
cover. The strict locus contains the quintic, bicubic, and complete-intersection CY
families; the isotrivially fibred locus contains
$K3 \times E$ and its twisted analogues; the abelian-type locus contains $T^6$ and its
finite-group quotients (Borcea--Voisin analogues).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition theorem for compact K\"ahler manifolds with trivial
canonical bundle (Beauville 1983, \emph{Vari\'et\'es K\"ahleriennes dont la premi\`ere
classe de Chern est nulle}) partitions compact CY$_3$ by holonomy group:
$\mathrm{Hol}(X) = \mathrm{SU}(3)$ (strict), $\mathrm{SU}(2) \times \{1\}$
(K3-fibred or $K3 \times E$ class), or abelian (torus quotient).
The three cases correspond to the three strata; exhaustion is the statement of the
Beauville--Bogomolov theorem. Smoothness is inherited; projectivity is preserved
under the decomposition in dimension $3$ (Beauville 1983, Proposition~1).
\end{proof}

\section{The existence/obstruction theorem}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-existence}

\begin{conjecture}[Existence/obstruction for \texorpdfstring{$G(X)$}{G(X)} parametrised by CY class]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Let $X$ be a smooth projective compact Calabi--Yau threefold, belonging to exactly one
stratum of Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-tri-stratum-exhaustive}. The quantum vertex
chiral group $G(X)$ of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-G-X} is governed by the following
structural statements, one per stratum:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \textbf{Strict CY$_3$ stratum.} For $X$ with $h^{0,1}(X) = h^{0,2}(X) = 0$
        and $h^{0,3}(X) = 1$, a candidate $G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(X)$ exists only after
        the framed algebra $A_X^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ has been constructed under H1--H4;
        Tannakian reconstruction of a
        Hopf algebra is obstructed by the vanishing of the Mukai lattice and the failure
        of fusion rigidity. The category has no fibre functor to $\mathrm{Vect}_k$,
        equivalently no underlying Hopf algebra. The framed $\Phi_3$-output Hodge-supertrace
        modular characteristic
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\cA_X) = \Xi(X) = 0$ uniformly on the strict
        stratum (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}; Serre duality
        at odd $d$ gives $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$). The companion BCOV one-loop invariant
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}(\cA_X) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$
        is a separate construction ($-25/3$ at the quintic; see
        Remark~\ref{rem:kappa-ch-quintic-two-views} of
        Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification}). The Hodge-supertrace
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$ and BCOV one-loop
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ arise from two distinct
        constructions---the $\Phi_3$ categorical output versus the
        BCOV torsion---and take different values at a single CY (zero
        versus $-25/3$ at the quintic). The notation $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$
        without a Route A (Hodge) / Route B (BCOV) qualifier is
        meaningless in the CY$_3$ setting.
  \item \textbf{Isotrivially fibred stratum.} For $X = S \times E$ with $S$ a K3 surface
        and $E$ an elliptic curve, the category $G(X)$ reconstructs a Hopf algebra via
        Frenkel--Kac exponentiation on the rank-$24$ Mukai lattice
        $\LMuk = U^{\oplus 3} \oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$; the pentagon of
        Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence} converges on this Hopf algebra up to
        the $\rho$-stratification of
        Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-six-routes-generator-level-convergence}. The
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ weight is $c_N(0)/2 = 5$ at $N = 1$ (Igusa $\Phi_{10}$,
        Borcherds weight theorem).
  \item \textbf{Abelian-type stratum.} For $X = T^6$ or a finite CY-preserving quotient
        thereof, the Euler characteristic $\chi(T^6) = 0$ and the translation action
        $T^6 \times T^6 \to T^6$ trivialise the equivariant Donaldson--Thomas partition
        function: $Z^{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{eq}}(T^6) \equiv 0$. The chiral algebra
        $A_{T^6}$ collapses to the rank-$6$ Heisenberg $\mathcal{H}_{T^6}$ carried by the
        cohomology lattice $H^{\mathrm{even}}(T^6, \mathbb{Z}) \simeq \mathbb{Z}^{32}$
        with the wedge pairing, and the category $G(T^6)$ is the symmetric monoidal
        category of $\mathcal{H}_{T^6}$-modules with trivial braiding. In particular,
        $G(T^6)$ carries no quantum-group structure; it is the classical limit.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) Existence of the category is
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} (CY-A$_3$, $\infty$-categorical) followed by the
Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler identification of the Drinfeld centre with the $\Etwo$-modules
over the chiral derived centre (the Drinfeld-centre BZFN theorem of
Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center}). Obstruction to fibre functor: fusion rigidity requires
a finite number of simple objects closed under tensor product with semisimple dualities.
For the quintic, the category $\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_{\mathrm{quintic}})$ is infinite
and non-semisimple (the CoHA $\mathrm{DT}(\mathrm{quintic})$ has no quiver presentation,
and the Schiffmann--Vasserot framework does not apply beyond the toric-no-4-cycle case).
Fusion fails; Tannakian reconstruction (Deligne 1990, \emph{Cat\'egories
tannakiennes}) obstructs. The Hodge-supertrace vanishing
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(\cA_X) = 0$ on the strict stratum is the
$\Phi_3$-output entry identified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}: Serre duality at $d = 3$
odd with $\omega_X \simeq \cO_X$ gives $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$, and the Hodge-supertrace
comparator of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-d-kappa-stratification} transports this to
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$ on the CY$_3$ side. The BCOV companion
invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}(\cA_X) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$
is a separate construction (Remark~\ref{rem:kappa-ch-quintic-two-views}).
The identity $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ holds only on the slice
$d = 2$, $h^{1,0} = 0$ where Serre duality $\mathbb S = [2]$ collapses the
Hodge supertrace to the holomorphic Euler characteristic; at odd $d$,
Serre duality forces $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$ while $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$
remains nonzero ($-25/3$ at the quintic), so the equation fails outright
and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ must carry the dimension-stratified formula.

(ii) Frenkel--Kac exponentiation: rank-$24$ Mukai lattice VOA reconstructs a Hopf algebra
on the space of positive-norm vectors (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}).
The pentagon of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence} supplies the comparison with
Borcherds, orbifold, half-twist, and BLLPR routes, converging up to the $\rho$-stratum
stated in Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-six-routes-generator-level-convergence}. The Igusa
$\Phi_{10}$ weight is $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5$ (Borcherds 1992 Theorem~10.4).

(iii) Translation action: $T^6$ carries a free transitive action of itself. The
equivariant DT partition function is a character of this action, hence identically zero
for any non-trivial equivariant class (standard argument, Behrend--Bryan--Szendr\H{o}i
2013 for the abelian case). The chiral algebra $A_{T^6}$ therefore has no quantum
corrections beyond the classical lattice VOA structure; the braided monoidal category is
symmetric. Explicit computation: for $T^6 = E_1 \times E_2 \times E_3$ a product of
elliptic curves, $A_{T^6} = \Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(T^6)))$ is computed by K\"unneth from
$\Phi_1(D^b(\Coh(E_i)))$ = elliptic lattice VOA, and the $\Etwo$-structure on the
Drinfeld centre collapses to $E_\infty$ (symmetric monoidal) because each factor is
$E_\infty$ at $d = 1$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}, clause \textup{(U3)}
with $n(1) = \infty$).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Status: why \textup{Conjectured}]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-status}
Each of the three stratum statements (i)--(iii) carries its own conditional layer.
Clause (i) is conditional on the BCOV identification of the genus-$1$ GW generating
function with the chiral modular characteristic, an open problem beyond the
toric-CY$_3$-without-compact-$4$-cycle case. Clause (ii) is conditional on
Conjecture CY-C itself (Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism}), hence
already conjectural in its pentagon form. Clause (iii) is proved up to the
$\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(T^6)))$ K\"unneth formula and the vanishing of the equivariant DT
partition function, both of which are established at the
$\infty$-categorical level but not at chain level for non-formal inputs.
\end{remark}

\section{The fibre-functor obstruction on the quintic}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-quintic-obstruction}

\begin{proposition}[Fibre-functor obstruction on the quintic]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor}
\ClaimStatusConditional
Let $X = Q \subset \mathbb{P}^4$ be the quintic threefold, and fix a
framed datum $(Q,\Sigma_2,C)$ for which the object-level algebra
$A_Q^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} has been
constructed. The braided monoidal category
$G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q) =
\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_Q^{(\Sigma_2,C)}))$ of
Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-G-X} admits no fibre functor
$\omega : G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q) \to \mathrm{Vect}_k^{\mathbb{Z}}$
compatible with the braiding.
Equivalently: there is no Hopf algebra $H$ with
$G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q) \simeq \mathrm{Rep}(H)$ as braided monoidal categories.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
A fibre functor on a braided monoidal category reconstructs a Hopf algebra iff the
category is rigid (every object has a dual) and the fibre functor is monoidally exact
(Deligne 1990, Theorem~2.8). For the quintic, the category of $\Eone$-modules over
$A_Q^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ is not rigid: $Q$ has no compact exceptional collection
(Bondal--Orlov 2001 classification, arXiv:math/0206295), equivalently $D^b(\Coh(Q))$ has
no tilting object of finite global dimension. The Drinfeld centre inherits the failure
of rigidity: an object in $\mathcal{Z}$ has a dual iff its underlying object in
$\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_Q^{(\Sigma_2,C)})$ does. Hence
$G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q)$ is non-rigid, Tannakian reconstruction fails, and
no Hopf algebra $H$ with $G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q) \simeq \mathrm{Rep}(H)$
exists. The category is the target; there is no underlying algebra to be
reconstructed.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What remains computable on the quintic]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-quintic-computable}
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor} obstructs Hopf-algebra
reconstruction on any constructed framed datum, but not scalar shadows of the
quintic. The Hodge-supertrace characteristic
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(A_Q^{(\Sigma_2,C)}) = \chi(\cO_Q)=0$
when the framed object exists, the BCOV torsion, the DT partition function $Z^{\mathrm{DT}}(Q)$
(computed by MNOP 2006 to all orders in $q$; Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande,
arXiv:math/0312059), and the character of the centre
$\mathrm{ch}_{\mathcal{Z}(G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q))}(q)$ on a constructed framed
datum are numerical shadows, not Hopf-algebra witnesses. The Hopf structure is
what fails; the categorical structure is conditional on the fixed framing, and the
scalar invariants do not supply that framing.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Quintic strictification and framing obstruction]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-quintic-strictification-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusConditional
Let $X_5 \subset \mathbb P^4$ be a smooth quintic threefold. The standard
geometric data of $X_5$ do not construct a strict uncurved
chain-level witness for the compact non-formal branch of
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$. More precisely, any strict framed witness on the
large-volume branch would have to provide a cyclic null-homotopy of the
transferred cubic operation
\[
  m^{\mathrm{KS}}_3 :
  \mathrm{Sym}^3 H^1(T_{X_5}) \longrightarrow
  H^3(\mathcal O_{X_5}) \simeq \mathbb C
\]
or a named Maurer--Cartan curvature term absorbing it. In the
large-volume mirror normalisation this cubic has classical coefficient
\[
  Y_3(X_5) = \int_{X_5} H^3 = 5.
\]
Consequently a strict model in which the transferred cubic vanishes is
impossible on this branch. The negative-cyclic CY$_3$ orientation
exists, and the scalar shadows
\[
  \chi(\mathcal O_{X_5}) = 1-0+0-1=0,\qquad
  \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5)/24=-200/24=-25/3
\]
are computable, but these data do not provide the missing
$S^3$-framing/OPE-completion witness required by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. The remaining proof obligation is
exactly:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item a cyclic curved $A_\infty$ or $L_\infty$ model whose curvature
        kills the non-zero Yukawa class while preserving the
        negative-cyclic CY$_3$ trace;
  \item an admissible specialisation $(\Sigma_2,C)$ and analytic
        completion for the resulting $\Eone$-chiral object;
  \item compatibility of that framed object with the derived-centre
        construction defining $G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(X_5)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The CY$_3$ orientation of $D^b_{\mathrm{dg}}\Coh(X_5)$ is supplied by
Serre duality: $\omega_{X_5}\simeq\mathcal O_{X_5}$ gives
$S_{X_5}=[3]$, and the trace lifts to a negative-cyclic CY class by
Kontsevich--Soibelman's smooth-proper CY formalism. This is the
orientation input; it is not an explicit framed chiral output.

By HKR and the Barannikov--Kontsevich transfer of the
Kodaira--Spencer dg Lie algebra, the first non-formal cubic on the
large-volume branch is the Yukawa coupling. For a quintic hypersurface,
adjunction gives $H^3=\deg(X_5)=5$, so the mirror-normalised classical
Yukawa coefficient is non-zero. A strictification with vanishing
transferred $m_3$ would make this cohomology class zero. Since the
target $H^3(\mathcal O_{X_5})$ is one-dimensional and the coefficient
is $5$, no such null-homotopy is contained in the Hodge table, Serre
CY class, or BCOV genus-one scalar.

The scalar calculations are independent checks, not construction data.
The Hodge column $(h^{0,0},h^{0,1},h^{0,2},h^{0,3})=(1,0,0,1)$ gives
$\chi(\mathcal O_{X_5})=0$, while
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5)=2(h^{1,1}-h^{2,1})=2(1-101)=-200$ gives
the BCOV shadow $-25/3$. Neither scalar is a cyclic null-homotopy of
$m_3^{\mathrm{KS}}$, and neither singles out a Borcherds denominator
identity: the quintic has no K3 Mukai lattice and no known
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$. The listed three items are therefore necessary
before the compact non-formal quintic can be counted as a constructed
strict/framed $\Phi_3$ witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Compact Costello--Li source bridge versus the quintic obstruction]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-compact-cl-source-bridge}
The compact Costello--Li/open--closed source residual is not the
obstruction in
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-quintic-strictification-obstruction}. The
source-side map is supplied by
Theorem~\ref{thm:compact-cy3-finite-stage-open-closed-source}: after a
negative-cyclic CY$_3$ class, finite holomorphic-jet order, finite
Costello--Li RG order, and anomaly cancellation are fixed, the map
\[
  C^-_\bullet(\Perf(X_5))_{\leq N}
  \longrightarrow
  C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}
  \bigl(A_{X_5}^{N,r},A_{X_5}^{N,r}\bigr)
\]
is a chain map compatible with the finite-stage BV and chiral
Hochschild differentials. The strict compact obstruction is sharper:
the quintic still requires a curved cyclic model absorbing the non-zero
Yukawa cubic, an admissible $(\Sigma_2,C)$ specialisation with analytic
OPE completion, and the derived-centre/Hall orientation comparison that
turns the source-side boundary algebra into a Hopf-valued target.
\end{remark}

\section{Explicit construction on local \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^2$}{P-2}}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-local-P2}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$G(X)$}{G(X)} on local \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^2$}{P-2}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-local-P2}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Proved on the toric Hall/critical-CoHA model for local $\mathbb P^2$; identification with the framed $G(X)$ target uses the hCS-to-Hall and Drinfeld-centre comparison hypotheses.}
Let $X = \mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb{P}^2}) = \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^2}(-3)$ be local
$\mathbb{P}^2$, a non-compact toric CY threefold. Let
$(Q_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2},W_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2})$ be the standard
$dP_0$ quiver with cubic superpotential obtained from the Beilinson tilting collection
on $\mathbb P^2$ by the $3$-Calabi--Yau completion. The critical CoHA
$\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2}, W_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2})$ is a
finitely generated associative Hall algebra, and on the framed toric Hall-comparison
locus:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item the Drinfeld double
        $D(\mathcal{H}(Q_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2},W_{\mathrm{loc}\,\mathbb{P}^2}))$
        is a Hopf algebra in $\mathbb{Z}[[\hbar]]$-modules;
  \item the $\Etwo$-braided representation category
        $\mathrm{Rep}^{\Etwo}(D(\mathcal{H}))$ is the Hopf-valued model for
        the framed target $G^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(X)$ of
        Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-G-X} after the hCS-to-Hall comparison;
  \item the fibre functor $\omega : \mathrm{Rep}^{\Etwo}(D(\mathcal{H})) \to
        \mathrm{Vect}_k^{\mathbb{Z}}$ exists, exhibiting $G(X)$ as the representation
        category of the Hopf algebra $D(\mathcal{H})$ whenever the framed comparison is fixed.
\end{enumerate}
The local $\mathbb{P}^2$ case is the basic local toric CY$_3$ test: the zero section
$\mathbb{P}^2 \subset X$ is a compact Fano surface whose Beilinson tilting presentation
supplies the quiver structure, and the canonical line-bundle direction supplies the
Calabi--Yau orientation in complex dimension three.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Beilinson's exceptional collection
$(\mathcal O,\mathcal O(1),\mathcal O(2))$ on $\mathbb P^2$ gives a finite tilting
algebra (Beilinson 1978, \emph{Coherent sheaves on $\mathbb P^n$ and problems of
linear algebra}). Passing to the local Calabi--Yau total space
$\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb P^2})$ is the $3$-Calabi--Yau completion of this algebra:
equivalently one obtains the standard $dP_0$ quiver with three vertices, three arrows
in each cyclic direction, and cubic potential (Ginzburg 2006, arXiv:math/0612139,
\S 4; Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001, arXiv:math/9908027, Theorem~1.2). Rapcak--Soibelman--
Yang--Zhao 2020 (arXiv:2007.13365, Theorem~1.1) identify the critical CoHA of toric
CY$_3$ quivers with potential with the positive half of the corresponding affine
quiver Yangian; in the local $\mathbb P^2$ case this is the Hall positive-half model
attached to the $dP_0$ quiver.

(i) Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 (arXiv:1202.2756 for the Jordan quiver;
arXiv:1310.3655 for the general affine-type case) construct the Drinfeld double
$D(\mathcal{H}) = \mathcal{H} \bowtie \mathcal{H}^{*\mathrm{cop}}$ via the Hopf pairing
coming from the Joyce integration map and the motivic DT orientation data. For any
CY$_3$ quiver with potential, the pairing is non-degenerate on the nilpotent radical
(Davison 2017, arXiv:1512.04179, Theorem~A), giving a well-defined Hopf algebra.

(ii) The $\Etwo$-braiding is given by the universal $R$-matrix of the Drinfeld double,
constructed from the half-braiding as in
Construction~\ref{constr:cy-r-matrix} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:yang-r-matrix-ybe}. The equivalence with
$\mathcal{Z}(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_X))$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha}(i) specialised to the local $\mathbb P^2$
Hall/chiral comparison model.

(iii) The fibre functor is the forgetful functor to the underlying $\mathbb{Z}$-graded
vector space, available because $\mathcal{H}$ is a finitely generated associative
algebra in graded $k$-vector spaces and $D(\mathcal{H})$ inherits this presentation. The
Tannakian reconstruction applies: $G(X) \simeq \mathrm{Rep}(D(\mathcal{H}))$ as braided
monoidal categories.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why local \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^2$}{P-2} works but quintic fails]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-local-vs-compact}
The contrast is Bondal--Orlov: local $\mathbb{P}^2$ is controlled by the exceptional
collection $(\mathcal O,\mathcal O(1),\mathcal O(2))$ on $\mathbb P^2$ and its
$3$-Calabi--Yau completion, with finite-dimensional quiver input; the compact quintic does not (no tilting
object, no exceptional collection of finite length). The CoHA quiver presentation
depends on an exceptional collection; without one, the associative algebra $\mathcal{H}$
is not finitely generated, the Drinfeld double is not a Hopf algebra in the categorical
sense of item~(i), and the fibre functor of item~(iii) fails. The strict CY$_3$ locus is
precisely the locus where no exceptional collection generates, forcing the
obstruction of Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor}.
\end{remark}

\section{Stable transfer to toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} and the compact-CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} \texorpdfstring{$2.5$}{2.5}-mechanism obstruction}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-stable-transfer-and-compact-obstruction}

Local CY$_3$ models are toric or toric-adjacent: the Beilinson
exceptional collection on $\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$ for $Y$ a Fano toric
surface transports the $T^3$-equivariant cohomology of $Y$ to a CoHA
quiver presentation on the bundle, and the non-compact fibre direction
carries the Calabi--Yau orientation. Compactification breaks the toric
torus: a smooth projective compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$ and
trivial canonical class carries no non-trivial holomorphic vector
field (Matsumura 1963, \emph{Proc.\ Japan Acad.}~39 \S 1; Bogomolov
1974, \emph{Izv.\ Akad.\ Nauk SSSR Ser.\ Mat.}~38 Theorem~1), so
$\mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$ is trivial, and the Sumihiro equivariant
completion theorem (Sumihiro 1974, \emph{J.\ Math.\ Kyoto}~14 \S 1,
Theorem~1) forbids any $T^d$-equivariant structure with $d \geq 1$ on
$X$. Four candidate obstructions to the transfer,
(O1) toric fan-completeness, (O2) BCOV $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$-curving,
(O3) $\mathrm{Aut}^0$ rigidity, (O4) finite-quiver equivariance, reduce
under Sumihiro--Bogomolov--Matsumura--Van den Bergh--Bocklandt--Hartogs
to $2.5$ mechanism types: (O1) $\Leftrightarrow$ (O3) via
Sumihiro; (O1) $\Rightarrow$ (O4) via Bocklandt superpotential with
Hartogs extension over $R = \mathbf C$; and (O2) logically independent but
numerically dormant on the compact $h^{0,1}=0$ catalogue,
sourced from the Costello--Li one-loop BV counter-term with target
$H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-target}.

\begin{theorem}[Stable transfer to toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-stable-transfer-toric}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $X$ be a smooth quasi-projective CY$_3$ with compact Fano base
$Y = X^{T}$ the fixed locus of a chosen algebraic torus action
$T = T^d \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Aut}(X)$ with $d \geq 1$. Assume $X$
is of local type: $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$ for a compact Fano toric
surface $Y$, or a toric resolution of an affine Gorenstein toric CY$_3$
singularity. Write $Q_X$ for the Beilinson quiver of $Y$ lifted along
the bundle projection, and $W_X$ for the cubic Yoneda superpotential.
The following data stably transfer from $Y$ to $X$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(T\arabic*)}]
  \item \textbf{Equivariant cohomology.}
        $H^\bullet_{T}(X, \mathbf Q) \simeq H^\bullet_T(Y, \mathbf Q)
        \otimes_{H^\bullet_T(\mathrm{pt})} H^\bullet_T(\mathrm{fibre})$
        via the toric-bundle localisation of Demazure--Fulton
        (Fulton 1993, \emph{Introduction to Toric Varieties},
        \S 3.4; Demazure 1970, \emph{Ann.\ Sci.\ \'ENS}~3).
  \item \textbf{Exceptional collection.} Pull-back of the Beilinson
        collection on $Y$ along $\pi : X \to Y$ generates $D^b\Coh(X)$
        and remains $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}$-rigid: the Serre-duality
        obstruction of Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}
        is removed by the non-compact fibre direction (absence of
        Serre functor restricted to compact objects on $X$).
  \item \textbf{Critical CoHA.} The Kontsevich--Soibelman critical
        CoHA $\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)$ is finitely generated over the
        equivariant parameter ring $H^\bullet_T(\mathrm{pt})$
        (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008, arXiv:0811.2435, \S 7; formality
        and finite generation on toric CY$_3$: Rapcak--Soibelman--%
        Yang--Zhao 2020, arXiv:2007.13365, Theorem~1.1).
  \item \textbf{Drinfeld double.} $D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X))$ is a Hopf
        algebra over $H^\bullet_T(\mathrm{pt})$ with universal
        $R$-matrix from the Joyce integration pairing
        (Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013, arXiv:1310.3655 Theorem~A;
        Davison 2017, arXiv:1512.04179 Theorem~A).
  \item \textbf{$E_2$-braided fibre functor.} The forgetful functor
        $D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X))\mathrm{-Rep} \to
        \mathrm{Vect}_k^{\mathbf Z}$ exists, giving $G(X) \simeq
        \mathrm{Rep}^{E_2}(D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)))$ as braided
        monoidal $E_2$-category via
        Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha}(i).
\end{enumerate}
The transfer is stable under resolution of toric affine CY$_3$
singularities (Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001, arXiv:math/9908027,
Theorem~1.2, derived McKay on $[\mathbf C^3/G]$ for finite $G \subset
\mathrm{SU}(3)$) and under partial compactification preserving the
$T^d$-action.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(T1).} The bundle $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y) \xrightarrow{\pi} Y$
carries the pull-back $T$-action from the toric $Y$ (trivial on the
fibre direction), and the Atiyah--Bott localisation formula
(Atiyah--Bott 1984, \emph{Topology}~23, Theorem~3.5) applied to the
toric-bundle $T$-fixed locus
$X^T \simeq Y^T \hookrightarrow Y \subset X$ gives the Demazure--Fulton
splitting. For toric affine CY$_3$ resolutions, the fan-theoretic
equivariant Chow ring (Cox--Little--Schenck 2011, \emph{Toric
Varieties}, \S 12.4) plays the same role.

\emph{(T2).} The tilting bundle $T_Y = \bigoplus_i E_i$ on the Fano
base $Y$ satisfies $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_Y(T_Y, T_Y) = 0$ and generates
$D^b\Coh(Y)$ (Bondal 1989 Theorem~3.2; strong exceptional collection).
The pull-back $\pi^* T_Y$ on $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$ is a tilting
generator of the category of compactly supported perfect complexes
$\mathrm{Perf}_c(X) \subset D^b\Coh(X)$: projection formula gives
$\mathrm{R}\mathrm{Hom}_X(\pi^* T_Y, \pi^* T_Y) = \mathrm{R}\mathrm{Hom}_Y(T_Y, T_Y
\otimes \mathrm{R}\pi_* \mathcal O_X)$, and $\mathrm{R}\pi_* \mathcal O_X
= \mathrm{Sym}^\bullet(\omega_Y^{-1})$ (locally free on $Y$, hence
flat), so $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_X(\pi^* T_Y, \pi^* T_Y)
= \bigoplus_{n \geq 0} \mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_Y(T_Y, T_Y \otimes
\omega_Y^{-n}) = 0$ term-wise, using $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_Y(T_Y, T_Y
\otimes \omega_Y^{-n}) = 0$ by the strong exceptional property extended
to tensor twists (Bridgeland 2006, arXiv:math/0602129 Proposition~4.3;
Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003, arXiv:math/0204218 Theorem~1.1). The
Serre-duality-on-CY$_3$ obstruction
$\mathrm{Ext}^3(E, E) \neq 0$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}
does not trigger on $X$: the non-compact fibre direction means $X$ is
not smooth projective, the Serre functor is not a shift-by-$3$ on
compactly supported perfect complexes (the dualising complex is
$\omega_X = \mathcal O_X[-3]$ restricted to the compact base, with the
non-compact direction absorbing the shift), and the CY pairing on the
category $\mathrm{Perf}_c(X)$ is the $Y$-pairing composed with
integration along the non-compact fibre, not a CY$_3$ Serre pairing.

\emph{(T3).} Finite generation of the critical CoHA on toric CY$_3$
quivers is Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao 2020 Theorem~1.1: the super
affine Yangian $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak g}_{Q_X})$ of the associated
super Kac--Moody datum is isomorphic to $\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)$ with
finite set of Drinfeld--Chevalley generators, each indexed by a vertex
of $Q_X$ and finitely many derivation loops. The toric $T^d$-equivariance
makes every structure constant a rational function in the equivariant
parameters $t_1, \ldots, t_d$ (Okounkov 2018, arXiv:1806.11282 \S 2).

\emph{(T4).} Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 Theorem~A constructs the
Drinfeld double $D(\mathcal H) = \mathcal H \bowtie \mathcal H^{*\mathrm{cop}}$
with Hopf-algebra structure on any CY$_3$ quiver with potential. The
Joyce integration pairing (Joyce--Song 2008, arXiv:0810.5645, \S 3) is
non-degenerate on the nilpotent radical for CY$_3$ quivers satisfying
the motivic-DT stability structure (Davison 2017 Theorem~A); toric
CY$_3$ quivers do.

\emph{(T5).} The fibre functor is the forgetful functor to graded
$H^\bullet_T(\mathrm{pt})$-modules, available because $\mathcal H$ is
finitely generated over this base (T3) and $D(\mathcal H)$ inherits
the presentation. The $E_2$-braiding is the universal $R$-matrix of
the Drinfeld double; equivalence with
$\mathcal Z(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(A_X))$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha}(i).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Compact CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} $2.5$-count obstruction]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-compact-cy3-25-count}
\ClaimStatusTheorem
Let $X$ be a smooth projective compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$.
The toric-stable-transfer Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-stable-transfer-toric}
fails on $X$: $X$ admits no Hopf-algebra presentation
$\Phi_3(D^b\Coh(X)) = D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X))$ with CoHA of a
Beilinson-quiver-with-potential. Four candidate obstructions to the
transfer,
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(O\arabic*)}]
  \item[\textup{(O1)}] \textbf{Toric fan-completeness.} There is no
        complete toric fan $\Sigma_X$ with $X = X_{\Sigma_X}$; in
        particular, the Beilinson-type exceptional collection on a toric
        Fano base cannot globalise to $X$.
  \item[\textup{(O2)}] \textbf{BCOV 1-loop curving.} The
        Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa one-loop class
        $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X) = (\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24)
        [\Omega_X]^{0,1} \in H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$
        is the one-loop curving channel
        (Costello--Li 2016, arXiv:1606.00365, Proposition~5.2; BCOV 1994
        arXiv:hep-th/9309140 \S 4). Under the present compact
        $h^{1,0}(X)=0$ hypothesis the target group is zero, so this
        channel is a formal independent mechanism rather than a
        non-vanishing obstruction; it becomes effective only after
        relaxing the compact $h^{1,0}=0$ hypothesis or adding boundary/defect data.
  \item[\textup{(O3)}] \textbf{$\mathrm{Aut}^0$ rigidity.}
        $\mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$ is trivial (Matsumura 1963 \S 1,
        Bogomolov 1974 Theorem~1): no non-trivial holomorphic
        vector fields, hence no algebraic torus $T^d$ with $d \geq 1$
        acts on $X$.
  \item[\textup{(O4)}] \textbf{Finite-quiver equivariance.} Any
        tilting module $T \in D^b\Coh(X)$ satisfies
        $R = \mathrm{End}_{\mathcal O_X}(T)$ a finite-dimensional
        $\mathbf C$-algebra with $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}(T, T) = 0$. The
        Beilinson-type quiver presentation $R \simeq J(Q, W)$
        (Bocklandt 2008, arXiv:math/0603558, Theorem~3.1) requires an
        outer gauge torus $T_Q^{\mathrm{out}} \subset \mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$
        of positive dimension to realise the CoHA-quiver structure,
        which fails under $\mathrm{Aut}^0(X) = \{e\}$
        (Van den Bergh 2004, arXiv:math/0207170, \S 4).
\end{enumerate}
The four obstruction channels reduce logically to a $2.5$-mechanism count:
\[
  \boxed{\quad (\mathrm{O}1) \;\Longleftrightarrow\; (\mathrm{O}3),
    \qquad (\mathrm{O}1) \;\Longrightarrow\; (\mathrm{O}4),
    \qquad (\mathrm{O}2) \;\text{logically independent}. \quad}
\]
Equivalent formulation: the independent source types are
$\{(\mathrm{O}1 \equiv \mathrm{O}3), (\mathrm{O}2)\}$ with (O4)
downstream. On compact CY$_3$s with $h^{0,1}=0$, only the
automorphism-geometry mechanism is effective; (O2) remains a separate
BCOV channel but has zero value by Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-factor-split}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(O1) $\Leftrightarrow$ (O3) via Sumihiro 1974 and the Luna slice.}
Sumihiro 1974 (\emph{J.\ Math.\ Kyoto}~14:1, Theorem~1) states that
any normal algebraic variety $X$ with an algebraic torus action
$T \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Aut}(X)$, $\dim T \geq 1$, admits a
$T$-equivariant open embedding into a toric variety $X_{\Sigma_X}$ with
fan $\Sigma_X$; if $X$ is complete, $\Sigma_X$ is a complete
fan (Fulton 1993, \emph{Introduction to Toric Varieties}, \S 3.4,
$|\Sigma| = N_\mathbf R$ criterion for completeness). The Luna \'etale
slice theorem (Luna 1973, \emph{Bull.\ Soc.\ Math.\ France}, M\'em.~33,
Theorem~1) pins the local structure at each $T$-fixed point to the
formal toric structure on the tangent cone, so $T$ extends to
$(\mathbf G_m)^{\dim X}$ globally: $X$ is toric completable iff
$\mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$ contains an algebraic torus of dimension
$\dim X$. On a compact smooth CY$_3$, the trivial canonical bundle
$\omega_X \simeq \mathcal O_X$ gives $T_X \simeq \Omega^2_X$
(via $T_X = (\Omega^1_X)^\vee = \Omega^2_X \otimes \omega_X^{-1}
= \Omega^2_X$), hence $h^0(X, T_X) = h^{2,0}(X)$. The
Beauville--Bogomolov--Matsumura three-case stratification of
$\mathrm{Aut}^0$ on compact K\"ahler $X$ with $c_1(X) = 0$
(Matsumura 1963 \emph{Proc.\ Japan Acad.}~39, Theorem~1;
Bogomolov 1974 \emph{Izv.\ Akad.\ Nauk SSSR Ser.\ Mat.}~38,
Theorem~1; Beauville 1983 \emph{J.\ Differential Geometry}~18 \S 2,
Theorem~A) reads: up to finite \'etale cover, $X$ splits as
$X = X_{\mathrm{strict}} \times X_{\mathrm{hk}} \times A$ with
$X_{\mathrm{strict}}$ strict Calabi--Yau ($\mathrm{Hol} = \mathrm{SU}
(d)$), $X_{\mathrm{hk}}$ hyperk\"ahler ($\mathrm{Hol} = \mathrm{Sp}(k)$),
$A$ abelian; and $\mathrm{Aut}^0(X) = \mathrm{Aut}^0(X_{\mathrm{strict}})
\times \mathrm{Aut}^0(X_{\mathrm{hk}}) \times A$ with
$\mathrm{Aut}^0(X_{\mathrm{strict}}) = \mathrm{Aut}^0(X_{\mathrm{hk}}) =
\{e\}$ by Bochner vanishing. Under the hypothesis $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$,
the abelian factor $A$ is trivial (any abelian factor of dimension
$k \geq 1$ contributes $h^{1,0}(A) = k \geq 1$ to $h^{1,0}(X)$ by
K\"unneth), and in the CY$_3$ dimension the hyperk\"ahler factor is
forbidden ($\mathrm{Sp}(k)$ is available only in even complex
dimension $2k$), so $X = X_{\mathrm{strict}}$ up to finite \'etale
cover: a strict CY$_3$ with $h^{2,0}(X) = 0$. Thus $h^0(X, T_X) =
h^{2,0}(X) = 0$, and $\mathrm{Aut}^0(X) = \{e\}$. Neither (O1) nor
(O3) is satisfiable on $X$: both reduce to the single fact
$\mathrm{Aut}^0(X) = \{e\}$.

\emph{(O1) $\Rightarrow$ (O4) via Van den Bergh, Bocklandt, Hartogs
(over $R = \mathbf C$).} Suppose toward contradiction that $X$ admits
a tilting bundle $T$ with $R = \mathrm{End}_{D^b\Coh(X)}(T)
= \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal O_X}(T, T)$ finite-dimensional over $\mathbf C$,
$\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_X(T, T) = 0$, and $D^b\Coh(X) \simeq D^b(R\mathrm{-mod})$
(Van den Bergh 2004, arXiv:math/0207170, Theorem~A; NCCR presentation
of $X$, where $R$ is a Noetherian $\mathbf C$-algebra of finite global
dimension $3$). Bocklandt 2008 (arXiv:math/0603558 Theorem~3.1) shows
any graded $3$-Calabi--Yau algebra of finite global dimension over
$R = \mathbf C$ is superpotential: $R \simeq J(Q, W) = \mathbf C Q /
\langle \partial_a W : a \in Q_1 \rangle$ for a finite quiver
$Q = (Q_0, Q_1)$ with cyclic superpotential
$W \in \mathbf C Q / [\mathbf C Q, \mathbf C Q]$. The vertex set
$Q_0 = \{1, \ldots, n\}$ and the dimension vector
$\mathbf v = (\dim_\mathbf C e_i T)_{i \in Q_0}$ give a gauge group
$G_{\mathbf v} = \prod_{i \in Q_0} \mathrm{GL}(\mathbf v_i, \mathbf C)$
acting by change of basis on the affine representation scheme
$\mathrm{Rep}(Q, \mathbf v) = \bigoplus_{a \in Q_1}
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbf C^{\mathbf v_{s(a)}}, \mathbf C^{\mathbf v_{t(a)}})$,
and the moduli-stack automorphism group is
$\mathrm{Aut}(R, \mathbf v) = G_{\mathbf v}/\mathbf C^\times$ (the
diagonal $\mathbf C^\times$ acts trivially; Domokos--Zubkov 2001,
\emph{Transform.\ Groups}~6 \S 2).

The key construction is the \emph{outer gauge torus}
$T_Q^{\mathrm{out}} = \mathrm{ker}(\mathbf Z^{Q_1} \to \mathbf Z)$
where the kernel is with respect to the total-arrow-count map
sending each arrow $a \in Q_1$ to $+1$, tensored over $\mathbf Z$
with $\mathbf C^\times$. Concretely, $T_Q^{\mathrm{out}}$ acts on
$\mathrm{Rep}(Q, \mathbf v)$ by $(\lambda_a \cdot \phi)_a = \lambda_a
\phi_a$ (scaling each arrow independently), and is required to
preserve the superpotential: $\lambda \cdot W = W$ in
$\mathbf C Q / [\mathbf C Q, \mathbf C Q]$ forces the constraint
$\prod_{a \in \text{cycle}} \lambda_a = 1$ for each cyclic term
in $W$. By the CY$_3$ superpotential condition (Bocklandt 2008
Theorem~3.1), $T_Q^{\mathrm{out}}$ has dimension equal to
$|Q_1| - n - \mathrm{rank}(W\text{-relations in kernel})$, which is
$\geq d - 1 = 2$ for any superpotential algebra with a non-trivial
CY$_3$ presentation (the standard count: $|Q_1|$ arrow scalings
minus $n - 1$ gauge redundancies minus the $W$-cycle constraints,
giving $\geq 2$ for $d = 3$). This torus commutes with $G_\mathbf v$
(arrow scaling commutes with basis change) and descends to a torus
action on the GIT quotient.

The derived McKay comparison (Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001,
arXiv:math/9908027, Theorem~1.2, applied to the affine
quiver-representation stack) gives
$X \simeq \mathrm{M}_\zeta(Q, W; \mathbf v) =
\mathrm{Rep}^{\zeta\text{-stable}}(J(Q, W), \mathbf v) \mathbin{/\mkern-6mu/}
G_\mathbf v$ as a King-type GIT quotient on the Jacobi algebra locus
$\{x \in \mathrm{Rep}(Q, \mathbf v) : \partial_a W(x) = 0 \forall a\}$.
Hartogs' extension theorem (Hartogs 1906; Griffiths--Harris 1978
\emph{Principles of Algebraic Geometry} \S 0 for the algebraic form)
ensures that every $G_\mathbf v$-equivariant regular function on
$\mathrm{Rep}(Q, \mathbf v)$ extends uniquely across the codimension-%
$\geq 2$ unstable locus to a $G_\mathbf v$-invariant regular function
on the stable open; together with the $T_Q^{\mathrm{out}}$-commuting
property, this gives a $T_Q^{\mathrm{out}}$-action on $X$ by regular
automorphisms, i.e., an algebraic subtorus
$T_Q^{\mathrm{out}} \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$ of positive
dimension. This contradicts $\mathrm{Aut}^0(X) = \{e\}$ established
above: (O1) forces (O4). The residual case $n = 1$ (one-vertex
quiver with loops) gives $R = \mathbf C\langle x, y, z\rangle / W$
free-Jacobi of dimension $3$; commutative image is $\mathbf C[x,y,z]$
whence $X \simeq \mathbf A^3$ affine toric, contradicting compactness.

\emph{(O2) is logically independent.} The BCOV one-loop class
$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X) = (\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24)[\Omega_X]^{0,1}
\in H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$ sources from the quantisation of
Kodaira--Spencer theory: the one-loop BV counter-term for the chiral
factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(X))$ is the obstruction to
gluing chart-wise $E_2$-deformations into a global sheaf of chiral
algebras (Costello--Li 2016, arXiv:1606.00365, Proposition~5.2). This
is a cohomological mechanism valued in $H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$ with
topological coefficient $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$; it is independent
as a source from the automorphism-geometry mechanism (O1 $\equiv$ O3)
valued in the tangent sheaf $H^0(X, T_X)$. Logical independence is
independence of cohomological source, not non-vanishing of the
numerical value: on the programme catalogue both factors in
$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ can independently vanish (quintic:
$H^{0,1} = 0$ so $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X_5) = 0$; $K3 \times E$:
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ so $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(K3 \times E) = 0$;
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-factor-split} factor-split),
yet (O2) remains an independent mechanism because its cohomological
source $H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$ is logically disjoint from the
source $H^0(X, T_X)$ driving (O1 $\equiv$ O3). Hence (O2) is not
reducible to any combination of (O1) or (O3) at the level of
mechanism type, and is logically independent.

\emph{Mechanism count.} The three reductions (O1) $\Leftrightarrow$
(O3), (O1) $\Rightarrow$ (O4), (O2) independent organise the obstruction
lattice as
\[
  \underbrace{(\mathrm{O}1 \equiv \mathrm{O}3)}_{\text{automorphism-geometry mechanism}}
  \;+\;
  \underbrace{(\mathrm{O}2)}_{\text{BV-anomaly mechanism}}
  \;+\;
  \underbrace{(\mathrm{O}4)}_{\text{downstream of (O1)}},
\]
hence two independent mechanisms plus one downstream implication:
$2.5$ mechanism types. On the compact $h^{0,1}=0$ locus the BV-anomaly
channel has zero value, so the effective obstruction is the
automorphism-geometry mechanism with (O4) downstream. Citing ``four
independent obstructions'' obscures the Sumihiro--Bogomolov--Matsumura
origin of (O1 $\equiv$ O3) and the downstream status of (O4).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[BCOV target: $H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$, not polyvector]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-target}
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere
The BCOV one-loop anomaly channel of a smooth compact CY$_3$ $X$ is valued as
\[
  \alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X) \;=\;
  \frac{\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)}{24}\, [\Omega_X]^{0,1}
  \;\in\; H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X),
\]
in the structure sheaf, not in any polyvector-field symmetric
power $H^{0,1}(X, \mathrm{Sym}^{\leq 2} T_X^*)$. The $(0,1)$-Dolbeault
representative $[\Omega_X]^{0,1}$ is the $\bar\partial$-primitive of the
Atiyah class $\mathrm{At}(\omega_X)$ of the holomorphic volume form;
the Calabi--Yau trivialisation $\omega_X \simeq \mathcal O_X$ reduces
$\mathrm{At}(\omega_X)$ to zero in the structure-sheaf Atiyah class,
so the actual cohomology class vanishes whenever $H^{0,1}(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$.
The proposition records the target and normalization of the channel, not
non-vanishing on the strict compact catalogue.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Costello--Li 2016 (arXiv:1606.00365, Proposition~5.2) computes the
one-loop BV anomaly coefficient of holomorphic BCOV theory on a
Calabi--Yau $X$ as $(\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24) [\Omega_X]^{0,1}$ with
cohomological target $H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$: the one-loop diagram
is a determinant of the twisted $\bar\partial$-operator on
$\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(X) = \bigoplus_{p, q} A^{0, q}(X, \wedge^p T_X)$
contracted to the scalar $(0, 1)$-sector by the CY pairing
$\int_X \mathrm{tr}(\cdot) \wedge \Omega_X$. The Atiyah--Calabi--Yau
trivialisation $\omega_X \simeq \mathcal O_X$ reduces the $p = 3$
sector to the structure sheaf, collapsing the anomaly target from
$H^{0,1}(X, \mathrm{Sym}^\bullet T_X^*)$ to $H^{0,1}(X, \mathcal O_X)$.
The BCOV ancestor (Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa 1994,
arXiv:hep-th/9309140, \S 4 eq.~(2.13)) first identified the coefficient
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$; Costello--Li fixed the target precisely.
Alternative putative targets in $H^{0,1}(X, \mathrm{Sym}^{\leq 2} T_X^*)$
conflate the BV anomaly with higher-order Kodaira--Spencer deformation
classes, which live in different cohomology groups and carry no
topological prefactor $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Four-case factor-split of
  \texorpdfstring{$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$}{alpha\_BCOV}]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-factor-split}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
The factor-product $(\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24) \cdot [\Omega_X]^{0,1}$
of the BCOV class admits four distinct vanishing mechanisms on CY$_3$
in the programme, distinguished by which of the two factors vanishes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(F\roman*)}]
  \item \textbf{Non-compact retractable}
        (conifold, local $\mathbb P^2$, local Hirzebruch and del Pezzo surfaces).
        $[\Omega_X]^{0,1} = 0$ via retraction to the compact Fano base
        with $h^{0,1} = 0$ on the Fano surface or the compact exceptional
        locus of the resolution; $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} \neq 0$ (conifold
        has $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 2$). Dolbeault-factor vanishing.
  \item \textbf{Strict compact $h^{0,1} = 0$} (quintic, bicubic,
        $(2,2,3)$-CI, generic Calabi--Yau complete intersection in
        weighted $\mathbf P^n$). $h^{0,1}(X) = 0$ trivialises the
        Dolbeault factor; $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) \neq 0$
        ($\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5) = -200$,
        $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_{3,3}) = -144$,
        $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_{2,2,3}) = -144$ by the
        Friedman--Morrison 1983 formula for CI in weighted $\mathbf P^n$).
        Cohomology-factor vanishing.
  \item \textbf{K\"unneth on $K3 \times E$}.
        $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times E) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) \cdot
        \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E) = 24 \cdot 0 = 0$ by the K\"unneth
        formula for topological Euler characteristic.
        $[\Omega_X]^{0,1}$ is non-zero (from the $E$-factor
        $H^{0,1}(E) = k$), but the topological prefactor vanishes.
        Topological-factor vanishing.
  \item \textbf{Abelian factor}
        ($X = X_{\mathrm{CY}} \times A$ with $A$ abelian variety).
        $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(A) = 0$ for every abelian variety of
        positive dimension, so the factor-product vanishes identically.
        Topological-factor vanishing, compound.
\end{enumerate}
The genuinely obstructing locus for (O2) is the complement: compact
CY$_3$ with $h^{0,1}(X) > 0$, no abelian factor in the
Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition, and $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) \neq 0$.
By Beauville 1983 \S 2 Theorem~A, any compact K\"ahler manifold with
$c_1(X) = 0$ splits (up to finite \'etale cover) as a product of
strict Calabi--Yau, hyperk\"ahler, and abelian factors; the
strict stratum has $h^{0,1} = 0$ by holonomy $\mathrm{Hol}(X) =
\mathrm{SU}(3)$ and Bochner vanishing, and any $h^{0,1} > 0$ forces an
abelian factor. Hence the complement is empty: (O2) is silent on the
entire programme catalogue of compact CY$_3$. The logical independence
of (O2) in Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-compact-cy3-25-count} is
therefore independence as a mechanism, not effective non-vanishing: on
every compact CY$_3$ the BCOV curving $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ is
numerically zero, yet its role as an independent cohomological
obstruction source is preserved by the factor-split.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(Fi).} The conifold $\mathbf Y = \mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal O(-1)
\oplus \mathcal O(-1))_{\mathbb P^1}$ deformation-retracts onto
$\mathbb P^1$, and $H^1(\mathbb P^1, \mathcal O) = 0$ by projectivity.
Local Fano surfaces such as $\mathbb P^2$, Hirzebruch surfaces, and del Pezzo
surfaces retract onto their compact base and have $h^{0,1}=0$. For general toric
resolutions of Gorenstein affine CY$_3$ singularities, the retraction
is onto the compact exceptional locus, a union of toric divisors each
of which has $h^{0,1} = 0$ by Fulton 1993 \S 3.5.

\emph{(Fii).} The quintic $X_5 \subset \mathbb P^4$ has
$h^{0,1}(X_5) = 0$ by Lefschetz on the ambient $\mathbb P^4$
(Griffiths--Harris 1978 \emph{Principles of Algebraic Geometry}, \S 2).
Same applies to bicubic $X_{3,3}$, $(2,2,3)$-CI $X_{2,2,3}$, and every
smooth projective CICY with ambient a product of projective spaces and
degree vector respecting Gorenstein--CI condition (Candelas--Dale--%
Lütken--Schimmrigk 1988 nuclear phys B~298). $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5) =
\int_{X_5} c_3(T_{X_5}) = -200$ by adjunction from
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathbb P^4) = 5$ and the quintic relation
$c(T_{X_5}) = c(T_{\mathbb P^4}|_{X_5})/c(\mathcal O(5)|_{X_5})$.

\emph{(Fiii).} The topological K\"unneth formula
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(A \times B) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(A) \cdot
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(B)$ (Spanier 1966, \emph{Algebraic Topology}, Ch.~5
Theorem~16) and $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E) = 0$ for elliptic curve $E$
give $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times E) = 24 \cdot 0 = 0$. The
elliptic-factor $(0,1)$-form $dz$ on $E$ pulls back to a non-zero class
in $H^{0,1}(K3 \times E) = H^{0,0}(K3) \otimes H^{0,1}(E) \oplus
H^{0,1}(K3) \otimes H^{0,0}(E) = k$ (only the first summand survives
since $h^{0,1}(K3) = 0$). The class $[\Omega_{K3 \times E}]^{0,1}$ is
non-zero in $H^{0,1}(K3 \times E, \mathcal O)$.

\emph{(Fiv).} For any abelian variety $A$ of positive dimension,
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(A) = 0$: abelian varieties are parallelisable
($T_A = \mathcal O_A^{\oplus \dim A}$), so all Chern classes
$c_i(T_A) = 0$ for $i \geq 1$, and $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(A) =
\int_A c_{\dim A}(T_A) = 0$. K\"unneth on $X = X_{\mathrm{CY}} \times A$
gives $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_{\mathrm{CY}})
\cdot 0 = 0$.

\emph{Effective locus.} A compact smooth CY$_3$ with $h^{0,1}(X) > 0$
carries a non-zero holomorphic $1$-form, hence by Bogomolov 1974
Theorem~1 and the Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition (Beauville 1983,
\emph{J.\ Differential Geometry}~18, \S 2) splits as $X' \times A$ for
abelian $A$, falling into case (Fiv). The complement of (Fi)--(Fiv)
is empty on the programme catalogue.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the $K3 \times E$ five-fold obstruction]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-25-count-vs-5fold}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-global-nccr-obstruction} lists five
obstructions to a global Van den Bergh \NCCR\ specifically on
$K3 \times E$; Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-compact-cy3-25-count}
lists four obstructions to the Hopf-algebra reconstruction
$\Phi_3(D^b\Coh(X)) = D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X))$ on every compact
smooth CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$. The two lists are different because
they obstruct different things and different hypothesis classes:
the five mechanisms of (i)--(v) rule out a single-chart tilting
module on a category with abelian sub-factor ($h^{1,0}(K3 \times E)
= h^{1,0}(K3) + h^{1,0}(E) = 0 + 1 = 1$ by K\"unneth, so
$K3 \times E$ falls outside the 2.5-count hypothesis $h^{1,0} = 0$);
the four mechanisms of (O1)--(O4) rule out the toric-equivariant
CoHA quiver presentation that would supply a Hopf algebra on the
Drinfeld-doubled critical cohomology. The five-fold is
K3-K\"ahler-specific; the $2.5$-count is universal on compact
CY$_3$ under $h^{1,0} = 0$. The $K3 \times E$ case is the K\"unneth
factor-split case (Fiii) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-factor-split}: the BCOV
(O2) topological prefactor $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times E) =
\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) \cdot \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E) = 24 \cdot 0 = 0$
vanishes by the topological K\"unneth formula, so $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}
(K3 \times E) = 0$ identically; the obstruction to the global Hopf
algebra descends through the five mechanisms (i)--(v) of the
five-fold, with the Oberdieck--Pandharipande modular character
$-1/\Phi_{10}$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-quasi-nccr-positive-half-character})
surviving as the Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ positive-half
character on the isotrivially fibred locus.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ $5$-fold substitute catalogue]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-serre-eq-quasi-nccr-5fold}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
On a compact smooth CY$_3$ $X$ with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$ falling in
factor-split case (Fii) or (Fiii) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-factor-split}, the Serre-equivariant
quasi-\NCCR\ of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-serre-equivariant-quasi-nccr}
replaces five classical constructions that all fail, each for an
independent reason:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(S\roman*)}]
  \item \textbf{Van den Bergh reflexive-tilting.} Fails: any reflexive
        tilting module $M$ has
        $\mathrm{Ext}^3(M, M) \neq 0$ by
        Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}.
        (Van den Bergh 2004 arXiv:math/0207170 \S 4.1.)
  \item \textbf{Bridgeland--King--Reid derived McKay.} Fails: requires
        $X = [\mathbf C^3/G]$ with $G \subset \mathrm{SU}(3)$ finite,
        with class group $G^{\mathrm{ab}}$ finite abelian; compact
        CY$_3$ has infinite class group (e.g.\ $\mathrm{Cl}(X_5) =
        \mathbf Z$ generated by hyperplane class, in contrast to
        $\mathrm{Cl}([\mathbf C^3/\mu_3]) = \mathbf Z/3$).
        (Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001 arXiv:math/9908027 Theorem~1.2;
        class-group comparison between conifold/orbifold and compact
        CY$_3$.)
  \item \textbf{Kuznetsov HPD self-dual.} Fails on the strict compact
        stratum: HPD self-duality (Kuznetsov 2007, arXiv:math/0610957,
        Theorem~7.7) applies to smooth cubic fourfolds and Pfaffian CY,
        not to the quintic or K3$\times$E whose derived categories are
        the whole Kuznetsov component (no non-trivial HPD decomposition).
  \item \textbf{Mukai lattice vanishing.} Fails: the Mukai lattice
        $\widetilde H(X, \mathbf Z) = H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \mathbf Z)$
        of Mukai 1984 (\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~77 \S 1; signature
        $(4, 20)$ at the standard Hodge involution) for a compact
        K\"ahler variety with trivial canonical class is defined only
        in the CY$_2$ = K3 case as a rank-$24$ lattice carrying the
        Mukai pairing and a faithful Hodge structure pinning
        derived autoequivalences to the $\widetilde O^+(\widetilde
        H(X, \mathbf Z))$ index-two subgroup (Orlov 2002,
        arXiv:math/0206295, Theorem~1.1). The CY$_3$ analogue is the
        numerical Grothendieck group $K^{\mathrm{num}}_0(D^b\Coh(X))$
        equipped with the Euler pairing $\chi(E, F) = \sum_k (-1)^k
        \dim \mathrm{Ext}^k(E, F)$; on a strict compact CY$_3$,
        $K^{\mathrm{num}}_0(D^b\Coh(X)) \otimes \mathbf Q \simeq
        H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \mathbf Q)$ of rank $2 + 2 h^{1,1}(X)$
        (two trivial ranks $H^0, H^6$ plus two equal ranks
        $H^2, H^4$ by Poincar\'e duality), and the Euler pairing is
        degenerate on a codimension-$2$ isotropic sublattice when the
        Hodge decomposition is triangular (no non-trivial $(p, q)$-%
        diagonal blocks beyond $(0, 0), (3, 3)$). This degeneracy
        obstructs a Mukai-style full tilting object: a tilting NCCR
        requires the numerical Grothendieck group to be of maximal
        rank carrying a non-degenerate Euler pairing, which holds on
        K3 via the rank-$24$ Mukai lattice but fails on strict
        CY$_3$.
  \item \textbf{Brav--Bussi--Joyce symmetric obstruction theory.} Fails
        as a single-chart object: the symmetric $[-1]$-shifted symplectic
        structure on the moduli stack $\mathrm{Perf}(X)$
        (Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2013, arXiv:1111.3209,
        Theorem~$2.5$; Brav--Bussi--Joyce 2019, arXiv:1305.6302,
        Theorem~5.18 on local Darboux models of $[-1]$-shifted
        symplectic derived schemes) is defined globally on the derived
        moduli stack but admits no single-chart Darboux parametrisation
        on the compact CY$_3$ (Brav--Dyckerhoff 2019, arXiv:1812.11913,
        \S 4: local-to-global gluing obstruction is precisely
        $\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(X)$ of
        Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-target}).
\end{enumerate}
Each of (Si)--(Sv) rules out a classical construction; the
Serre-equivariant quasi-\NCCR\ is the stratified replacement: a sheaf
of tilted algebras over $\Stab(D^b\Coh(X))$ with locally constant
chambers, wall-crossing transitions by Seidel--Thomas twists, and the
Serre-equivariance axiom replacing the single-chart tilting condition.
The positive-half character descends to the modular DT generating
function of Oberdieck--Pandharipande on $K3 \times E$, and to the
Katz--Klemm--Vafa modular generating function on the quintic
(Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010, arXiv:1001.1821,
Theorem~1).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Each of (Si)--(Sv) is a direct specialisation of the cited primary
sources. (Si) is Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction} applied
to VdB 2004 \S 4.1 on compact CY$_3$. (Sii) is the class-group
comparison of Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001 Theorem~1.2 against
Matsumura--Beauville--Bogomolov automorphism rigidity. (Siii) is
Kuznetsov 2007 Theorem~7.7 applied to the compact CY$_3$ stratum where
the Kuznetsov component is the whole derived category
(Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-quintic-kuznetsov-full}). (Siv) contrasts
the rank-$24$ Mukai lattice of Mukai 1984 on K3 (signature $(4, 20)$,
faithful Hodge structure, Orlov's derived-Torelli) with the Euler-%
degenerate numerical Grothendieck group $K^{\mathrm{num}}_0(D^b\Coh(X))$
of rank $2 + 2h^{1,1}(X)$ on strict compact CY$_3$; the Euler pairing
fails to be non-degenerate on any codimension-$2$ isotropic sublattice,
obstructing a Mukai-style full tilting object. (Sv) is
Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2013 Theorem~2.5 $[-1]$-shifted
symplectic structure plus Brav--Dyckerhoff 2019 \S 4 local-to-global
obstruction being the BCOV class of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-bcov-target}. The Serre-equivariant
quasi-\NCCR\ of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-serre-equivariant-quasi-nccr}
circumvents all five: local-constancy on Bridgeland chambers replaces
single-chart VdB reflexive-tilting (Si); wall-crossing Seidel--Thomas
twists replace derived-McKay orbifold data (Sii); the Serre-equivariance
axiom replaces HPD duality (Siii); the DT character on the stratified
manifold replaces Mukai lattice rank (Siv); and the sheaf structure
over $\Stab$ replaces the single-chart symmetric obstruction theory
Darboux presentation (Sv).
\end{proof}

\section{Consequences for the pentagon framework}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-pentagon-consequences}

\begin{corollary}[Scope of Conjecture CY-C]
\label{cor:cy-c-beyond-pentagon-scope}
\ClaimStatusConditional
Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism} (CY-C as six-route convergence) applies only
to the isotrivially fibred stratum of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-tri-stratum-exhaustive}, where all six routes have
well-defined inputs. On the strict stratum, Conjecture CY-C reduces to the
category-level statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}\textup{(i)} and cannot be upgraded to an
