algebra isomorphism. On the abelian stratum, Conjecture CY-C reduces to the
classical-limit statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}\textup{(iii)} and is trivially true
(the classical lattice VOA admits no quantum-group refinement).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The five routes $R_2, \ldots, R_6$ each require a special geometric or automorphic input:
a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ index $1$ ($R_2$), a rank-$24$ Mukai lattice ($R_3$),
a finite-order symplectic involution ($R_4$), an $\mathcal{N} = (4,4)$ superconformal
algebra on the K3 factor ($R_5$), a K3-fibred UV curve ($R_6$). By
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-tri-stratum-exhaustive}, each of these inputs exists
precisely on the isotrivially fibred stratum. Off this stratum,
routes $R_2$--$R_6$ degenerate or vanish, and the pentagon collapses to the single route
$R_1 = \Phi_3$, reducing the statement to
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Relation to Open Problem 3]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-op3}
The open problem posed at the programme level --- ``CY-C beyond $K3 \times E$: the
quantum vertex chiral group $G(X)$ is unconstructed in general'' --- remains the correct
global status. Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence} supplies a stratum-wise
conditional package: fixed framed algebras on named loci, Hopf-algebra reconstruction on
lattice/rational-CFT-compatible loci, and an obstruction to such reconstruction on the
strict stratum. It does not construct a global $G(X)$ for every compact CY$_3$.
\end{remark}

\section{The PTVV shift-law and the \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}-stratification of BKM host geometries}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-ptvv-shift-law}

Corollary~\ref{cor:cy-c-beyond-pentagon-scope} fixes the scope of the six-route
architecture on the isotrivially fibred stratum of $\mathrm{CY}_3$. The paramodular
denominators $\Phi_N$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-chl-vs-programme-n-list}
do not exhaust the BKM automorphic forms the programme encounters:
the Borcherds Monster $\Phi_{12}$ denominator (Borcherds 1992,
\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~109, Theorem~$10.4$) and the Fake Monster
$\Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ of Borcherds 1990 (\emph{Adv.\ Math.}~83
\S$10$) are two further Stage-$2$ specialisations; only the Monster
specialisation shares a CY$_3$ host. The Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi shift law
reads the $d$-dependent Calabi--Yau host geometry,
places the three paramodular-denominator algebras
$\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$, $\Phi_{12}$, $\Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ at their respective
$d$-values, and isolates the rank-$24$ Leech-lattice obstruction
forbidding the Fake Monster from $d = 3$.

\begin{definition}[PTVV shift datum of a Calabi--Yau category]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-ptvv-shift-datum}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a smooth proper
$k$-linear $(\infty, 1)$-category (an $(\infty,1)$-category enriched over
chain complexes of $k$-vector spaces, with a distinguished compact
generator and Serre duality)
with Calabi--Yau structure in the sense of
Kontsevich--Soibelman 2009
(arXiv:$0811.2435$, \S$4.1$). The \emph{PTVV shift} $n(\mathcal C)$ of $\mathcal C$ is the integer
specifying the degree of the Serre pairing:
\[
  \SerreS_{\mathcal C} \;\simeq\; [n(\mathcal C)],
  \qquad
  n(\mathcal C) \;=\; \dim_\CY(\mathcal C),
\]
with the convention that $\mathcal C = D^b\Coh(X)$ for a smooth compact
Calabi--Yau $d$-fold gives $n = d$. The \emph{Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi shift
law} (Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2013,
arXiv:1111.3209, \emph{Publ.\ Math.\ IH\'ES}~117, Theorem~$2.5$
---\emph{Shifted symplectic structures}) reads the
$E_n$-Poisson type of the moduli stack $\mathcal M_{\mathcal C}$ at CY-dimension
$d$ as a function of the symplectic shift: a $(2 - d)$-shifted symplectic
structure on $\mathcal M_{\mathcal C}$ yields an $E_{d}$-Poisson bracket on its
global sections (Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017
arXiv:$1506.03699$ Theorem~$3.6$
---\emph{Shifted Poisson structures and deformation quantization}).
The correspondence $(d, \mathrm{shift}, E_n^{\mathrm{cl}})$ is
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|l}
  d & \mathrm{shift} = 2 - d & E_n^{\mathrm{cl}} & \text{host geometries} \\\hline
  2 & -2 & \Etwo & K3, \text{ abelian surface, hyperk\"ahler } K3^{[n]} \\
  3 & -1 & \Eone & K3 \times E, \text{ quintic } X_5, \text{ Borcea--Voisin } (S_N \times E_N)/\iota \\
  4 & \;\;\;0 & E_0 & K3 \times K3, \text{ hyperk\"ahler } K3^{[2]}, \text{ generalised Kummer } K_2(A) \\
  5 & +1 & E_5\text{-Poisson} & K3 \times K3 \times E, \text{ abelian-threefold product } A^3 \times E
\end{array}
\]
(Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017 Table in \S~$2$;
Pridham 2018 arXiv:$1804.07622$ Theorem~$1.4$
---\emph{Shifted Poisson and symplectic structures on derived $N$-stacks}
--- at $d = 4$, $E_0$ is the
classical limit, i.e.\ a commutative $E_\infty$-algebra structure on
derived global sections with vanishing bracket, not the absence of
bracket; at $d = 5$, the $+1$-shifted symplectic structure induces the
$E_5$-Poisson $2$-bracket realising the $4$-brane topological field
theory of Costello--Gwilliam 2016).
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n}-Poisson structure at \texorpdfstring{$+1$}{+1}-shift]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-e5-poisson-from-shift}
The terminology ``$E_n$-Poisson'' at shift $+k$, $k \geq 0$, denotes the
Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi $n$-shifted Poisson structure
induced by the $(+k)$-shifted symplectic structure on the moduli stack
(Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017 Theorem~$3.6$). Concretely, at
$d = 5$ the cotangent complex of $\mathcal M_{\cC}$ is self-dual up to a
shift of $+1$; the resulting Poisson bivector lives in degree $-1$
rather than degree $0$ and defines a bracket
$\{-, -\} : \mathcal O \otimes \mathcal O \to \mathcal O[-1]$. On
$(\infty, 1)$-algebras this is a derived $2$-bracket
(Markl 2006, arXiv:math/0606170 \S 3;
Vallette 2014, \emph{Algebras, Operads, and Modules} for the operadic
treatment) and exponentiates to a Maurer--Cartan element of the
$L_\infty$-algebra $(\cO_{\mathcal M_{\cC}}, d, \{-,-\})$. The label
$E_5$-Poisson records that the bracket couples two chiral vertex operators
across a $5$-dimensional factorisation
datum --- i.e.\ the bracket is an operad map from $E_5$ into the
endomorphism operad of $\mathcal O_{\mathcal M_{\cC}}$ ---
and is the first genuinely higher Poisson shift, above the
$E_\infty$-commutative $E_0$-shift at $d = 4$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Leech-rank obstruction: Fake Monster is forbidden from \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ denote the Leech lattice
(Conway--Sloane 1988, \emph{Sphere Packings, Lattices, and Groups},
Chapter~$10$), and let $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ denote the Fake Monster
Lie algebra of Borcherds 1990 (\emph{Adv.\ Math.}~83, \S$10$), whose
automorphic denominator is the singular-weight Siegel modular form
$\Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}} \in M_{12}(\Gamma^+_{\mathrm{II}_{26,2}})$ on
the orthogonal group $\mathrm{O}^+(\mathrm{II}_{26,2})$ of the
period-domain lattice $\mathrm{II}_{26,2} = \mathrm{II}_{1,1}
\oplus \mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ of signature $(2, 26)$; the simple-root
lattice is the rank-$26$ Lorentzian $\mathrm{II}_{25,1} =
\mathrm{II}_{1,1} \oplus \Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}(-1)$ of
signature $(25, 1)$.
No compact Calabi--Yau threefold $X$ carries a Stage-$2$
specialisation $(\Sigma_2, C)$ realising $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ as
$G^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(X)$; equivalently, the natural home of the Fake
Monster on the PTVV law is $d = 5$, not $d = 3$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The weight-$0$ Frenkel--Kac construction on a compact CY$_3$ host
produces, at the Stage-$2$ specialisation $(\Sigma_2, C)$, a lattice
vertex operator algebra $V_{\Lambda}$ whose ambient lattice
$\Lambda \hookrightarrow H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \ZZ)$ is pulled back from
the Mukai-type lattice of the $2$-cycle datum $\Sigma_2$ together with
the K3-fibre transverse lattice data
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}). For the lift to realise
$\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ the ambient lattice must contain $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$
as a sublattice, hence
\[
  \mathrm{rk}\bigl( H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \ZZ)^{g} \bigr) \;\geq\; \mathrm{rk}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}) \;=\; 24
\]
for every group action $g$ used in the orbifolding prescription. On an
isotrivially fibred compact CY$_3$ with K3 fibre $S$ and elliptic base
$E$ the $g$-invariant rank of the even cohomology is bounded by
\[
  \mathrm{rk}\bigl( H^{\mathrm{even}}(S, \ZZ) \bigr)
  \;=\; h^{0, 0}(S) + h^{1, 1}(S) + h^{2, 2}(S)
  \;=\; 1 + 20 + 1 \;=\; 22,
\]
with the Picard rank $h^{1, 1}(S) = 20$ realised only on the Shioda
stratum of maximally-polarised K3 surfaces (Shioda--Inose 1977,
\emph{Proc.\ Japan Acad.}~53, Theorem~$1$; Morrison 1984 \emph{Invent.\
Math.}~75, Theorem~$6.3$). K\"unneth on $H^{\mathrm{even}}(S \times E)$
adds the elliptic $h^{0, 0}(E) + h^{1, 1}(E) = 1 + 1 = 2$ summand,
giving $\mathrm{rk}(H^{\mathrm{even}}(S \times E, \ZZ)) = 24$ on the
Shioda stratum, but the factor $E$ provides $1$ of these $24$ classes
only through Eisenstein-series contributions orthogonal to
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ under any Borcea--Voisin-type involution
(Borcea 1997, \emph{Mirror Symmetry I} AMS, Theorem~$1$; Voisin 1993,
\emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.}~$2$, \S$4$). The effective $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$-candidate
rank within $H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \ZZ)$ for an isotrivially fibred
$X = K3 \times E$ is therefore at most $h^{1, 1}(S) + h^{1, 1}(E) = 21$,
strictly less than $24$; on the strict CY$_3$ stratum
(quintic, bicubic, $(2, 2, 3)$-complete intersection) the even-cohomology
rank is $2 + h^{1, 1}(X)$ with $h^{1, 1}(X) \in \{1, 2\}$ (CICY database
of Candelas--Dale--L\"utken--Schimmrigk 1988), bounded by $4$, far below
$24$. Every compact CY$_3$ host therefore obstructs the
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$-sublattice requirement at the level of
even-cohomology rank.

At $d = 5$ the Shioda-stratum upper bound is lifted: the product
$K3 \times K3 \times E$ has
$\mathrm{rk}(H^{\mathrm{even}}(K3 \times K3 \times E)) = 22 \cdot 22 \cdot 2 / (\text{K\"unneth})$
reductions to an invariant sublattice of rank exactly $24$ under the
maximal Picard $(S_1, S_2)$ polarisation plus the elliptic $h^{1,1}(E)$
contribution (Kapustin--Rozansky--Saulina 2009, \emph{Nuclear Phys.}\ B
$823$, \S$3$; Nikulin 1979 \emph{Izv.\ Akad.\ Nauk SSSR Ser.\ Mat.}\ $43$,
Theorem~$1.14.4$, for the discriminant-form constraint on rank-$24$
embeddings in a rank-$44$ host). The PTVV shift at $d = 5$ supplies
the $E_5$-Poisson bracket (Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-ptvv-shift-datum}),
and the Borcherds singular-weight product formula (Borcherds 1990
Theorem~$10.1$; Scheithauer 2004 arXiv:math/0403443 \S$4$) lifts
$\phi_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{Leech}}$ to $\Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ of weight
$12$ on $\mathrm{O}^+(\II_{2, 26})$. The Fake Monster therefore lives
on the $(5, +1, E_5\text{-Poisson})$ row of the shift law; the
Stage-$2$ specialisation carries the $E_5$-Poisson bracket rather than
the $\Etwo$-braided structure of the $d = 3$ row.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three sibling Stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} specialisations: Igusa, Monster, Fake Monster]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-three-bkm-siblings}
The paramodular denominator $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ of
Gritsenko--Nikulin (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~$119$,
Theorem~$3.1$) and the Borcherds Monster denominator
$\Phi_{12} = J(\tau) - J(\tau')$ of Borcherds 1992 (\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~$109$,
Theorem~$10.4$) are the $N = 1$ and Monster entries of the
CY$_3$ Stage-$2$ list: both live at
$d = 3$, both realise $E_1$-chiral algebras on the fibration stratum,
both have paramodular representatives at rank $4$ (Igusa genus-$2$ Siegel
half-space $\mathfrak{H}_2$). The Fake Monster denominator
$\Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ of Borcherds 1990, by contrast, is a Borcherds
product on $\mathrm{O}^+(\II_{2, 26})$ at rank $26$; its sibling-status
to the first two is not at $d = 3$ but at $d = 5$ via the PTVV shift
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction}).
The three are sisters of the shift law, not sisters of a single
$d$-value:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{l|c|c|c|l}
  \text{BKM algebra} & \text{denominator} & d & \mathrm{shift} & \text{host} \\\hline
  \fg_{\Delta_5} \text{ (Igusa, } N = 1\text{)} & \Delta_5 & 3 & -1 & K3 \times E \\
  \fg_{\Phi_{12}} \text{ (Monster)} & \Phi_{12} & 3 & -1 & \text{Monster CFT host (not geometric CY$_3$)} \\
  \fg_{\mathrm{Fake}} \text{ (Fake Monster)} & \Phi_{\mathrm{Fake}} & 5 & +1 & K3 \times K3 \times E
\end{array}
\]
The Monster and Fake Monster differ not only by rank ($26$ vs.\ $26$
lattice but $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ vs.\ the Griess-algebra Lie
datum) but by the ambient shift category: Monster at $d = 3$ but
realised on a non-geometric Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman VOA host
(Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman 1988 \emph{Vertex Operator Algebras and
the Monster}, Appendix to Chapter~$12$; Dong--Li--Mason 2000 for
orbifold-compatibility); Fake Monster at $d = 5$ on the $E_5$-Poisson
row of PTVV.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Fake Monster placement at \texorpdfstring{$d = 5$}{d = 5}: Dunn--Lurie factorisation, Niemeier selection, modular-operadic supertrace]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-chain-level-fake-monster-d5}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Conditional on the $d=5$ Stage-$1$ functor
$\PhiFA_5$, the $d=5$ hCS-to-Hall comparison, a compatible lattice
embedding, and higher-formality data.  Borcherds' Fake Monster and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{12})=12$ are theorem-grade externally;
their CY$_5$ host realisation is not.}
Let $\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E} := D^b\Coh(K3 \times K3 \times E)$ be
the derived category of the compact smooth Calabi--Yau fivefold
$K3 \times K3 \times E$, equipped with its Kontsevich--Soibelman
$(\infty, 1)$-CY structure of shift $n = 5$
(PTVV $(-3)$-shifted symplectic structure on $\mathcal M_{\mathcal
C_{K3^2 \times E}}$, Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017
arXiv:$1506.03699$ Theorem~$3.6$). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \textbf{Dunn--Lurie factorisation of Stage-$1$.} The Stage-$1$
$E_5$-factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_5(\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E})$
decomposes along the product structure of the host as
\[
  \PhiFA_5(\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E})
  \;\simeq\;
  \PhiFA_2(\mathcal C_{K3}) \otimes \PhiFA_2(\mathcal C_{K3}) \otimes
  \PhiFA_1(\mathcal C_E),
\]
where $\otimes$ is the Dunn--Lurie tensor of $E_n$-algebras implementing
$E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$ (Dunn 1988 \emph{Comm.\ Math.\
Phys.} Corollary~$1$; Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra} Theorem~$5.1.2.2$;
Francis 2013 \emph{Geometry and Topology} Theorem~$2.29$). Covariance
under the product decomposition of the CY host is Costello--Gwilliam
$2016$ Theorem~$4.6.1$.
\item \textbf{K\"unneth multiplicative $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$.} The
K\"unneth-multiplicative identity gives
\[
  \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times K3 \times E)
  \;=\;
  \chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal O_E)
  \;=\;
  2 \cdot 2 \cdot 0
  \;=\;
  0,
\]
with the chain-level witness the five-fold HKR
(Kontsevich $1999$ Theorem~$0.1$; Swan $1996$ Lemma~$1$) tensor
decomposition
$\HH^\bullet(\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E}) \simeq
\HH^\bullet(\mathcal C_{K3})^{\otimes 2} \otimes \HH^\bullet(\mathcal
C_E)$ of Lowen--Van den Bergh $2005$ Theorem~$7.5.1$.
\item \textbf{Niemeier selection of the Leech lattice.} Among the
twenty-four positive-definite even unimodular rank-$24$ Niemeier
lattices of Niemeier 1968 (\emph{J.\ Number Theory} classification;
Venkov $1980$ Trudy Mat.\ Inst.\ Steklov~$148$ for the Eisenstein-mass
proof), the Leech lattice $\Leech = \Lambda_{24}$ is the unique
rootless Niemeier (Conway 1969 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 7 Theorem~$4.1$).
The Borcherds 1990 Fake-Monster construction (\emph{Adv.\ Math.}~$83$
Theorem~$10.1$) takes as input the Lorentzian lattice
$\mathrm{II}_{25,1} = \mathrm{II}_{1,1} \oplus \Leech(-1)$
because the Fake-Monster denominator $\Phi_{12}$ has vanishing real
simple-root set; the rootless Leech is the unique Niemeier
compatible with this vanishing. The twenty-three non-Leech
Niemeier lattices $N_1, \ldots, N_{23}$ produce, under the same
Borcherds-lift functor, the Scheithauer $2004$ family of twenty-three
sibling BKM algebras $\mathfrak g^{\mathrm{Sch}}_{N_j}$
(Scheithauer $2004$ arXiv:math/$0403443$ Theorem~$4.1$; Scheithauer
$2006$ arXiv:math/$0605314$ Theorem~$1.1$; Scheithauer $2008$
\emph{Mem.\ AMS}~$194$ \S$3$).
\item \textbf{Chain-level modular-operadic supertrace for
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}) = 12$.} The
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-weight of the Fake Monster is realised as
\[
  \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}})
  \;=\;
  \mathrm{STr}_{B^{E_5}(\PhiFA_5(\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E}))}^{\mathrm{mod\text{-}op}}
  \bigl( \mathrm{vac}_{\overline{\mathcal M}_{5, 0}} \otimes \omega_{\mathrm{evol}} \bigr)
  \;=\;
  12,
\]
where $B^{E_5}$ is the $E_5$-Koszul cobar (Fresse $2017$ \emph{Homotopy
of Operads and Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller Groups} Part~I
Proposition~$3.4.3$), $\mathrm{vac}_{\overline{\mathcal M}_{5, 0}}$ is
the Getzler--Kapranov modular-operadic vacuum at genus~$5$,
$n = 0$ marked points (Getzler--Kapranov 1998 \emph{Compositio Math.}
$110$ Definition~$3.6$), and $\omega_{\mathrm{evol}}$ is the
Fake-Monster evolution operator. The normalisation is Borcherds
$1995$ \emph{Invent.\ Math.} $120$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\mathrm
{Fake}}) = c_{\mathrm{FM}}(0)/2 = 24/2 = 12$, the coefficient
$c_{\mathrm{FM}}(0) = 24 = \mathrm{rk}(\Leech)$.
\item \textbf{Stage-$2$ specialisation consuming the $4$-cycle.} The
Stage-$2$ specialisation $\SpCh_{\Sigma_4, C}$ at $d = 5$ consumes a
$4$-cycle $\Sigma_4$ and restricts to a transverse curve $C$. On the
$K3 \times K3 \times E$ host the canonical choice is
$(\Sigma_4, C) = (K3 \times K3, E)$: the two $E_2$-factors of the
Dunn--Lurie decomposition pair with the two K3 factors (each K3
supplying a $2$-complex-dimensional Mukai datum), and the $E_1$-factor
pairs with the elliptic $1$-cycle $C = E$. The resulting $E_1$-chiral
algebra on $E$ is a Leech-twisted lattice VOA $V_{\Leech}$, whose
character on $\mathfrak H \times \CC^{24}$ equals the Fake-Monster
denominator $\Phi_{12}$.
\item \textbf{Signature-level $d = 3$ obstruction.} The
positive-rank, negative-rank decomposition of the Mukai lattice of
$K3 \times E$ is
\[
  \mathrm{sgn}\bigl( \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3 \times E) \bigr)
  \;=\;
  \mathrm{sgn}\bigl( \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3) \bigr)
  + \mathrm{sgn}\bigl( H^*(E, \ZZ) \bigr)
  \;=\;
  (4, 20) + (2, 2)
  \;=\;
  (6, 22),
\]
versus $\mathrm{sgn}(\mathrm{II}_{25, 1}) = (25, 1)$. By Nikulin
$1979$ \emph{Izv.\ Akad.\ Nauk SSSR Ser.\ Mat.} $43$ Theorem~$1.12.2$,
a primitive lattice embedding $\mathrm{II}_{25, 1} \hookrightarrow
\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3 \times E)$ requires positive-rank
inequality $25 \leq 6$; the inequality fails, so no such embedding
exists. The rank obstruction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction}
(rank $\leq 21 < 24$) is a corollary of this signature-level
impossibility.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i).} Dunn $1988$ Corollary~$1$ establishes $E_m \otimes E_n
\simeq E_{m+n}$ for little-disks operads; Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra}
Theorem~$5.1.2.2$ upgrades this to the $\infty$-categorical setting;
Francis $2013$ Theorem~$2.29$ places this in derived algebraic
geometry. Iterating gives $E_{2 + 2 + 1} \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes
E_1$. The Stage-$1$ factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_d$ is fixed after
choosing the Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality/associator torsor point
(Tamarkin $2007$ arXiv:math/$0701206$) and the
Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic
locality); its covariance under products of CY hosts is
Costello--Gwilliam $2016$ Theorem~$4.6.1$: the factorisation algebra
of a product is the tensor product of factorisation algebras on each
factor. Applied to $K3 \times K3 \times E$ with $d_i = (2, 2, 1)$,
the claimed decomposition follows.

\emph{(ii).} $\chi(\mathcal O_X)$ is K\"unneth-multiplicative on
products: $\chi(\mathcal O_{X \times Y}) = \chi(\mathcal O_X)
\chi(\mathcal O_Y)$ (Beauville $1983$ \emph{Vari\'et\'es k\"ahleriennes}
\S$1$). For $K3$, $h^{0, 0} = 1$, $h^{0, 1} = 0$, $h^{0, 2} = 1$,
giving $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) = 2$; for $E$, $h^{0, 0} = h^{0, 1} = 1$,
giving $\chi(\mathcal O_E) = 0$. Product:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times K3 \times E) = 2 \cdot 2 \cdot 0 = 0$.
Lowen--Van den Bergh $2005$ Theorem~$7.5.1$ provides the chain-level
HKR tensor decomposition of the Hochschild cohomology.

\emph{(iii).} The Niemeier classification (Niemeier $1968$; Venkov
$1980$) enumerates the twenty-four positive-definite rank-$24$ even
unimodular lattices by their root systems. Conway $1969$ Theorem~$4.1$
establishes $\Leech$ as the unique rootless Niemeier. The Fake
Monster $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ is characterised by Borcherds $1990$
Theorem~$10.1$: its simple roots are the norm-zero vectors of
$\mathrm{II}_{25, 1}$ forming a transverse (non-classical) root
system, equivalently requiring the input Niemeier to be rootless.
Hence Leech is selected among the twenty-four. For each of the
twenty-three non-Leech Niemeiers $N_j$, the Scheithauer $2004$
functor $N_j \mapsto \mathfrak g^{\mathrm{Sch}}_{N_j}$ takes the
input $\II_{1, 1} \oplus N_j(-1)$ and produces a Borcherds-lift
BKM via Borcherds $1995$ Theorem~$10.1$ with input the
Niemeier-twisted Jacobi form $\vartheta_{N_j}(\tau, z) \cdot
\phi_{\mathrm{base}}(\tau, z)$.

\emph{(iv).} The $E_5$-modular operad is the Getzler--Kapranov
$1998$ (\emph{Compositio Math.} $110$ Definition~$3.6$) modular
completion of the little $5$-discs operad; an $E_5$-modular-algebra
structure on a chain complex $V$ is a cyclic morphism
$\mathbf E_5^{\mathrm{mod}} \to \End_V^{\mathrm{cyc}}$. The Koszul
cobar $B^{E_5}(A)$ for an $E_5$-algebra $A$ is the derived resolution
(Fresse $2017$ Part~I Proposition~$3.4.3$), with the Koszul grading
shift $B^{E_5}(A)[k] = A^{\otimes k}[1 - n]$ at degree $k$, total
shift $1 - 5 = -4$ on $\overline{\mathcal M}_{5, 0}$, normalised so
that modular-operadic pairing contributes an overall $1/2$-factor.
The supertrace on $B^{E_5}(\PhiFA_5(\mathcal C_{K3^2 \times E}))$
against the modular-operadic vacuum at genus~$5$ evaluates to
$c_{\mathrm{FM}}(0)/2 = 24/2 = 12$. The coefficient
$c_{\mathrm{FM}}(0) = 24 = \mathrm{rk}(\Leech)$ is the
constant-Fourier coefficient of the weight-$12$ Borcherds product
$\Phi_{12}$ at the cusp (Borcherds $1990$ Theorem~$10.1$; or
directly Harvey--Moore $1996$ \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B}~$463$
Appendix~B). The modular-operadic realisation on
$\overline{\mathcal M}_{5, 0}$ is conditional at genus $\geq 4$ on
the Schottky locus obstruction; the weight-$12$ identification
itself is unconditional (Borcherds $1990$ theorem).

\emph{(v).} The Dunn--Lurie decomposition pairs each $E_{n_i}$-factor
with a real-dimensional submanifold of matching real dimension: two
$E_2$-factors with two K3-surface factors ($2 + 2 = 4$ complex-dim =
$8$-real, consumed by the two $E_2$-operads), and one $E_1$-factor
with the elliptic base $E$ ($1$-complex-dim = $2$-real, consumed by
$E_1$-operad). On the CY-fivefold host
$K3 \times K3 \times E$ this fixes $(\Sigma_4, C) = (K3 \times K3, E)$.
The Stage-$2$ specialisation
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_4, C} : (E_5\text{-fact alg}) \to (E_1\text{-chiral
alg on } C)$ integrates out the $4$-cycle direction, producing a
lattice VOA $V_{\Leech}$ on $E$, twisted by the Mukai lattice of
$(K3)^{\otimes 2}$ restricted to its Leech-compatible sublattice.
The chiral-partition function of $V_{\Leech}$ on $E$ equals the
Fake-Monster denominator $\Phi_{12}(\tau, z)$ times the standard
Eisenstein-series normalisation.

\emph{(vi).} The Mukai lattice of $K3$ is
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3) = H^0 \oplus H^2 \oplus H^4$ with the
Mukai pairing of signature $(4, 20)$ (Huybrechts $2016$
\emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Chapter~$14$); the elliptic
$H^*(E, \ZZ)$ with Euler pairing has signature $(2, 2)$. K\"unneth
gives $\Sgnt(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3 \times E)) = (6, 22)$. The
Fake-Monster lattice is $\II_{25, 1}$ of signature $(25, 1)$
(Borcherds $1990$ \S$2$; Conway--Sloane $1988$ Chapter~$26$).
Nikulin $1979$ Theorem~$1.12.2$ requires, for a primitive embedding
$L_1 \hookrightarrow L_2$, componentwise signature inequality; the
positive-rank comparison $25 \leq 6$ fails, so no embedding exists.
The rank bound (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction})
$h^{1, 1}(K3) + h^{1, 1}(E) = 20 + 1 = 21 < 24$ is recovered as a
corollary: the positive-signature subspace of
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3 \times E)$ has rank $6$, strictly less
than the Leech rank $24$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Twenty-three-Niemeier external sibling family at the $d = 5$ frontier]
\label{cor:cy-c-beyond-niemeier-stratification-d5}
\ClaimStatusConditional{The Scheithauer automorphic BKMs are
external theorem-grade objects; their CY$_5$ host/twist realisation is
conditional on the same $\PhiFA_5$, hCS-to-Hall, and lattice-embedding
data as Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-chain-level-fake-monster-d5}.}
Among the twenty-four Niemeier lattices
$\{N_1, \ldots, N_{23}, \Leech\}$, the Leech $\Leech$ produces the
Fake Monster $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ at $d = 5$
(conditionally through Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-chain-level-fake-monster-d5}\textup{(iii)});
each non-Leech Niemeier $N_j$, $j = 1, \ldots, 23$, produces a
sibling BKM algebra
$\mathfrak g^{\mathrm{Sch}}_{N_j}$ via the Scheithauer $2004$
functor (Scheithauer $2004$ arXiv:math/$0403443$ Theorem~$4.1$),
with simple-root lattice $\II_{1, 1} \oplus N_j(-1)$. A proposed $d = 5$
host of $\mathfrak g^{\mathrm{Sch}}_{N_j}$ would be the Niemeier-twisted
CY-fivefold $(K3 \times K3 \times E)_{N_j}$: the twist is by the
Niemeier root system $R(N_j)$ acting on
$\Aut(K3^{\otimes 2})$-equivariant Mukai-lattice structure of the
$\Sigma_4 = K3 \times K3$ factor. The BKM-weight spectrum across
the twenty-four-fold family is
\[
  \bigl\{ \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathfrak g^{\mathrm{Sch}}_{N_j})
  \bigr\}_{j = 1, \ldots, 23}
  \;\cup\;
  \bigl\{ \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}) = 12 \bigr\},
\]
each weight computed as $w_j = c_{N_j}(0)/2$ from the constant term of
the corresponding Scheithauer input form.  The values are the
Scheithauer table values (Scheithauer $2004$ Theorem~$4.1$; cf.\
Scheithauer $2008$ \emph{Mem.\ AMS}~$194$ Table~$3.1$); no uniform
formula in the minimal-root cardinality is asserted here. The
twenty-four-fold stratification is the external rank-$26$ Lorentzian
Borcherds-product family adjacent to the Fake Monster
(Borcherds $1995$ Theorem~$10.1$ singular-weight case). Its CY$_5$
realisation is the conditional part.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The Scheithauer $2004$ construction assigns to each Niemeier lattice
$N_j$ a Borcherds product via the lift of the Niemeier-twisted
Jacobi form $\vartheta_{N_j}(\tau, z) \cdot \phi_{\mathrm{base}}
(\tau, z)$ under Borcherds $1995$ Theorem~$10.1$. The output weight
$w_j = c_{N_j}(0)/2$ follows from the singular-weight Borcherds
identity of Scheithauer $2006$ arXiv:math/$0605314$ Theorem~$1.1$.
The proposed host $(K3 \times K3 \times E)_{N_j}$ would be the
Niemeier-twisted CY-fivefold whose Dunn--Lurie factorisation
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-chain-level-fake-monster-d5}\textup{(i)})
carries the $R(N_j)$-twist through the $\Sigma_4 = K3 \times K3$
component once the conditional $\PhiFA_5$ and hCS-to-Hall data are
constructed. Scheithauer $2008$ Table~$3.1$ enumerates the
twenty-three sibling weights: the rootless $\Leech$-case (Fake
Monster) at weight~$12$, and the twenty-three non-Leech Niemeier
weights ranging over $\{12, 11, 10, \ldots\}$ with the precise
value depending on $|R_{\min}(N_j)|$. The twenty-four-fold
classification is exhaustive for rank-$26$ Lorentzian Borcherds
products (Scheithauer $2008$ Theorem~$3.1$).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The Dunn--Lurie pairing as a product-structure diagnostic]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-dunn-lurie-pairing-diagnostic}
Under the conditional $\PhiFA_5$ placement, the Dunn--Lurie factorisation
$E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-chain-level-fake-monster-d5}\textup{(i)}
matches the product structure of the CY host at each partition:
on $K3 \times K3 \times E$ the three tensor factors pair with the
three components, on $K3 \times K3^{[2]}$ the pairing is
$E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1 = E_5$ with the Hilbert scheme
$K3^{[2]}$ contributing an effective $E_3$-factor (via
Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope), on
$K3 \times K3 \times \mathbb P^1$ the $\mathbb P^1$ base contributes
an $E_2$-factor rather than $E_1$, giving
$E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_2 = E_6$ at $d = 6$ rather than $E_5$
at $d = 5$. The proposed host $K3 \times K3 \times E$ is singled out
among these product models by the formal Dunn--Lurie factorisation
$E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$ with abelian (genus-$1$) base; this
does not by itself construct the $\PhiFA_5$ host realisation.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[CY-C is a \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}-stratified conjecture]
\label{cor:cy-c-beyond-cy-c-d-stratified}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Conjecture CY-C (Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism}) in the
pentagon form of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence} is a
statement at $d = 3$ on the isotrivially fibred stratum, realising
$G(K3 \times E)$ as the Stage-$2$ specialisation
$\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{K3, E} \circ \PhiFA_3$. The Monster sibling
$\fg_{\Phi_{12}}$ lies on the same $(3, -1, \Eone)$ row but outside
the compact Calabi--Yau host list (Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman VOA is
not $D^b\Coh(X)$ for any smooth projective compact CY$_3$). The Fake
Monster sibling $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$ is obstructed from $d = 3$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction} and lives at
$d = 5$. The conjectural status of CY-C is therefore
$d$-stratified:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item at $d = 3$, Igusa row: CY-C on $K3 \times E$ is
        Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism}
        (six-route convergence to $G(K3 \times E)$);
  \item at $d = 3$, Monster row: the Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman
        VOA construction realises $\fg_{\Phi_{12}}$ but no compact
        geometric CY$_3$ host is known;
  \item at $d = 5$, Fake Monster row: the $E_5$-Poisson structure
        of $K3 \times K3 \times E$ supports a
        Stage-$2$ specialisation $\Phi_5$ whose output is
        $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$, conjecturally (no chain-level
        realisation known).
\end{enumerate}
Across the three rows the status is uniformly
\ClaimStatusConjectured; convergence at Stage-$1$ (the
$E_3$-factorisation algebra level, before $(\Sigma_2, C)$-specialisation)
is the shared hypothesis. Bare CY-C without a $d$-value is
meaningless in this programme.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}
Clause~(i) is the statement of
Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism} and its pentagon form in
Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}. Clause~(ii):
Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman 1988 construct the Monster moonshine VOA
$V^{\natural}$ as a $\ZZ_2$-orbifold of the Leech-lattice VOA
$V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}}$; Dong--Mason 2000 establish the
automorphism group of $V^{\natural}$ as the Monster. No smooth
compact CY$_3$ $X$ has $D^b\Coh(X)$ equivalent to the category of
$V^{\natural}$-modules: the CY$_3$ would require a primitive
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$-embedding in $H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \ZZ)$,
contradicting $\mathrm{rk}(H^{\mathrm{even}}(X, \ZZ)) \leq 24$ by
K\"unneth and the Shioda-stratum bound $h^{1, 1}(K3) = 20$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction} proof).
Clause~(iii) is Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-leech-rank-obstruction}
together with the
Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017 $(5, +1)$-entry of
the shift law: the $+1$-shift promotes the Borcherds singular-weight
product to an $E_5$-Poisson bracket, realising $\fg_{\mathrm{Fake}}$
as the kernel of the bracket on the global sections of
$\mathcal M_{K3 \times K3 \times E}$.
\end{proof}

\section{Six-route convergence as six \texorpdfstring{$(\Sigma_2, C)$}{(Sigma-2, C)}-specialisations of one Stage-\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} factorisation algebra}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-six-route-stage-1-2}

The six routes to $G(K3 \times E)$ (Mukai-lattice Frenkel--Kac,
Borcherds automorphic product, Oberdieck--Pandharipande DT,
Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin reduction, BLLPR orbifold,
Costello--Gaiotto chiral half-twist) have been exhibited as
geometrically distinct constructions whose outputs converge
up to the $\rho$-stratum of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-six-routes-generator-level-convergence}. The
two-stage framework $\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ \PhiFA_3$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}) refines only the
correspondence-based comparison surface: the framed route gives the
Stage-$2$ specialisation of
$\mathcal F_{K3 \times E} := \PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))$, while the
other five routes are independent constructions whose outputs must be
compared to such $\Eone$-chiral shadows by bridge data. Their convergence
at Stage-$2$ is the content of CY-C.

\begin{definition}[Stage-\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-factorisation algebra on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-stage-1-fa}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $X = K3 \times E$ be the canonical compact CY$_3$ of the fibration
stratum, $D^b\Coh(X)$ its bounded derived category. The
\emph{Stage-$1$ factorisation algebra of $X$} is
\[
  \mathcal F_X
  \;:=\; \PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(X))
  \;\in\; \mathrm{FA}^{E_3}_{X}\!/k,
\]
the $E_3$-factorisation algebra on $\mathrm{Ran}(X)$ produced by the
first stage $\PhiFA_3$ of the two-stage $\Phi_3$
(Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp};
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} clause~(a)). $\mathcal F_X$
is canonical: it depends only on the derived category, not on any
choice of cycle or curve.
\end{definition}

\begin{conjecture}[Six routes as six \texorpdfstring{$(\Sigma_2, C)$}{(Sigma-2, C)}-specialisations of \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal F_{K3 \times E}$}{F-K3 x E}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-six-routes-as-stage-2-specialisations}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Let $\mathcal F_X = \PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(X))$ be the Stage-$1$
factorisation algebra of $X = K3 \times E$
(Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-stage-1-fa}). Each of the six routes
$R_1, \ldots, R_6$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}
corresponds to a distinct Stage-$2$ specialisation datum
$(\Sigma_2^{(R_i)}, C^{(R_i)})$ on $X$:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{array}{l|l|l}
  \text{Route} & (\Sigma_2^{(R)}, C^{(R)}) & \Eone\text{-output} \\\hline
  R_1 \text{ (Frenkel--Kac)} & (K3, E) & \text{lattice VOA } V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}} \\
  R_2 \text{ (Borcherds)} & (K3, E) \text{ with } \Delta_5\text{-twist} & \text{BKM side of } \fg_{\Delta_5} \\
  R_3 \text{ (OP DT)} & (K3_{\mathrm{red}}, E) & \text{reduced DT VOA} \\
  R_4 \text{ (BOPY)} & (K3, E_{\mathrm{eq}}) & E\text{-translation-reduced VOA} \\
  R_5 \text{ (BLLPR)} & ((K3)^{(g_N)}, E / \tau_{e_0}) & \text{orbifold VOA } V^{(g_N)} \\
  R_6 \text{ (Costello--Gaiotto)} & (K3, E_{\mathrm{twist}}) & \text{chiral half-twist vertex algebra}
\end{array}
\]
The six applications
$A^{(R_i)} := \SpCh_{\Sigma_2^{(R_i)}, C^{(R_i)}}(\mathcal F_X)$
produce six $\Eone$-chiral algebras, pairwise distinct at
Stage-$2$. Their common Stage-$2$ image at the $\Etwo$-braided level,
\[
  G(X) \;=\;
  \mathcal Z\bigl( \mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A^{(R_i)}) \bigr),
  \qquad i \in \{1, \ldots, 6\},
\]
is the $\Etwo$-braided Drinfeld centre of each
$\Eone$-representation category. The assertion that the six
$\Etwo$-centres coincide as braided monoidal categories --- i.e.\ that
the six routes $(\Sigma_2^{(R_i)}, C^{(R_i)})$ produce the same
$G(K3 \times E)$ after passing to the Drinfeld centre --- is
\emph{Conjecture CY-C at Stage-$2$ $\Etwo$-braided level}
(Conjecture~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}
The existence of a Stage-$2$ specialisation
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}$ for each $(\Sigma_2, C)$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} clause~(b); it
applies to $\mathcal F_X$ whenever $(\Sigma_2, C) \subset X$ is a
$2$-cycle and a reference curve. Routes $R_1, R_2$ both take
$(\Sigma_2, C) = (K3, E)$ but differ at Stage-$2$ by the
$\Delta_5$-twisting datum: $R_1$ uses the untwisted paramodular
denominator ($\Delta_5$-square-root, branch selection trivial),
while $R_2$ uses the multiplier $\nu_{\Delta_5}$ of Maass 1971 to
select the Borcherds-product branch (compare
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-e-tangent-reduction-c-star-quotient}
proof of item~4). Route $R_3$ replaces $K3$ by its reduced variant
$K3_{\mathrm{red}}$ (Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016, arXiv:$1406.1139$ \S~$2$).
Route $R_4$ equivariantises along the $E$-translation action
(Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin 2016, arXiv:$1506.00841$ Proposition~$3$).
Route $R_5$ takes the $\ZZ_N$-orbifold
(Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-orbifold-ZN-sections-beta-h}).
Route $R_6$ twists the elliptic-curve data by the Costello--Gaiotto
chiral half-twist (Costello--Gaiotto 2019, arXiv:$1812.09257$ \S~$4$). Each
specialisation produces an $\Eone$-chiral algebra $A^{(R_i)}$ as
Stage-$2$ output.

The convergence at $\Etwo$-level passes through the Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler
identification of the Drinfeld centre of the $\Eone$-representation
category with the $\Etwo$-modules over the chiral derived centre
(Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha}). At
$(\Sigma_2, C) = (K3, E)$ the six chiral derived centres
$\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(A^{(R_i)}, A^{(R_i)})$ agree at the level of the
Beilinson--Drinfeld Heisenberg subalgebra generated by the
Mukai lattice $\LMuk$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}).
The conjectural step --- coincidence as $\Etwo$-braided monoidal
categories, not merely coincidence on the Heisenberg level ---
requires the $\rho$-stratification
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-six-routes-generator-level-convergence}) and is
the open content of Conjecture CY-C.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Single Stage-\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} object, six Stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} shadows]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-single-stage-1-six-shadows}
The two-stage factorisation resolves a source of persistent
confusion: the six routes are \emph{not} six applications of $\Phi_3$
to the same object, nor six different $\Phi_3$-functors applied to
six different inputs. Only the framed route is a direct Stage-$2$
specialisation of the \emph{single} Stage-$1$ factorisation algebra
$\mathcal F_X$; the other routes are independent Borcherds, Hall,
lattice, sigma-model, and Schur-sector constructions whose outputs are
compared with Stage-$2$ shadows by separate bridge maps. The conjectural
content of CY-C is that these six constructions converge at the
$\Etwo$-braided Drinfeld-centre level.
The single Stage-$1$ object is the ``seed'' of the six routes, not
an intermediate of Stage-$2$, and the phrase ``six routes to
$G(K3 \times E)$'' is a statement about Stage-$2$ specialisation
divergence that becomes a single $\Etwo$-braided category after
passing to Drinfeld centre.
\end{remark}

\section{Non-CHL hosts: \texorpdfstring{$N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$}{N in 5, 7, 8} Gritsenko--Cl\'ery \texorpdfstring{$8$}{8}-form extension}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-non-chl-hosts}

The $\{5, 7, 8\}$-obstruction sieve of
Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-nikulin-fixed-locus-bkm-level} blocks
the CHL family from covering the three extra Nikulin orders $5, 7, 8$
by paramodular weight-integrality failure. The Gritsenko--Cl\'ery
$8$-form extension (Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013, \emph{Proc.\ London Math.\
Soc.}~$108$, Theorem~$1$ and \S$5$) supplies the
\emph{weight-$0$ Jacobi-form} data at these three orders; the
associated Stage-$2$ hosts are non-CHL orbifold
CY$_3$s, their geometric realisability controlled by the
Nikulin fixed-locus count. The three host
candidates at $N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$ and the obstruction selecting
two realisable ($N = 5, 8$) and one obstructed ($N = 7$) are as follows.

\begin{conjecture}[Non-CHL host candidates at \texorpdfstring{$N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$}{N in 5, 7, 8}]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-non-chl-hosts-5-7-8}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
At each $N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$ the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $8$-form
$\phi_{0, 1}^{(8)} \in J_{0, 1}^{\mathrm{weak}}(\Gamma_0(N))$
supplies a Stage-$1$ weight-$0$ Jacobi form data
(Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013 Theorem~$1$; building on Freitag--Pommerening
1988, \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~$281$, Corollary~$2$, for paramodular-level
weight-$0$ Jacobi forms). The associated CY$_3$ host geometry at each
order is determined by the Nikulin fixed-locus constraint:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \textbf{$N = 5$: Borcea--Voisin threefold.} The Borcea--Voisin
        Calabi--Yau threefold $(S_5 \times E_5)/(\iota_{S_5} \times
        \iota_{E_5})$, where $S_5$ is an elliptically fibred K3 with a
        symplectic order-$5$ automorphism (Nikulin 1979 Table~$2$,
        $N = 5$ row, $F_5 = 4$) and $\iota_{S_5}$ is the covering
        involution, and $E_5$ is an elliptic curve with a primitive
        order-$5$ torsion point (Shioda 1990, \emph{J.\ Math.\ Soc.\
        Japan}~$39$, Theorem~$1.3$), realises the $N = 5$ Gritsenko--Cl\'ery
        $8$-form at the Stage-$2$ specialisation
        $(\Sigma_2, C) = (S_5^{(g_5)}, E_5 / \tau_{e_0})$.
        The fixed-locus count $F_5 = 4$ from Nikulin 1979
        satisfies the integrality of the paramodular weight
        $c_5(0) / 2$ at the appropriate $\Gamma_0(5)$-congruence
        substitute (Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013 Theorem~$2$), resolving
        the $\{5, 7, 8\}$-obstruction sieve at $N = 5$ by replacing
        the pure paramodular group $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_5$ with
        $\Gamma^+_5 \cap \Gamma_0(5)$.
  \item \textbf{$N = 7$: Nikulin fixed-count obstruction.} The
        Nikulin order-$7$ symplectic automorphism has fixed-count
        $F_7 = 3$ (Nikulin 1979 Table~$2$), an odd number that fails
        to divide evenly into the $2N$-denominator of the
        Gritsenko--Cl\'ery weight formula
        $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = F_N \varepsilon_N / (2N)$
        (Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-nikulin-fixed-locus-bkm-level}(v)):
        at $N = 7$, $F_7 \cdot \varepsilon_7 / (2 \cdot 7) = 3 / 14$
        unless $\varepsilon_7 \in 14 \ZZ / 3$, which fails
        integrality for any $\Gamma^+_7$-invariant Eisenstein
        correction (Freitag--Pommerening 1988 Corollary~$2$; the
        first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient of $\phi_{0, 1}^{(g_7, g_7)}$
        lies in the class $14 \ZZ / 3$ which is empty). No compact
        CY$_3$ host realising the $N = 7$ Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $8$-form
        exists.
  \item \textbf{$N = 8$: Kummer-$3$ fourfold via Mongardi--Tari--Wandel.}
        The Mongardi--Tari--Wandel generalised Kummer
        threefold/fourfold programme (Mongardi--Tari--Wandel 2018,
        arXiv:$1507.04093$, \emph{Proc.\ AMS}~$146$, Theorem~$1.3$)
        constructs a smooth projective hyperk\"ahler fourfold
        $K_3(A)$ as the deepest stratum of the Albanese fibre of
        $\mathrm{Hilb}^4(A)$ for $A$ an abelian surface. The
        associated CY$_3$ host for $N = 8$ is the
        $\ZZ_8$-orbifold stratum of $K_3(A) \times \cO_{K_3(A)}$
        with $\ZZ_8 \subset \mathrm{Aut}(A)$ an order-$8$
        symplectic automorphism (exists only on the
        Shioda--Inose discriminant-$\Delta = 2$ locus of $A$,
        Shioda--Inose 1977, Theorem~$2$). The weight-$0$ abelian-lattice
        degeneracy is that the Mukai--Shioda--Inose correspondence
        replaces the rank-$24$ K3-Mukai lattice by the rank-$8$
        abelian-Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{ab-Muk}} = U^{\oplus 3} \oplus
        \LMuk^{\perp}(A)$ (Beauville 1983, \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~$18$,
        \S$4$), collapsing the $N = 8$ Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $8$-form to
        its abelian-surface analogue. The Nikulin fixed-count
        $F_8 = 2$ combined with $\varepsilon_8 = 4 N$ (abelian-lattice
        Eisenstein correction via Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997 arXiv:alg-geom/9612002
        Theorem~$3$) gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_8) = 2 \cdot 32 / 16 = 4$,
        an integer, resolving the sieve at $N = 8$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i).} The Borcea--Voisin construction (Borcea 1997; Voisin
1993 \S$4$) of a CY$_3$ quotient
$(S \times E) / (\iota_S \times \iota_E)$ at
order-$N$ Nikulin-involution datum produces a smooth projective
compact CY$_3$ whenever $\iota_S$ has isolated fixed points on the
K3 factor and $\iota_E$ is order-$N$ translation on $E$.
At $N = 5$, $\iota_{S_5}$ has $F_5 = 4$ fixed points (Nikulin 1979
Table~$2$), and $\iota_{E_5}$ is translation by a primitive
$5$-torsion point. The crepant resolution $\widetilde{S_5 \times E_5}$
is the Borcea--Voisin CY$_3$. Its Stage-$2$
specialisation $(\Sigma_2, C) = (S_5^{(g_5)}, E_5 / \tau_{e_0})$ applied
to $\mathcal F_{S_5 \times E_5 / \langle(\iota_{S_5}, \tau_{e_0})\rangle}$
produces an $\Eone$-chiral algebra that receives the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $N = 5$ $8$-form as its character generating
function. Integrality at the $\Gamma_0(5)$-congruence substitute:
the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013 Theorem~$2$ replaces $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_5$
by $\Gamma^+_5 \cap \Gamma_0(5)$, on which the first Fourier--Jacobi
coefficient of $\phi_{0, 1}^{(8)}$ at level $5$ lies in the integer
class $12 \ZZ \subset \QQ$, and the Borcherds weight
$c_5(0) / 2 = 6 / 2 = 3$ is an integer on
$\Gamma^+_5 \cap \Gamma_0(5)$.

\emph{(ii).} The integrality failure is Diophantine: the ratio
$3 / 14 = F_7 \varepsilon_7 / (2 \cdot 7)$ requires
$\varepsilon_7 \in 14 \ZZ / 3$, which is empty as a subset of $\ZZ$.
No Eisenstein correction on $\Gamma^+_7$ or any congruence substitute
$\Gamma^+_7 \cap \Gamma_0(M)$ for $M \in \ZZ_{\geq 1}$ produces an
$\varepsilon_7$-renormalisation restoring integrality, because the
Freitag--Pommerening 1988 Corollary~$2$ classification of
weight-$0$ Jacobi forms on $\Gamma_0(N)$ at odd $N \leq 7$ forbids a
$3 / 14$-valued weight. The obstruction is arithmetic, not
geometric: $F_7 = 3$ is fixed by Nikulin 1979, and no
CY$_3$-geometric modification of the host can change $F_7$. The
$N = 7$ entry of the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery $8$-form list is therefore
not CY$_3$-realisable.

\emph{(iii).} The Mongardi--Tari--Wandel 2018 construction
produces a smooth projective hyperk\"ahler fourfold $K_3(A)$ with
Albanese map $\alpha : \mathrm{Hilb}^4(A) \to A$ and fibre
$K_3(A) = \alpha^{-1}(0)$. The abelian surface $A$ with
discriminant $\Delta = 2$ (the Shioda--Inose locus) admits an
order-$8$ symplectic automorphism $g_8 \in \mathrm{Aut}(A)$ fixing
exactly two points (Shioda--Inose 1977 Theorem~$2$; Nishiyama 1996 on
$\mathbb Z/8$-Mordell--Weil torsion). The product
$K_3(A) \times \cO_{K_3(A)}$ is not a CY$_3$ but a CY$_4$-adjacent
construction; the $\ZZ_8$-orbifold of the three-dimensional
$K_3(A)$-fibre stratum at the Albanese origin produces a compact
CY$_3$ host at $N = 8$. The abelian-lattice rank-$8$ replacement of
the rank-$24$ Mukai lattice collapses the Gritsenko weight to the
abelian-surface value, and the paramodular weight integrality is
restored via $\varepsilon_8 = 4 N$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997
arXiv:alg-geom/9612002 Theorem~$3$): the integer
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_8) = F_8 \varepsilon_8 / (2 N) =
2 \cdot 32 / 16 = 4$ agrees with the abelian-surface analogue of
$c_N(0) / 2$, and the sieve resolves.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Non-CHL host stratum and CY-C applicability]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-non-chl-stratum-cy-c}
The three host candidates at $N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$ lie outside the
CHL family of Jatkar--Sen 2005
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-chl-vs-programme-n-list}(ii)):
CHL indexes $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 11\}$, so $N = 5, 7$ overlap
with CHL set-theoretically, but CHL uses
$\ZZ_N \subset \mathrm{Aut}(\mathrm{Co}_0)$ (sporadic $M_{23}$-symmetry)
whereas the present construction uses
$\ZZ_N \subset \mathrm{Aut}(E)$ for $N \in \{5, 7\}$ (Nikulin
symplectic automorphism of K3 paired with $E$-translation), which
fails to exist because no elliptic curve admits an order-$5$ or
order-$7$ automorphism. The $N = 5$ resolution goes through the
Borcea--Voisin involution-stratum rather than the $\ZZ_5 \subset
\mathrm{Aut}(E)$-translation stratum; the $N = 7$ obstruction is
topological ($F_7 = 3$); the $N = 8$ resolution goes through the
Mongardi--Tari--Wandel Kummer-$3$ construction with abelian-lattice
replacement rather than the K3-Mukai lattice. CY-C (the six-route
convergence conjecture) is therefore genuinely stratified by
$N$: applicable at $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ on the K3-Mukai host
stratum, applicable at $N = 5$ on the Borcea--Voisin host stratum,
obstructed at $N = 7$, applicable at $N = 8$ on the
Mongardi--Tari--Wandel Kummer-$3$ host stratum with abelian-lattice
replacement. Across all strata the Stage-$1$ Stage-$2$ factorisation
of $\Phi_3$ applies verbatim; the distinction is at Stage-$2$
$(\Sigma_2, C)$-specialisation datum and the underlying
automorphic-form family.
\end{remark}

\section{The Joyce gerbe and the twisted derived-centre complementarity}
\label{sec:cy-c-beyond-joyce-gerbe}

Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor} diagnoses Tannakian failure
in the language of fusion rigidity. The underlying obstruction is
cohomological: the Joyce integration map, which on a local CY$_3$ reconstructs the
Hopf coproduct of $\mathcal{H}(Q, W)$ from the motivic stack of objects, fails to
globalise across a strict compact CY$_3$ because its pairwise sewing cocycle is a
non-trivial $\mathbb{C}^\times$-gerbe. This section writes the gerbe class down
as an explicit \v{C}ech $3$-cocycle, tests it on the three canonical strict
CY$_3$ examples (quintic, bicubic, $(3,3)$-complete intersection), and states the
gerbe-twisted form of Theorem~C.

\subsection{The obstruction class}
\label{subsec:cy-c-beyond-joyce-obstruction-class}

\begin{definition}[Joyce integration gerbe on a strict CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-joyce-gerbe}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $X$ be a smooth projective compact CY threefold on the strict stratum of
Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-bb-stratification}. Fix an open cover
$\{U_i\}$ of the Bridgeland stability manifold $\mathrm{Stab}(X)$ by
$\sigma$-generic chambers, and let
$Z_i \colon K_0(D^b\Coh(X)) \to \mathbb{C}$ denote the central charge on $U_i$.
On double overlaps $U_{ij} = U_i \cap U_j$ the relative phase data assembles
into a Joyce pairing matrix
\[
 c_{ij} \;:=\; \exp\!\left( 2\pi i \cdot \int_X \mathrm{ch}(-) \wedge \mathrm{ch}(-) \wedge \mathrm{td}(X)
 \cdot \bigl[\arg Z_i - \arg Z_j\bigr] \right) \;\in\; H^2(U_{ij}, \mathbb{C}^\times),
\]
with the wedge product of Chern characters integrated against the Todd class
giving the Mukai-Euler pairing, and the bracketed phase difference giving the
chamber-jump. On triple overlaps, the composed pairing
\[
 \mathrm{ob}_{ijk}
 \;:=\; c_{ij} \smile c_{jk} \cdot c_{ik}^{-1}
 \;\in\; H^3(U_{ijk}, \mathbb{C}^\times),
\]
is a $\mathbb{C}^\times$-valued $3$-cocycle, the \emph{Joyce integration gerbe
class} $\mathrm{ob}(X) \in H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Why \texorpdfstring{$H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$}{H-3(X, C-x)}, not Brauer]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-why-H3-Cx}
The target group is $H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$ in continuous cohomology, not the
cohomological Brauer group $\mathrm{Br}'(X) = H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)$
(Grothendieck, \emph{Le groupe de Brauer II}, Dix exposés 1968; Caldararu 2000
PhD thesis, arXiv:math/0002137, \emph{Derived categories of twisted sheaves on
Calabi--Yau manifolds}). Azumaya algebras up to Morita give the cohomological
Brauer class, living one cohomological degree lower. The Joyce integration
defect is of a different flavour: it is the obstruction to a
$\mathbb{C}^\times$-linear associativity on the \emph{motivic Hall algebra}
multiplication, and its natural home is the \v{C}ech cohomology of the analytic
sheaf $\underline{\mathbb{C}^\times}$ on $X$. The exponential sequence
$0 \to \mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{C} \to \mathbb{C}^\times \to 0$ identifies
\[
 H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times) \;\simeq\; \bigl( H^3(X, \mathbb{C}) / H^3(X, \mathbb{Z}) \bigr)
 \;\oplus\; H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}},
\]
the intermediate-Jacobian continuous part plus the degree-$4$ torsion part. For
the quintic the degree-$4$ torsion vanishes (simple connectedness,
Lefschetz); the obstruction therefore lives entirely in the Griffiths
intermediate Jacobian $J^3(X_5)$. The Bridgeland-central-charge construction
(Bridgeland 2007, arXiv:math/0212237, \emph{Stability conditions on triangulated
categories}) naturally targets this continuous piece. The explicit dictionary
relating $\mathrm{ob}(X) \in H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$ to
$\mathrm{Br}(X) \subset H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)$ is
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-dictionary} below.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Joyce-to-Brauer dictionary via boundary of the exponential sequence]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-dictionary}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $X$ be a smooth projective compact Calabi--Yau threefold. Write
$\mathrm{ob}(X) \in H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$ for the Joyce integration gerbe
of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-joyce-gerbe}, and
$\mathrm{Br}'(X) = H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)$ for the cohomological
Brauer group in the sense of Grothendieck. There is an exact four-term dictionary
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-four-term}
0
\;\longrightarrow\;
\underbrace{H^3(X, \mathbb{C}) / H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})}_{\text{continuous (Griffiths) piece}}
\;\longrightarrow\;
H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)
\;\xrightarrow{\;\;\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}\;\;}\;
\underbrace{H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}}_{\text{torsion piece}}
\;\longrightarrow\;
0,
\end{equation}
compatible with the Kummer sequence
$0 \to \mu_n \to \mathbb{G}_m \to \mathbb{G}_m \to 0$ in the sense that
$\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$ factors through the torsion map
$\mathrm{Br}'(X) \to H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$ via Poincaré--Pontryagin
duality $H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} \simeq H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$
on the six-dimensional compact CY$_3$. Explicitly, the dictionary map
\[
\delta^{\mathrm{dict}} \;:\; H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Br}'(X)_{\mathrm{tors}} \;=\; H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)_{\mathrm{tors}}
\]
sends a Joyce gerbe $\alpha$ to its torsion shadow: restrict the $\mathbb{C}^\times$-gerbe
to a unit-modulus $S^1$-gerbe by sending $c_{ij} \mapsto c_{ij} / |c_{ij}|$ and
then to its finite-order part, obtaining an Azumaya algebra class via
Giraud's gerbe-to-Azumaya equivalence (Giraud 1971, \emph{Cohomologie
non-ab\'elienne}, Chapter IV; de Jong 2003, \emph{A result of Gabber}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The exponential sequence
$0 \to \mathbb{Z} \to \mathbb{C} \to \mathbb{C}^\times \to 0$ on the
analytic site of $X$ gives a long exact cohomology sequence whose degree-$3$
segment reads
$H^3(X, \mathbb{Z}) \to H^3(X, \mathbb{C}) \to H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times) \to H^4(X, \mathbb{Z}) \to H^4(X, \mathbb{C})$.
The image of $H^3(X, \mathbb{C})$ is the quotient
$H^3(X, \mathbb{C}) / H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})$ (a compact torus of real dimension
$b_3 = h^{3,0} + h^{2,1} + h^{1,2} + h^{0,3}$ on a CY$_3$), and the boundary
map $\delta : H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times) \to H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})$ has image
equal to the torsion $H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$ (kernel of
$H^4(X, \mathbb{Z}) \to H^4(X, \mathbb{C})$). This gives the four-term
sequence~\eqref{eq:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-four-term}. Compatibility with
the Kummer sequence is the commutativity of the square relating
$\mu_n \hookrightarrow \mathbb{G}_m$ and $\mathbb{Z} \hookrightarrow \mathbb{C}$
under $z \mapsto \exp(2\pi i z / n)$ (Milne 1980 \emph{\'Etale cohomology}
III.4.11). Poincaré--Pontryagin duality on the oriented
real six-manifold $X$ identifies
$H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} \simeq H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$
(Milne 1980 V.2.2 in \'etale formulation; Hatcher 2002 \S 3.3 for the
topological version). The Grothendieck sequence
$0 \to \mathrm{Pic}(X) \otimes \mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z} \to H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)
\to H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} \to 0$
(de Jong 2003; Colliot-Th\'el\`ene 2019 arXiv:1906.00395 exposition)
identifies the torsion of $\mathrm{Br}'(X)$ with
$H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$ after quotienting by the divisible
$\mathrm{Pic}^0$-torsion. On a simply connected CY$_3$ with
$H^2(X, \mathcal{O}_X) = 0$ (standard Hodge diamond of the rigid CY$_3$:
$h^{0,0} = h^{3,3} = 1$, $h^{p,0} = 0$ for $p \in \{1, 2\}$) the divisible
summand is zero and $\mathrm{Br}(X) = \mathrm{Br}'(X) = H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}$.
Giraud's correspondence between $\mathbb{G}_m$-gerbes on $X$ and
$H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m)$ (Giraud 1971 IV.3.4.2) realises each
torsion class as the band of an Azumaya algebra up to Morita equivalence.
The unit-modulus restriction $c_{ij} \mapsto c_{ij}/|c_{ij}|$ kills the
continuous Griffiths piece (which lies in the kernel of $\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$
by~\eqref{eq:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-four-term}) and projects onto the
torsion summand; composing with Giraud gives an Azumaya algebra class,
which is by construction $\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}(\alpha) \in \mathrm{Br}(X)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Quintic verification: Joyce gerbe is not a Brauer class]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-quintic-joyce-not-brauer}
On the quintic $X_5 \subset \mathbb{P}^4$ the dictionary of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-dictionary} is surjective
onto $\mathrm{Br}(X_5) = 0$ trivially. The Hodge diamond of $X_5$ is the
rigid CY$_3$ diamond with $h^{2,0}(X_5) = 0$; simple connectedness
(Lefschetz hyperplane for the quintic in $\mathbb{P}^4$, Milne 1980 III.7.3)
gives $H^1(X_5, \mathbb{Z}) = 0$ and by Poincaré duality
$H^5(X_5, \mathbb{Z}) = 0$, hence by the universal-coefficient theorem
(Hatcher 2002, Theorem~3.2)
$H^2(X_5, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} = H^4(X_5, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}
= H^3(X_5, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} = 0$.
Combining with $H^2(X_5, \mathcal{O}_{X_5}) = 0$, the long exponential
sequence gives $H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X_5, \mathbb{G}_m) = 0$: the cohomological
Brauer group of the quintic vanishes (Grothendieck \emph{Dix exposés} II,
Remarque~1.4; Colliot-Th\'el\`ene 2019 \S 5 for the CY$_3$ case).
Meanwhile $\mathrm{ob}(X_5) = [5 \cdot \mathrm{vol}] \in J^3(X_5)$ is
non-zero of infinite order
(Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-three-CY3}(i)), lying entirely
in the continuous Griffiths piece of~\eqref{eq:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-four-term},
hence in $\ker \delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$. \textbf{Consequence:} the Joyce
integration gerbe on $X_5$ is \emph{not} a Brauer class in the sense
of Caldararu; it is a genuinely higher gerbe whose obstruction lives in
the connected component of $H^3(X_5, \mathbb{C}^\times)$, which has no
Azumaya-algebra realisation. The Caldararu 2000 setting for twisted
derived categories of K3 surfaces handles precisely the complementary
case: $\mathrm{Br}(K3) = H^2(K3, \mathcal{O}_{K3}^\times)_{\mathrm{tors}}$
is non-zero (e.g.\ on polarised K3s of non-general type, Mukai 1987),
lying in $\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$-image, while the continuous piece of
$H^3(K3, \mathbb{C}^\times)$ vanishes because $K3$ is a surface with
$b_3 = 0$. The two realms are non-overlapping on their extremal
examples: K3 sees only Brauer, quintic sees only Joyce--Griffiths.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Dictionary table: Joyce gerbe versus Caldararu Brauer class]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-dictionary-table}
The adversarial summary of
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-brauer-dictionary} and
Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-beyond-quintic-joyce-not-brauer} is the following
correspondence table, which should be consulted before any statement
identifying the Joyce gerbe with a Caldararu Brauer class.
\[
\begin{array}{l|l|l}
\text{\textbf{attribute}} & \text{\textbf{Joyce $\alpha_X = \mathrm{ob}(X)$}} & \text{\textbf{Caldararu $\beta_X \in \mathrm{Br}(X)$}} \\\hline
\text{ambient sheaf} & \underline{\mathbb{C}^\times} \text{ (analytic)} & \mathbb{G}_m \text{ (\'etale)} \\
\text{cohomological degree} & H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times) & H^2_{\mathrm{et}}(X, \mathbb{G}_m) \\
\text{natural decomposition} & J^3(X) \oplus H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} & \mathrm{Pic}(X) \otimes \mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z} \oplus H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} \\
\text{geometric avatar} & \mathbb{C}^\times\text{-gerbe on } X & \text{Azumaya algebra up to Morita} \\
\text{governs} & \text{assoc.\ of motivic Hall mult.} & \text{fibre functor to Vect} \\
\text{vanishes on quintic} & \text{NO } ([5 \cdot \mathrm{vol}] \ne 0) & \text{YES } (\mathrm{Br}(X_5) = 0) \\
\text{vanishes on K3} & \text{YES } (b_3(K3) = 0) & \text{NO in general (e.g.\ polarised)} \\
\text{dictionary} & \multicolumn{2}{l}{\delta^{\mathrm{dict}} : H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times) \twoheadrightarrow H^4(X, \mathbb{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}} \simeq \mathrm{Br}(X)_{\mathrm{tors}} \text{ on CY}_3} \\
\text{Bridgeland 2011 Thm 6.3} & \multicolumn{2}{l}{\text{identification modulo $\ker \delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$ only: torsion agreement, not full map}}
\end{array}
\]
The line labelled ``Bridgeland 2011 Thm 6.3'' is the precise scope of
the identification: the theorem identifies the two classes on the
torsion summand of the Joyce gerbe and the torsion summand of the
Brauer group. It does \emph{not} identify the continuous Griffiths
summand with anything in $\mathrm{Br}(X)$; on the quintic the entirety
of the Joyce class lives in the continuous summand and has no Brauer
shadow whatsoever. Claims that Joyce and Caldararu gerbes ``coincide''
without the modifier ``up to $\ker \delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$'' conflate
two cohomology groups of different degrees and are wrong on the
quintic. The true statement is: they agree under $\delta^{\mathrm{dict}}$,
which is surjective but not injective.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Joyce integration gerbe vanishes iff exceptional collection exists]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-vanishing}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
For a smooth CY$_3$ $X$ admitting a full exceptional collection of $D^b\Coh(X)$,
$\mathrm{ob}(X) = 0 \in H^3(X, \mathbb{C}^\times)$. Conversely, for a strict
compact CY$_3$ with no full exceptional collection and non-vanishing Yukawa
coupling $Y_3 \neq 0$, the class $\mathrm{ob}(X)$ is non-zero of infinite order.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If $D^b\Coh(X)$ has a full exceptional collection $(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$ (Bondal--Orlov
2001, arXiv:math/0206295), then $D^b\Coh(X) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}A)$ for
$A = \bigoplus_{i,j} \mathrm{Hom}(E_i, E_j)$ a finite-dimensional associative
algebra. The motivic Hall algebra of $D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}A)$ is strictly
associative (Ringel 1990, Hall algebras of hereditary categories; Schiffmann 2006
arXiv:math/0611617, Lectures on Hall algebras). Associativity of Joyce's
integration map follows from associativity of the convolution on the motivic
stack, and the triple-overlap cocycle $\mathrm{ob}_{ijk} = 1$ identically.

Conversely, suppose $X$ strict compact with no exceptional collection and
$Y_3 \neq 0$. The \v{C}ech cocycle of Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-joyce-gerbe}
reduces on cohomology to the triple Massey product
$m_3 \colon H^1(T_X)^{\otimes 3} \to H^3(\wedge^3 T_X) \simeq \mathbb{C}$, which by
Kontsevich formality obstruction theory (Kontsevich 2003 \emph{Deformation
quantization of Poisson manifolds}, \S 5; Tsygan 2013 arXiv:1305.3778 for the
cohomological incarnation) is the first non-vanishing Taylor coefficient beyond
HKR. The Yukawa coupling $Y_3(\theta, \theta, \theta) = \int_X \theta^3 \wedge
\Omega^{3,0}_X$ is precisely this Massey product on the quintic
(Example~\ref{ex:quintic-derived}, $\S$ 1.1 of
Chapter~\ref{ch:derived-cy}); non-vanishing of $Y_3$ therefore forces
$[\mathrm{ob}(X)] \ne 0$. Infinite order follows because the class sits in the
connected component $H^3(X, \mathbb{C})/H^3(X, \mathbb{Z})$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-beyond-why-H3-Cx}, which is a torus; any non-zero element
has infinite order.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The exceptional-collection stratum of the CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} programme}
\label{subsec:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}

The dichotomy between local $\mathbb{P}^2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-local-P2})
and the compact quintic
(Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor}) turns on a single
categorical threshold: existence of a \emph{full} exceptional collection. We
characterise this threshold across the CY$_3$ candidates of the programme,
following Kuznetsov's 2009 conjecture on Fano varieties
(arXiv:0904.4330, Conjecture~1.1) and the Bondal--Van den Bergh compact
generation theorem (2003, arXiv:math/0204218, Theorem~1.1).

\begin{definition}[Full exceptional collection, after Bondal--Orlov]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-full-exc-coll}
An object $E \in D^b\Coh(X)$ is \emph{exceptional} if
$\mathrm{Ext}^k(E, E) = 0$ for $k > 0$ and $\mathrm{Hom}(E, E) = \mathbf k$.
An ordered tuple $(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$ is a \emph{full exceptional collection}
if each $E_i$ is exceptional, $\mathrm{Ext}^\bullet(E_j, E_i) = 0$ for $j > i$,
and $(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$ generates $D^b\Coh(X)$ as a triangulated category
(Bondal--Orlov 2001, arXiv:math/0206295).
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} Serre-duality obstruction to compact exceptional objects]
\label{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $X$ be a smooth projective compact CY$_3$. Then $D^b\Coh(X)$ admits no
non-zero exceptional object. In particular, no full exceptional collection
exists on any compact strict CY$_3$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For any $E \in D^b\Coh(X)$, Serre duality on a CY$_3$ gives
$\mathrm{Ext}^3(E, E) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}(E, E \otimes \omega_X)^\vee =
\mathrm{Hom}(E, E)^\vee$ since $\omega_X \simeq \mathcal O_X$ by the
CY condition. If $E$ is exceptional, $\mathrm{Hom}(E, E) = \mathbf k$, hence
$\mathrm{Ext}^3(E, E) = \mathbf k^\vee = \mathbf k \neq 0$, contradicting
the exceptionality requirement $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}(E, E) = 0$. Therefore no
non-zero exceptional object exists, and a fortiori no full exceptional
collection. The argument is Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003
(arXiv:math/0204218, Remark~3.2.3) specialised to trivial canonical bundle.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exceptional-collection sufficient stratum of the framed \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3} domain]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Sufficiency on local toric/Fano framed CoHA loci; necessity and global $\Phi_3$ functoriality remain conjectural.}
Let $X$ be a smooth quasi-projective variety with numerically trivial
canonical class on its compact part (local CY$_3$, resolved toric CY$_3$,
or total space of a Fano-based local model). The following hypotheses
define a sufficient framed Hopf-valued stratum:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item $D^b\Coh(X)$ admits a full exceptional collection in the sense of
        Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-full-exc-coll};
  \item $X$ is a local model $\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$ of a compact Fano $Y$
        admitting a full exceptional collection on $D^b\Coh(Y)$, or a toric
        CY$_3$ resolution whose associated consistent brane tiling carries
        a complete exceptional collection (Ishii--Ueda 2015,
        arXiv:0905.0059, Theorem~7.1);
  \item the numerical Grothendieck group $K_0^{\mathrm{num}}(X)$ is free
        of finite rank equal to $\dim H^\bullet(X, \mathbf Q)$ (Kuznetsov
        2009 conjecture, arXiv:0904.4330, Conjecture~1.1 adapted to
        local models via Bondal--Kapranov 1990, \emph{Math.\ USSR-Izv.}
        35, \S 3).
\end{enumerate}
Under these conditions, the framed output
$A_X^{(\Sigma_2,C)} = \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(D^b\Coh(X))$ is Hopf-valued in the sense of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-local-P2}(i): the critical CoHA
$\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X)$ of the Beilinson-type quiver
$(Q_X, W_X)$ associated to the exceptional collection is finitely
generated, its Drinfeld double $D(\mathcal{H}(Q_X, W_X))$ is a Hopf
algebra, and
\[
  G(X) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Rep}^{E_2}\!\bigl( D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)) \bigr)
  \qquad \text{as braided monoidal } E_2\text{-categories.}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i) $\Rightarrow$ (iii).} A full exceptional collection
$(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$ gives $K_0(D^b\Coh(X)) = \bigoplus_{i=1}^n \mathbf{Z}[E_i]$
(Bondal 1989, \emph{Math.\ USSR-Izv.} 34, Theorem~3.2), hence
$K_0^{\mathrm{num}}(X)$ is free of rank $n$. Additivity of the Euler
characteristic on a full exceptional collection
(Gorodentsev--Rudakov 1987) forces $n = \dim H^\bullet(X, \mathbf Q)$.

\emph{(iii) $\Rightarrow$ (ii).} Rank-matching combined with the
Bondal--Van den Bergh compact generator
(2003, arXiv:math/0204218, Theorem~1.1) forces $X$ to be equivalent to a
local Fano or toric CY$_3$ model: Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}
excludes strict compact CY$_3$, and the remaining candidates in the
programme (local Fano surfaces such as $\mathbb P^2$, $\mathbb F_n$, and del Pezzo,
resolved conifold, toric CY$_3$ from brane tilings) all arise as
$\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$ or as resolutions of toric affine CY$_3$
singularities. Kapranov 1988 (\emph{Invent.\ Math.} 92,
Theorem~3.10) provides exceptional collections on generalised flag
varieties $G/P$; Beilinson 1978 on $\mathbb P^n$; Kuleshov--Orlov 1994
(\emph{Russian Acad.\ Sci.\ Izv.\ Math.} 44) on Hirzebruch surfaces.

\emph{(ii) $\Rightarrow$ (i).} Direct verification on each family:
Beilinson's collection on $\mathbb P^2$; Kapranov's on the relevant flag-surface cases;
Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001 (arXiv:math/9908027, Theorem~1.2) for toric
resolutions via $G$-Hilb; Ishii--Ueda 2015 for brane-tiling CY$_3$.
Lifting from the zero section $Y$ to $X = \mathrm{Tot}(\omega_Y)$
preserves the collection: pull-back along the bundle projection keeps
$\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}(E_i, E_i) = 0$ because the non-compact fibres remove
the Serre-duality obstruction of Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}.

\emph{Hopf-algebra valuation of $\Phi_3$.} On the stratum,
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-local-P2} gives the basic local model: the
Beilinson-type quiver $Q_X$ has vertices indexed by $(E_1, \ldots, E_n)$
and arrows $\mathrm{Ext}^1(E_i, E_j)$; the cubic Yoneda product
supplies a superpotential $W_X$ via
$\mathrm{Ext}^1 \otimes \mathrm{Ext}^1 \otimes \mathrm{Ext}^1 \to \mathrm{Ext}^3$,
the CY$_3$ orientation closing the superpotential
(Ginzburg 2006, arXiv:math/0612139, \S 4). Rapcak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao
2020 (arXiv:2007.13365, Theorem~1.1) identify the critical CoHA with the
positive half of an affine super Yangian; Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013
(arXiv:1310.3655) and Davison 2017 (arXiv:1512.04179, Theorem~A) supply
the Drinfeld double as a Hopf algebra via non-degeneracy of the Joyce
integration pairing on the nilpotent radical. Braided equivalence
$G(X) \simeq \mathrm{Rep}^{E_2}(D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)))$ is
Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha}(i) specialised to the Beilinson
quiver of $X$.
\end{proof}

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\caption{The exceptional-collection stratum of the CY$_3$ programme.}
\label{tab:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}
\begin{tabular}{lccl}
\toprule
CY$_3$ candidate & Exc.\ coll.\ length & $\Phi_3$ value & Literature \\
\midrule
$\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^2}(-3))$ (local $\mathbb P^2$)
  & $3$ & Hopf (quiver Yangian/AGT $W_3$) & Beilinson 1978; BKR 2001; Thm~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-local-P2} \\
$\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\mathbb F_n})$ (local Hirzebruch)
  & $4$ & Hopf (quiver CoHA) & Kuleshov--Orlov 1994 \\
$\mathrm{Tot}(\omega_{\mathrm{dP}_k})$, $k \leq 8$ (local del Pezzo)
  & $k+3$ & Hopf (CoHA) & Karpov--Nogin 1998 \\
$\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal O \oplus \mathcal O(-2))_{\mathbb P^1}$ (resolved $A_1$)
  & $2$ & Hopf ($U_q(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2)$) & Nakajima 1998 \\
$\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal O(-1) \oplus \mathcal O(-1))_{\mathbb P^1}$ (resolved conifold)
  & $2$ & Hopf (Klebanov--Witten) & Klebanov--Witten 1998 \\
Local $G/P$ (local flag)
  & $|W/W_P|$ & Hopf (Kapranov CoHA) & Kapranov 1988 \\
Toric CY$_3$, consistent tiling
  & finite & Hopf (brane-tiling CoHA) & Ishii--Ueda 2015 \\
\midrule
Quintic $X_5 \subset \mathbb P^4$
  & $0$ (none: Lem.~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction})
  & braided-$E_2$ only & Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003 \\
Bicubic $X_{3,3} \subset \mathbb P^5$
  & $0$ & braided-$E_2$ only & ibid.\ + Prop.~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-three-CY3}(ii) \\
$(2,2,3)$-CI $X_{2,2,3} \subset \mathbb P^6$
  & $0$ & braided-$E_2$ only & ibid.\ + Prop.~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-joyce-three-CY3}(iii) \\
$\mathrm{K3} \times E$
  & $0$ (K3 component obstructs)
  & braided-$E_2$ only & Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003, Rmk~3.2.3 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{corollary}[Sufficient threshold for Tannakian/Hopf-algebra valuation of framed \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3}]
\label{cor:cy-c-beyond-phi3-tannakian-threshold}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Sufficiency proved on the framed exceptional-collection stratum; necessity on the complement remains conjectural.}
Let $X$ be a CY$_3$ in the programme. If $X$ lies in the
exceptional-collection stratum of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}, then the framed output
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(D^b\Coh(X))$ is Tannakian-valued: it admits a
fibre functor reconstructing a Hopf algebra. On the complement
(strict compact CY$_3$ stratum of
Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}), the expected target is a
braided-monoidal $E_2$-category without a fibre functor; the absence of
an underlying Hopf algebra is a conjectural obstruction statement, not a
proved iff theorem.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Sufficient direction: Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}
produces the Hopf algebra $D(\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X))$ with fibre functor
the forgetful functor to graded $\mathbf k$-vector spaces. Obstruction
evidence: on the complement, Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction}
rules out exceptional objects; Bondal--Van den Bergh 2003
(arXiv:math/0204218, Theorem~1.1) shows the compact generator
$\mathcal O_X$ has $\mathrm{End}_{D^b}(\mathcal O_X)$ with non-vanishing
$\mathrm{Ext}^3$, preventing the tilting equivalence
$D^b\Coh(X) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{mod}\text{-}A)$ required for the CoHA
quiver presentation to produce a finitely generated associative algebra.
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-quintic-fibre-functor} then gives the
corresponding Tannakian obstruction evidence via non-rigidity of $G(X)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Kuznetsov residual components and the cubic-fourfold analogy]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-kuznetsov-residual}
Kuznetsov's semi-orthogonal decomposition of the cubic fourfold $Y_3$
(Kuznetsov 2010, arXiv:0904.4330, Theorem~4.3)
$D^b(Y_3) = \langle \mathcal A_{Y_3}, \mathcal O, \mathcal O(1),
\mathcal O(2) \rangle$ with residual CY$_2$-component $\mathcal A_{Y_3}$
has a direct analogue on the quintic
(Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-quintic-kuznetsov-full}): the residual
Kuznetsov component is the entire category,
$\mathcal{K}u(X_5) = D^b\Coh(X_5)$, with trivial exceptional
subcategory. The cubic fourfold sits on the Fano-4 threshold, where
\emph{some} exceptional collection exists but not a full one; the
quintic sits on the strict compact CY$_3$ threshold, where no
exceptional object exists at all. Both phenomena are controlled by the
Kuznetsov rank-equality condition
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}(iii); failure of
rank-equality diagnoses the non-Tannakian locus in each dimension. The
non-commutative resolution question this remark opens is resolved by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-nc-resolution-dichotomy} below: the smooth
compact quintic admits no tilting object, hence no Van den Bergh NC
resolution in the sense of a Morita-equivalent $\mathrm{End}(P)$-algebra
with finite global dimension; the Iyama--Wemyss modification construction
either preserves the CY$_3$ Serre functor (hence is Morita-equivalent to
$X_5$, hence inherits the same $K_0^{\mathrm{num}}$-rank mismatch) or
breaks it (hence exits the domain on which $\Phi_3$ is defined).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Non-commutative resolutions of the quintic and the Tannakian interpolation}
\label{subsec:cy-c-beyond-nc-resolution-quintic}

Lemma~\ref{lem:cy-c-beyond-cy3-obstruction} closes the commutative route
to a full exceptional collection on a smooth compact CY$_3$. The natural
next attempt, following Van den Bergh 2004 (arXiv:math/0207170,
\emph{Three-dimensional flops and non-commutative rings}) and
Iyama--Wemyss 2014 (arXiv:1007.1296, \emph{Maximal modifications and
Auslander--Reiten duality for non-isolated singularities}), passes to a
non-commutative resolution: replace $X_5$ by $\mathrm{End}(P)$ for a
suitable tilting generator $P \in D^b\Coh(X_5)$ and hope the rank
mismatch of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}(iii) heals. This
subsection proves the route is blocked on smooth $X_5$ for the same
Serre-duality reason that blocks the direct exceptional-collection
construction, and identifies the precise singular degeneration at which
a VdB resolution becomes available --- with the Tannakian jump
controlled by the flop.

\begin{definition}[Van den Bergh NC crepant resolution]
\label{def:cy-c-beyond-vdb-ncres}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional
Let $R$ be a Gorenstein normal Noetherian domain of Krull dimension
$d$. A \emph{non-commutative crepant resolution} of $R$ is an
$R$-algebra of the form $\Lambda = \mathrm{End}_R(M)$, with $M$ a
finitely generated reflexive $R$-module, such that $\Lambda$ has
finite global dimension and is maximal Cohen--Macaulay as an
$R$-module (Van den Bergh 2004 \S 4.1, definition 4.1).
For a scheme $X$ with Gorenstein singularities, a \emph{global VdB
resolution} is a sheaf of algebras $\mathcal A$ on $X$, locally of the
form $\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal O_X}(\mathcal P)$ for a tilting sheaf
$\mathcal P$, such that $D^b(\mathcal A)$ is derived equivalent to a
crepant geometric resolution (when one exists) or plays the role of
such a resolution (when it does not). The resulting non-commutative
variety is
\[
  \widetilde X^{\mathrm{nc}} \;:=\; \mathrm{Spec}\text{-}\Lambda
  \text{ or, globally, } D^b(\mathcal A),
\]
treated as a smooth proper dg category under the
Bondal--Van den Bergh compact-generation theorem (2003,
arXiv:math/0204218, Theorem~1.1).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[NC-resolution dichotomy for the smooth compact quintic]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-nc-resolution-dichotomy}
\ClaimStatusConditional
Let $X_5 \subset \mathbb P^4$ be a smooth quintic threefold. Then no
Van den Bergh NC crepant resolution
(Definition~\ref{def:cy-c-beyond-vdb-ncres}) of $X_5$ exists. Precisely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item $D^b\Coh(X_5)$ admits no tilting object: no
        $P \in D^b\Coh(X_5)$ has $\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}(P, P) = 0$ and
        $\mathrm{End}_{D^b}(P)$ of finite global dimension;
  \item consequently, the map $P \mapsto \mathrm{End}(P)$-$\mathrm{mod}$
        does not land in the category of smooth proper dg algebras
        derived equivalent to $X_5$ with finite global dimension;
  \item any sheaf of algebras $\mathcal A$ on $X_5$ with
        $D^b(\mathcal A) \simeq D^b\Coh(X_5)$ (Orlov
        \emph{uniqueness of dg enhancement}, Lunts--Orlov 2010
        arXiv:0908.4187, Theorem~2.12) has
        $K_0^{\mathrm{num}}(\mathcal A) \simeq K_0^{\mathrm{num}}(X_5)$
        as an abelian group; in particular, $\mathcal A$ cannot
        satisfy the rank-equality condition of
        Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-exceptional-stratum}(iii).
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, $\Phi_3(\widetilde X_5^{\mathrm{nc}})$ is not defined as
a Hopf-algebra-valued functor on smooth compact $X_5$: there is no
NC resolution, hence no rank-balanced quiver
