2020 \S 5--6) with the Drinfeld double of the critical CoHA
$\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)$ under the
Schiffmann--Vasserot--Rapcak--Soibelman identification, so the
Aganagic--Okounkov character is exactly
$\chi_q(\Phi_3^{\mathrm{cat}}(X))$: the weight-$\gamma$
graded piece is the trace of the double grading
$(q^{L_0}, t^{J_0})$ on the CoHA weight-$\gamma$ summand
$\mathcal H(Q_X, W_X)_\gamma$, summed with K-theory-class
fugacity $Q^\gamma$. The equality of partition-function and
categorical character is then a specialisation of the
Negu\c{t}--RSYZ equivalence, valid on exactly the non-compact
exceptional-collection stratum where both sides are defined.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Five verification paths for the non-compact theorem]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-noncompact-five-paths}
The theorem's four claims each admit independent checks.
\textup{(1)} Local $\mathbb P^2$: the AGT-$W_3$ correspondence
(Wyllard 2009 arXiv:0907.2189; Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013
arXiv:1202.2756) identifies $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{cat}}(
\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb P^2})) \simeq U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}}_3)$
and computes $Z^{\mathrm{ref}} = \chi_q$ directly; the
conifold gives $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}}_{1|1})$
(Gopakumar--Vafa). \textup{(2)} Resolved conifold:
Gopakumar--Vafa 1998 (arXiv:hep-th/9812127) gives
$Z^{\mathrm{ref}}_{\mathrm{conifold}} = \prod_{n \geq 1}
(1 - q^n t^{n-1} Q)^{-1}(1 - q^{n-1} t^n Q)^{-1}$, which is the
character of the Klebanov--Witten Hopf algebra. \textup{(3)}
Large-volume degeneration from the compact quintic: the
Candelas--de la Ossa--Green--Parkes 1991 one-parameter
family of quintics degenerates, at $t \to \infty$, to a
limit whose dominant non-compact CY$_3$ chart is
$\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb P^2})$ (via the conifold transition
of Klemm--Theisen 1993 arXiv:hep-th/9304034), in which
limit the $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = -25/3$ scalar degenerates
to $0$ per claim (iii), and the Joyce gerbe
$[5 \cdot \mathrm{vol}]$ of
the CY-C CI-CY$_3$ strict-stratum gerbe proposition
trivialises per claim (ii). \textup{(4)} Categorical Hochschild computation:
$\mathrm{HH}^\bullet_{\mathrm{cat}}(D^b_c\Coh(\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb P^2})))$
concentrates in degrees $\{0, 1, 2\}$ with
$\mathrm{HH}^0_{\mathrm{cat}} = \mathbf k$ (the CoHA centre),
consistent with Theorem~H in the five-archetype ceiling and with
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0$. \textup{(5)} Stability manifold:
$\dim_{\mathbb C} \mathrm{Stab}(\mathrm{Tot}(K_{\mathbb P^2})) =
\mathrm{rk}\,K_0^{\mathrm{num}} = 3$, strictly larger than the
rank-$1$ stability manifold of compact $\mathbb P^2$ and
strictly smaller than the rank-$4$ stability manifold on the
compact quintic: the non-compact stratum carries a genuine
three-parameter Bridgeland moduli (Bayer--Macri 2011
arXiv:1103.5010), and the Aganagic--Okounkov refined vertex
is stability-invariant across this manifold by the
wall-crossing formula (Mozgovoy--Reineke 2019
arXiv:1512.03851, Theorem~A) --- a property the compact-CY$_3$
scalar $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \chi/24$ fails to share, which
diagnoses why the non-compact stratum is the Hopf-algebra
home of the CY$_3$ programme.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Consistency with the compact strict CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} scalar]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-noncompact-compact-consistency}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-c-beyond-noncompact-aganagic-okounkov}(iii)
gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0$ on the non-compact stratum; the
strict-compact formula of
the CY-C CI-CY$_3$ strict-stratum gerbe proposition gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \chi(X)/24 \neq 0$ on the compact CI
stratum. The two are not contradictory: they are the scalar
trace of $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{cat}}$ on two disjoint strata of the
CY$_3$ moduli, separated by a conifold transition (or by a
large-volume degeneration), and the discontinuity of
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$ across the transition is the genus-one
wall-crossing jump $\chi/24 \mapsto 0$. The Joyce gerbe
$\mathrm{ob}(X)$ experiences the same jump: non-zero of infinite
order on the compact stratum, trivial on the non-compact
stratum; the common mechanism is the presence or absence of
$H^6(X, \mathbb Z)$, with compact CY$_3$ carrying a fundamental
class and non-compact CY$_3$ lacking one. Theorem~H concentration
in $\{0, 1, 2\}$ survives the transition unchanged.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Humbert divisor \texorpdfstring{$H_1 \subset \mathcal{A}_2$}{H-1 subset A-2} and the Shioda--Inose partner of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{K3} \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{prop:cy-c-humbert-shioda-inose-k3e}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $\mathcal{A}_2$ denote the moduli stack of principally polarised
abelian surfaces. For a discriminant $\Delta \in \mathbb Z_{>0}$ with
$\Delta \equiv 0, 1 \pmod 4$, the \emph{Humbert surface}
$H_\Delta \subset \mathcal{A}_2$ is the locus of $(A, \Theta)$ whose
endomorphism ring $\End(A)$ contains a real-quadratic order of
discriminant $\Delta$; at the boundary value $\Delta = 1$ the locus
$H_1 \subset \mathcal{A}_2$ is the decomposable stratum
\[
  H_1 \;=\; \bigl\{\, (A, \Theta) \in \mathcal{A}_2 \;:\;
  (A, \Theta) \simeq (E_1 \times E_2, \Theta_{E_1} \boxplus \Theta_{E_2})
  \,\bigr\} \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Sym}^2(\mathcal{A}_1),
\]
with $\mathcal{A}_1 = \mathbb H/\SL_2(\mathbb Z)$ the modular curve.
The Gritsenko weight-$5$ Siegel paramodular cusp form
$\Delta_5 = \Phi_1$ (level $1$, character the non-trivial quadratic
character of the paramodular group $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_1$) has
\[
  \mathrm{div}_{\mathcal{A}_2}(\Delta_5) \;=\; H_1,
  \qquad
  \mathrm{ord}_{H_1}(\Delta_5) \;=\; 1,
  \qquad
  \mathrm{div}_{\mathcal{A}_2}(\Delta_{10}) \;=\; 2 H_1,
\]
where $\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ is the Igusa weight-$10$ Siegel cusp
form (Igusa 1962, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~84, Theorem~3). The
Shioda--Inose correspondence assigns to a Kummer surface
$\mathrm{Kum}(A)$ (the crepant resolution of $A/\langle -1 \rangle$)
a unique K3 surface $Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(A)$ carrying a Nikulin involution
$\iota_{\mathrm{N}}$ with quotient K3 birational to $\mathrm{Kum}(A)$;
the Shioda--Inose partner of $A = E_1 \times E_2 \in H_1$ is
\[
  Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E_1 \times E_2)
  \;\dashrightarrow\; \mathrm{Kum}(E_1 \times E_2)
\]
and its transcendental lattice satisfies
$T(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E_1 \times E_2)) \simeq T(E_1 \times E_2)(2) = U^{\oplus 2}(2)$,
a rank-$4$ even lattice of discriminant $16$, Hodge-isometric to
$T(\mathrm{Kum}(E_1 \times E_2))$ via the Shioda--Inose Hodge
isomorphism on transcendental cycles (Shioda--Inose 1977, \emph{Complex
Analysis and Algebraic Geometry}, Theorem~2; Morrison 1984,
\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~75, Corollary~6.4; the $T \mapsto T(2)$
rescaling by $2$ records the degree-$2$ quotient
$A \to \mathrm{Kum}(A)$, consistent with
\texttt{chapters/examples/derived\_categories\_cy.tex} eq.~$T_{\mathrm{Kum}} \subset T_A(2)$). Consequently the compact
threefold $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ admits, along the one-parameter
sub-locus where $\mathrm{K3} = Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E \times E')$ for some
auxiliary elliptic curve $E'$, a birational model
$(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E \times E')) \times E$ whose total cohomology
decomposes via the Shioda--Inose involution as
\[
  H^*(Y_{\mathrm{SI}} \times E) \;\simeq\;
  H^*(\mathrm{Kum}(E \times E') \times E)^{\iota_{\mathrm{N}}}
  \;\oplus\;
  H^*(E \times E')(2)^{-\iota_{\mathrm{N}}}
\]
where the second summand records the Kummer lattice correction.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Humbert classification is Humbert 1899 as modernised by van der
Geer 1988 (\emph{Hilbert Modular Surfaces}, \S IX.2): the locus of
$(A, \Theta)$ with $\End(A) \supset \mathcal O_{\Delta} = \mathbb Z[\frac{\Delta + \sqrt\Delta}{2}]$
is a non-empty divisor in $\mathcal A_2$ whenever $\Delta \equiv 0, 1
\pmod 4$. For $\Delta = 1$, $\mathcal O_1 = \mathbb Z[1]$ is the
trivial order, and $\End(A) \supset \mathbb Z$ is automatic; the
Humbert condition collapses to a codimension-$1$ \emph{reducibility}
condition on the polarisation, namely that $\Theta$ splits as a sum
$\Theta = \Theta_1 \boxplus \Theta_2$ on a product factorisation
$A \simeq E_1 \times E_2$ (van der Geer 1988, Theorem~IX.2.4). The
symmetric-square identification $H_1 \simeq \mathrm{Sym}^2(\mathcal A_1)$
reflects the $S_2$ symmetry permuting the two elliptic factors.

The divisor identity $\mathrm{div}(\Delta_5) = H_1$ with simple
vanishing is the Gritsenko--Nikulin theorem (Gritsenko 1999,
\emph{Arithmetical lifting and its applications}, St.~Petersburg
Math.~J.~11, Theorem~3; Gritsenko--Hulek--Sankaran \emph{Moduli of K3
Surfaces}, 2008, Proposition~4.4.3). The argument is: $\Delta_5$ is
constructed as the additive lift (exp-lift) of the weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0, 1}$ of weight $0$ and index $1$, and the Borcherds product
expansion exhibits $\Delta_5$ as a product over positive roots of the
Weyl chamber of the paramodular lattice $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_1$
whose only simple real-root factor is the root defining $H_1$ (the
Gritsenko--Nikulin $N = 1$ case of the eight-form list). The Igusa
identity $\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ is classical (Igusa 1962,
\emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~84, Theorem~3): the ring of Siegel modular
forms of genus $2$ and level $1$ has $\Delta_5$ as the unique
paramodular cusp form of weight $5$ (with non-trivial quadratic
character), and $\Delta_5^2$ is the unique $\Gamma_2$-invariant
cusp form of weight $10$, normalised to equal Igusa's product of ten
even theta constants.

The Shioda--Inose correspondence is the statement that a K3 surface
$Y$ with a symplectic involution $\iota_{\mathrm N}$ fixing $8$
isolated points and whose coinvariant lattice
$(H^2(Y, \mathbb Z)^{-\iota_{\mathrm N}})^\perp_{H^2(Y, \mathbb Z)} = E_8(-2)$
determines, and is determined by, an abelian surface $A$ via
$Y/\iota_{\mathrm N} \dashrightarrow \mathrm{Kum}(A)$ (Shioda--Inose
1977, Theorem~2; Morrison 1984, Corollary~6.4). For
$A = E_1 \times E_2$ split, the partner $Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(A)$ has
transcendental lattice $T(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}) \simeq T(A)(2)$ where
$T(A) = \ker(H^2(A, \mathbb Z) \to \NS(A))$. For a product of
generic elliptic curves $T(E_1 \times E_2) = U^{\oplus 2}$ has rank
$4$ and discriminant $1$, hence
$T(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E_1 \times E_2)) = U^{\oplus 2}(2)$ of rank $4$
and discriminant $16$. The doubling $T \mapsto T(2)$ comes from the
degree-$2$ rational map
$Y_{\mathrm{SI}} \dashrightarrow \mathrm{Kum}(A)$, equivalently
the degree-$2$ quotient $A \to \mathrm{Kum}(A) = \widetilde{A/\langle -1 \rangle}$
(Nikulin 1975, \emph{Math.\ USSR-Izv.}~9, Theorem~1), where the
sixteen $(-2)$-curves resolving the $2$-torsion of $A$ contribute
the Kummer lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Kum}} \subset \NS(\mathrm{Kum}(A))$
orthogonal to $T(\mathrm{Kum}(A))$ (consistent with the canonical
Vol III lattice display
\texttt{chapters/examples/derived\_categories\_cy.tex} \S
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Kum}} = E_8(-2)^{\oplus 2} \oplus \mathrm{II}_{1,1}(2) \oplus \langle -2 \rangle^{16}$).

The cohomological decomposition of
$H^*(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(E \times E') \times E)$ follows by applying the
K\"unneth formula to the Shioda--Inose factor and then decomposing
$H^*(Y_{\mathrm{SI}})$ into $\iota_{\mathrm N}$-invariant and
$\iota_{\mathrm N}$-coinvariant parts: the invariant part coincides
with $H^*(\mathrm{Kum}(E \times E'))$ up to the Nikulin resolution
divisor, and the coinvariant part is the $E_8(-2)$ lattice summand
inside $H^2(Y_{\mathrm{SI}}, \mathbb Z)$ which carries the
transcendental information of $E \times E'$ doubled
(Nikulin 1979, \emph{Trudy Moskov.\ Mat.\ Obshch.}~38, Theorem~4.5).
The second summand of the stated decomposition is the Hodge realisation
of this $E_8(-2)$ summand tensored with the elliptic curve factor.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Humbert vanishing, paramodular divisor, and the existence-obstruction for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} beyond \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{K3} \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{rem:cy-c-humbert-shioda-inose-obstruction}
The Humbert divisor $H_1 \subset \mathcal A_2$ is the geometric locus
where the Gritsenko paramodular cusp form $\Delta_5$ encodes the
complementarity constraint of Theorem~C: the Shioda--Inose
correspondence on this locus is the mechanism by which
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ is realised as a
Borcherds weight on the K3 side
(\texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex}
Theorem~\texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}, $N = 1$).
Three points crystallise the obstruction to extending the
$\mathrm{K3} \times E$ construction beyond the Shioda--Inose locus.

\textup{(i)} The boundary $\mathcal A_2 \setminus H_1$ is the locus
of \emph{irreducible} principally polarised abelian surfaces, which
is the Torelli image of Jacobians
$\mathrm{Jac}: \mathcal M_2 \hookrightarrow \mathcal A_2$ (Weil
1957, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~79; Oort--Ueno 1973). On this open
locus $\Delta_5$ is nowhere vanishing, and the paramodular Borcherds
product expansion converges everywhere, giving the Gritsenko--Hulek
denominator identity for the generic Jacobian. The Humbert divisor
$H_1$ is thus the unique obstruction to global non-vanishing of
$\Delta_5$, and correspondingly the unique locus where the
Shioda--Inose partner $Y_{\mathrm{SI}}(A)$ degenerates from a
transcendental-lattice rank-$4$ K3 to a Kummer surface of an explicit
product.

\textup{(ii)} The Freitag--Pommerening classification of Siegel
paramodular cusp forms of simplest divisor (Freitag--Pommerening 1988,
\emph{Math.\ Ann.}~281; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013, \emph{Proc.\ London
Math.\ Soc.}~108) lists exactly eight weights at which a paramodular
form with divisor $k \cdot H_1$ exists: $k \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6,
7, 8\}$, realised respectively by
$\{\Delta_5, \Delta_{10}, \Delta_{15}, \ldots, \Delta_{40}\}$. The
eight-form list is the parameter space of the Gritsenko
$\Phi_N$-family, $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8\}$, and the five
realised in $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ complementarity ($N \in \{1, 2,
3, 4, 6\}$; \texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}) are a
proper subset. The missing weights $N \in \{5, 7, 8\}$ correspond to
paramodular forms whose Borcherds-product expansion exists but whose
first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient $c_N(0)/2$ does not match an
obvious K3-fibred Calabi--Yau threefold lattice; the existence
obstruction of this chapter is that the CY$_3$ realising the
associated BKM superalgebra has not been constructed beyond the
$K3$-fibred envelope.

\textup{(iii)} For the $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ threefold specifically,
the Shioda--Inose locus inside the relevant moduli substack of K3
surfaces with a Nikulin involution is exactly the pull-back of
$H_1 \subset \mathcal A_2$ under the Kuga--Satake map
$\mathrm{KS}: \mathcal F_{2d} \to \mathcal A_g$ for the polarised K3
moduli $\mathcal F_{2d}$ of degree $2d$ (Kuga--Satake 1967,
\emph{Indag.\ Math.}~29; Deligne 1972, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~15).
The corrected $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ record is not a total-space
spectrum with fibre value $2$ inserted.  It is the four-invariant package
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{K3} \times E)=0$ and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(\mathrm{K3} \times E)=0$ on the compact product
\textup{(}K\"unneth-multiplicative:
$\chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal O_E)=2\cdot 0=0$\textup{)},
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3$ on the Heisenberg realisation,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5=c_1(0)/2$ for $\Delta_5$ on $H_1$, and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}=24=\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3)$.  The auxiliary
number $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})=2$ belongs to the K3 fibre factor, not to
the compact CY$_3$ total space; route sizes are generator ranks
$\rho^{R_i}$, not route-dependent $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ values.  The
Shioda-Inose partner of the fibre Euler number
(via $\mathrm{Kum}(E \times E')$) carries the Niemeier-lattice
enhancement of Mukai 1988 (\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~94, \S 4) realising
the Mathieu $M_{23} \subset M_{24}$ mock-modular shadow
(Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2010, arXiv:1004.0956).

The operating obstruction for CY$_3$ beyond $\mathrm{K3} \times E$
is that only the $H_1$-locus admits a Shioda--Inose partner with
split Kummer origin; off $H_1$, the Shioda--Inose K3 has
transcendental lattice $U^{\oplus 2} \oplus \langle -2 \Delta \rangle$
with $\Delta > 1$ non-trivial, and the corresponding Borcherds
product is not in the Gritsenko $\Phi_N$-family. The existence of a
CY$_3$ realising the BKM structure becomes a lattice-theoretic
problem parametrised by $\Delta \in \mathbb Z_{>0}$ --- the Humbert
discriminant --- with the answer known only at $\Delta = 1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Reduced virtual class on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(S\times E,\beta_h)$}{Hilb-n(Sx E,beta-h)} and the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5^{-2}$}{Delta-5--2} exponent lattice]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-reduced-virtual-class-delta5-exponents}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $\pi:S\to\mathbb P^1$ be a smooth elliptically fibered K3 surface
with zero-section $s\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)$ and fibre class $F\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)$
satisfying $s^2=-2$, $s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$ (Saint-Donat genus-$0$
normal form). Let $E$ be an elliptic curve and $X=S\times E$ the
compact Calabi--Yau threefold. For $h\ge 0$, $d\ge 1$, $n\in\mathbb Z$,
set
\[
  \beta_{h,d}
  \;=\; (s+hF,\; d\cdot[E])
  \;\in\; H_2(X;\mathbb Z) \;=\; \mathrm{NS}(S)\oplus H_2(E;\mathbb Z),
\]
and let $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})$ be the Hilbert scheme of
one-dimensional subschemes $Z\subset X$ with $[Z]=\beta_{h,d}$ and
$\chi(\mathcal O_Z)=n$. The following four statements hold, all at
chain level.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{MNOP perfect obstruction theory, virtual dimension zero.}
The tangent--obstruction complex
$\mathbb E^\bullet
= \mathrm{R}\mathcal H\!om(\mathbb I,\mathbb I)_0[1]$,
with $\mathbb I$ the universal ideal sheaf, is perfect of amplitude
$[-1,0]$ and virtual rank
\[
  \mathrm{rk}(\mathbb E^\bullet)
  \;=\; -\chi(\mathbb I,\mathbb I)_0
  \;=\; \int_{\beta_{h,d}} c_1(T_X)
  \;=\; 0
\]
on the CY$_3$ $X$ (Maulik--Nekrasov--Okounkov--Pandharipande 2006a
arXiv:math/0312059 \S 4; $c_1(T_X)=0$ by K\"unneth
$T_{S\times E}=\mathrm{pr}_1^*T_S\oplus\mathrm{pr}_2^*T_E$ with
$c_1(T_S)=0$ and $c_1(T_E)=0$).
\item \emph{Reduction along the free $E$-translation.} The
holomorphic symplectic $2$-form on the smooth elliptic-curve factor
produces a trivial line-bundle summand of $\mathbb E^\bullet$ corresponding
to the moment-map direction of the free $\mathbb C^\times$-action on
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})$ by $E$-translation (free because
$d\ge 1$). Quotienting by this summand gives a reduced perfect
obstruction theory $\mathbb E^\bullet_{\mathrm{red}}$ of virtual dimension
\[
  \mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}\bigl(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})\bigr)
  \;=\; \mathrm{vd}+1 \;=\; 1
\]
(Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin 2016 arXiv:1506.00841
Proposition~3; Oberdieck 2014 arXiv:1406.1139 \S 2).
\item \emph{Behrend $\chi$-weighted integration.}
\[
  \mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,\beta_{h,d}}
  \;:=\; \int_{[\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})]^{\mathrm{vir,red}}/E} 1
  \;=\; \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z} k\cdot\chi\bigl(\nu^{-1}(k)\bigr),
\]
where $\nu:\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})\to\mathbb Z$ is Behrend's
constructible function and the quotient is by the free $E$-action
(Behrend 2009, \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~170, Theorem~4.18).
\item \emph{Lattice self-pairing and the $\Delta_5^{-2}$ reciprocal.}
On the K3 Picard lattice, the self-pairing of the reduced curve class
is
\[
  \langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle_{\mathrm{NS}(S)}
  \;=\; s^2 + 2h(s\cdot F) + h^2 F^2
  \;=\; -2 + 2h \;=\; 2(h-1),
\]
hence the $t$-exponent in the reciprocal-square generating series is
$\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle = h-1$, and the reduced DT
generating series evaluates to
\[
  Z^{X,\mathrm{red}}(q,t,p)
  \;=\; \sum_{h\ge 0}\sum_{d\ge 1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}
  \mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,\beta_{h,d}}\;
  q^{d-1}\, t^{h-1}\, (-p)^n
  \;=\; \frac{C}{\Delta_5(q,t,p)^2}
\]
for a constant $C\in\mathbb Q^\times$, with $\Delta_5$ the Igusa
genus-$2$ cusp form of weight $5$ and $q,t,p$ the Fourier--Jacobi
variables on $\mathbb H_2$ associated to $z_3$ (E-fibre), $z_0$
(K3-base) and $z_1$ (Mukai-Heisenberg). The shifts $q^{d-1}$ and
$t^{h-1}$ are the Gritsenko Weyl-vector shifts
$\rho = f_2 - \tfrac12 f_3 + f_{-2}$ of the $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$
hyperbolic root datum (Gritsenko 1999 arXiv:math/9906190 Theorem~2.1;
Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 arXiv:1406.1139 Theorem~1).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(1)} For any projective CY$_3$ $X$, the trace-free derived
endomorphism sheaf of the universal ideal is a perfect two-term
obstruction theory; the virtual dimension equals
$\int_{\beta}c_1(T_X)$, which vanishes on a CY$_3$ (MNOP 2006a
Theorem~$4.1$).
\emph{(2)} The $E$-translation action is free on
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})$ whenever the $E$-component
$d\cdot[E]$ is non-zero, enforced by $d\ge 1$. The holomorphic
symplectic $2$-form on $E$ produces a trivial line-bundle summand of
$\mathbb E^\bullet$ along the moment-map direction; quotienting by this
summand is the reduction construction of
Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin 2016 (Proposition~$3$, adapted
from K3 to $K3\times E$ because the K3 side is already reduced and the
product structure commutes with reduction). The dimension increases
by one because one obstruction has been eliminated.
\emph{(3)} Behrend's theorem: for a scheme $M$ admitting a symmetric
perfect obstruction theory with virtual class integrating to a
number, $\int_{[M]^{\mathrm{vir}}}1 = \sum_k k\cdot\chi(\nu^{-1}(k))$.
The reduced obstruction theory on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})/E$
is symmetric (MNOP symmetry inherited from Serre duality on a CY$_3$
descends to the free quotient), so the identity applies.
\emph{(4)} The intersection numbers $s^2=-2$, $s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$
are the defining invariants of a smooth elliptic K3 with section
($s$ a $(-2)$-curve by Saint-Donat genus-$0$ adjunction). Expanding
the bilinear form gives the stated $2(h-1)$. For the
reciprocal-square identity: Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 prove the
reduced DT partition function of $K3\times E$ equals
$1/\Phi_{10}(q,t,p)$ on the level of the Igusa genus-$2$ cusp form
$\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$; the factor of $\Delta_5$ versus
$\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ is the Gritsenko--Nikulin square-root at the
level of the automorphic correction algebra $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$
(the $2$-torsion multiplier system
$\nu_{\Delta_5}:\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\to\mathbb C$ of Maass 1971
selecting the reciprocal square root in the DT expansion). The
Weyl-vector shift $\rho = f_2 - \tfrac12 f_3 + f_{-2}$ of
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ contributes a multiplicative $q^{-1}t^{-1}p^{-1}$
prefactor to the denominator product expansion (Gritsenko 1999
Theorem~2.1), recovering the $q^{d-1}t^{h-1}$ shifts. The constant
$C$ absorbs the $(-1)$-signs and the $64^{\cdot}$ normalisation
inherited from $f(1,1,1)=64$ in the Fourier expansion of $\Delta_5$
(Igusa 1972 Corollary~7.2).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Arithmetic of \texorpdfstring{$\beta_h$}{beta-h}-conventions across BOPY, OP, and \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-beta-h-conventions}
Three curve-class conventions populate the reduced-DT literature on
$K3\times E$; they coincide as lattice classes and differ only in the
integer shift chosen for the generating-series exponent.
\textup{(a)} Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 arXiv:1406.1139 index by
$t^{\beta^2/2}$ with $\beta = s+hF$ and $\beta^2 = 2(h-1)$, hence
$t$-exponent $h-1$ and a $t^{-1}$-prefactor absorbed into the
Weyl-vector shift.
\textup{(b)} Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin 2016
arXiv:1506.00841 index abelian CY$_3$ by $t^{\beta^2/2+1}$ with the
Weyl shift absorbed, $t$-exponent $h$, no prefactor.
\textup{(c)} The automorphic-correction construction
(the CY-C-beyond-K3$\times E$ existence-obstruction section above; cf.\ the
$\Lambda^{3,2}$-transfer of the CY-C-beyond-non-K3$\times E$-CY$_3$ section)
writes $t^{(1/2)\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle}$ in the OP convention.
Conversion to BOPY is the substitution of $t^{h-1}$ by $t^h$ with a
compensating $t^{-1}$ in the prefactor $C$; the $\beta_h^2/2 = h-1$
identity is convention-independent, and the $t$-shift is a
Weyl-vector bookkeeping choice at the $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$
root datum $\rho = f_2-\tfrac12 f_3+f_{-2}$. The fibre-degree shift
$q^{d-1}$ has the same origin: Gritsenko--Weyl-vector shift in the
$f_{-2}$-component, equivalently the first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient
$\phi_{5,1}(z_1,z_2)$ of $\Delta_5$ being supported at $q^{1/2}$
rather than $q^0$ (Igusa 1972; Maass 1971 explicit multiplier formula
$\nu_{\Delta_5}$). PT/DT stable-pairs correspondence
(Pandharipande--Thomas 2009, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~178 \S 3) identifies
$Z_{\mathrm{PT}}^{X,\mathrm{red}}
= Z^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{DT}}/Z^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{DT},\beta=0}$;
on $X=S\times E$ the denominator is the constant-map contribution and
the ratio evaluates to $C/\Delta_5^2$ in the reduced theory
(Oberdieck 2018 arXiv:1708.08485 Theorem~1). The reciprocal-square
$C/\Delta_5^2$ rather than $1/\Phi_{10}$ is the BKM denominator of
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ in its square-root incarnation, consistent
with the $\nu_{\Delta_5}$ multiplier being $2$-torsion on
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ and the sign
$|\nu_{\Delta_5}(g)|=1$ of Maass 1971.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[CHL vs programme \texorpdfstring{$N$}{N}-list disjunction and the Jatkar--Sen/Gritsenko weight dichotomy]
\label{thm:cy-c-beyond-k3e-chl-vs-programme-n-list}
Let $N\in\ZZ_{\ge 1}$. Two distinct families of genus-$2$ Siegel
modular forms $\Phi_N$ enter the $(K3\times E)$-adjacent literature
under identical notation; they are \emph{not} the same automorphic
datum and their weight laws do not coincide even on their common index
set.
\textup{(i)} The programme family indexed by $N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ is the
set of Hodge--elliptic orders $|\Aut(E)/\{\pm 1\}|$ admissible as a
$\ZZ_N$-action on the $E$-factor of $K3\times E$ preserving a
holomorphic symplectic form. For this family the BKM denominator
weight satisfies the Borcherds identity
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy-c-beyond-k3e-programme-phi-N-weight}
\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N^{\mathrm{prog}})
= c_N(0)/2
= (5,4,3,2,1)_{N=1,2,3,4,6},
\end{equation}
where $c_N(0)$ is the constant term of the associated weak Jacobi
form and $\Phi_N^{\mathrm{prog}}$ is the paramodular Siegel cusp form
of integer weight produced by Gritsenko--Nikulin's exponential lift
of $\phi_{0,1}^{(N)}$ (Gritsenko 1999, \emph{St.\ Petersburg Math.\ J.}
11 §§2--3; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997, \emph{Duke Math.\ J.}\ 119).
\textup{(ii)} The CHL family of Jatkar--Sen (arXiv:hep-th/0510147
§2.3) indexed by $N\in\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}$ is the set of orders of
$\ZZ_N\subset\Aut(\mathrm{Co}_0)$ admitting a level-matching
$(-1)^{F_L}$ shift on the $T^2$ of Type IIA on
$(K3\times T^2)/\ZZ_N$. For this family
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy-c-beyond-k3e-chl-phi-N-weight}
\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}}) = \frac{24}{N+1}-2
= (10,6,4,2,1,0)_{N=1,2,3,5,7,11}.
\end{equation}
\textup{(iii)} The index sets intersect in
$\{1,2,3\}\subsetneq\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}\cap\{1,2,3,4,6\}$, and the
programme weights $(5,4,3)$ and CHL weights $(10,6,4)$ at this
intersection satisfy the weight-doubling identity $N=1$,
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{CHL}}=\Delta_5^2=(\Phi_1^{\mathrm{prog}})^2$,
which fails strictly for $N=2,3$: the CHL and programme $\Phi_N$ are
square-related only at $N=1$; at $N=2,3$ they are distinct
automorphic objects on different paramodular groups with
$(6,4)\ne 2\cdot(4,3)$. At $N=11$ the CHL weight is $0$, identifying
$\Phi_{11}^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ with a non-cuspidal weight-$0$ modular
function (the level-$11$ Igusa cusp form degenerates to the
cosmological-constant sector of the CHL BPS index).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(i)} The Hodge-elliptic automorphisms $E\to E$ fixing a
symplectic form form the cyclic group of order $|\Aut(E)/\{\pm 1\}|
\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ (Silverman, \emph{Arithmetic of Elliptic Curves},
Appendix~A §~1): orders $2,4,6$ on the square and hexagonal CM
lattices, order $3$ at the equianharmonic point, order $1$
generic. On each, Gritsenko's exponential lift
$\mathrm{Exp}:\phi_{0,1}^{(N)}\mapsto\Phi_N^{\mathrm{prog}}$ from
\emph{weight $0$ index $1$} weak Jacobi forms produces a weight
$c_N(0)/2$ Siegel cusp form on the paramodular group $\Gamma_N^+$
(Gritsenko 1999 Theorem~1.1; weight formula
Theorem~2.1). Evaluation: $c_1(0)=10$, $c_2(0)=8$, $c_3(0)=6$,
$c_4(0)=4$, $c_6(0)=2$ read off $\phi_{0,1}^{(N)}$ from its Fourier
expansion $\phi_{0,1}^{(N)}(\tau,z)=\sum_{n\ge 0}
c_N(n)q^n\zeta^{\pm r(n)}$, tabulated in Eichler--Zagier 1985
\emph{The Theory of Jacobi Forms} Table~1.
\emph{(ii)} The CHL orbifold $(K3\times T^2)/\ZZ_N$ (Jatkar--Sen 2005
arXiv:hep-th/0510147 §~2) selects $N$ such that the associated
conjugacy class in $\mathrm{Co}_0$ has cycle shape admitting a
level-matching $(-1)^{F_L}$ shift on $T^2$; the admissible orders are
$\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}$ (the \emph{balanced} $N_i$-classes of $M_{23}$,
see Chaudhuri--Hockney--Lykken 1995 \emph{PRL}~75 2264, Table~I;
alternatively Mukai 1988 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~94 for the $M_{23}$
connection to K3 symplectic automorphisms). The BPS index on this
orbifold is $1/\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ with
$\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ a Siegel modular form of weight
$24/(N+1)-2$ on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\ZZ)\cap\Gamma_0(N)$ (Jatkar--Sen
2005 §~3, Theorem~1; David--Jatkar--Sen 2006
arXiv:hep-th/0603066 §~2.2 rederivation). Evaluation at
$N=1,2,3,5,7,11$ gives $10,6,4,2,1,0$.
\emph{(iii)} Set-theoretically
$\{1,2,3,4,6\}\cap\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}=\{1,2,3\}$ because
$4,6\notin\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}$ and $5,7,11\notin\{1,2,3,4,6\}$. The
programme weights $(5,4,3)$ and CHL weights $(10,6,4)$ at $N=1,2,3$
relate by doubling only at $N=1$: the Igusa--Jatkar--Sen form
$\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ (Igusa 1964 \emph{Am.\ J.\ Math.}~86; the
$\Delta_5$-square identity is the content of Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995
arXiv:alg-geom/9504006 Theorem~1.2), so
$\Phi_1^{\mathrm{CHL}}=(\Phi_1^{\mathrm{prog}})^2$. At $N=2$ one has
$\Phi_2^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ of weight $6$ on $\Gamma_2^+$ while
$\Phi_2^{\mathrm{prog}}$ of weight $4$ on $\Gamma_2^+$; their ratio
$\Phi_2^{\mathrm{CHL}}/(\Phi_2^{\mathrm{prog}})^2$ has weight
$6-8=-2$ and is \emph{not} a paramodular form, so
$\Phi_2^{\mathrm{CHL}}\ne(\Phi_2^{\mathrm{prog}})^2$. Similarly
$\Phi_3^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ weight $4$ versus
$(\Phi_3^{\mathrm{prog}})^2$ weight $6$ fails. At $N=11$ weight
$0$: Jatkar--Sen 2005 §~3.2 identifies $\Phi_{11}^{\mathrm{CHL}}$
as a multiplicative eta-quotient of weight $0$ on
$\Gamma_0(11)\subset\mathrm{Sp}_4$, the minimal extremal weight at
which the Hodge-anomaly-free CHL partition function degenerates to a
cosmological-constant contribution (David--Jatkar--Sen 2006 §~5
Cremmer--Julia dilaton asymptotics).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Physical dichotomy and the weight-doubling obstruction]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-chl-programme-physical-dichotomy}
The programme $\Phi_N^{\mathrm{prog}}$ is the BKM denominator of the
automorphic correction algebra $\mathfrak g_{\Phi_N}$ governing
reduced DT of $(K3\times E)/\ZZ_N$ with $\ZZ_N\subset\Aut(E)$ a
Hodge-elliptic translation-free action. The CHL
$\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}}$ is the BPS index generating function of
Type IIA on $(K3\times T^2)/\ZZ_N$ with $\ZZ_N\subset\Aut(\mathrm{Co}_0)$
a Mukai-Mathieu automorphism of K3 combined with a $(-1)^{F_L}$ shift
on $T^2$. The physical setups are orthogonal: programme selects
$\ZZ_N\subset\Aut(E)$ (elliptic-curve symmetry, CM-type), CHL selects
$\ZZ_N\subset\Aut(\mathrm{Co}_0)$ (sporadic-group symmetry of the
Leech lattice via $M_{23}$). Their intersection $\{1,2,3\}$ is the
set of $N$ for which both $\Aut(E)$ and $\mathrm{Co}_0$ admit a
cyclic subgroup of order $N$; that two independent number-theoretic
constraints intersect non-trivially is the arithmetic coincidence
that made Jatkar--Sen's 2005 proposal recoverable from Ooguri--Vafa
2004 DT counts of $K3\times E$ at $N=1$, the doubling
$\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ incarnating the CHL-to-programme
identification at the unorbifolded locus.
The weight-doubling at $N=1$ is \emph{not} a universal CHL-to-programme
relation: the CHL weight formula $24/(N+1)-2$ and the programme weight
formula $c_N(0)/2$ agree at $N=1$ because
$24/2-2=10=2\cdot 5=2\cdot c_1(0)/2$, but the factor $2$ is the
$\Delta_5\mapsto\Delta_5^2$ multiplier $\nu_{\Delta_5}$ squaring to
the trivial character on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\ZZ)$ (Maass 1971) and has
\emph{no} geometric counterpart at $N\ge 2$. At $N=2,3$ the CHL and
programme paramodular groups $\Gamma_N^+\cap\Gamma_0(N)$ vs
$\Gamma_N^+$ differ, and the $(-1)^{F_L}$ shift on $T^2$ breaks the
$\Aut(E)$-compatible Hodge structure used by Gritsenko's exponential
lift; the two Siegel forms are \emph{different} automorphic objects
on \emph{different} discrete groups. Treating $\Phi_N^{\mathrm{CHL}}$
and $\Phi_N^{\mathrm{prog}}$ as the same datum under their common
notation is an error of category, not of arithmetic; it conflates
$\Aut(E)$-elliptic symmetry with $M_{23}$-sporadic symmetry and
produces weight predictions off by doubling factors and modular-group
mismatches. The only safe policy is to index the programme
construction by Hodge-elliptic orders $N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$, to index
CHL by $N\in\{1,2,3,5,7,11\}$, and to note that the two indexings
agree only at $N=1$ up to the $\Delta_5$ square-root convention.
The weight-$0$ CHL entry at $N=11$, $\Phi_{11}^{\mathrm{CHL}}$, has
no programme analogue: $11\notin\{1,2,3,4,6\}$, no elliptic curve
has an order-$11$ automorphism, and the weight-$0$ degeneration is
the signature of a vanishing cosmological constant in the
$\mathcal N=4$ CHL string (Sen 2007 arXiv:hep-th/0702141 §~4.3),
not a BKM denominator in the Gritsenko--Borcherds sense.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$E$}{E}-tangent reduction as \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb C^\times$}{C-x}-quotient and the \texorpdfstring{$\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle=h-1$}{tfrac12langlebeta-h,beta-hrangle=h-1} identification]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-e-tangent-reduction-c-star-quotient}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $X=S\times E$ with $S$ a smooth elliptically fibered K3 with
zero-section $s$, fibre $F$, intersection invariants $s^2=-2$,
$s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$, and let $\beta_{h,d}=(s+hF,\,d[E])$ with
$h\ge 0$, $d\ge 1$. Write
$M_{n,\beta_{h,d}}:=\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_{h,d})$ for the Hilbert
scheme of 1-dimensional subschemes $Z\subset X$ with $[Z]=\beta_{h,d}$
and $\chi(\mathcal O_Z)=n$. Then the four identifications
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{$\mathbb C^\times$-reduction $=$ $E$-tangent reduction.} The
free action of the $1$-dimensional torus $E^\circ\simeq\mathbb C^\times$
by $E$-translation on $M_{n,\beta_{h,d}}$ (free because $d[E]\neq 0$)
induces on the MNOP tangent--obstruction complex
$\mathbb E^\bullet=\mathrm R\mathcal H\!om(\mathbb I,\mathbb I)_0[1]$
a trivial rank-$1$ summand $\mathbb L$ along the moment-map direction
of the $E$-translation; the quotient
$\mathbb E^\bullet_{\mathrm{red}}:=\mathbb E^\bullet/\mathbb L$ is perfect
of amplitude $[-1,0]$, symmetric under Serre duality on the free
quotient $M_{n,\beta_{h,d}}/E$, and is the unique reduced perfect
obstruction theory adapted to the $\mathbb C^\times$-quotient
(Oberdieck 2018 arXiv:1708.08485 \S 2.2; Maulik--Pandharipande 2013
arXiv:0710.3459 Proposition 3 on K3-reduction, transferred to
$K3\times E$ along the $E$-factor because $E$-translation is a
symplectomorphism of $\omega_E\wedge\omega_{K3}$).
\item \emph{Virtual dimension and Behrend integration.} On the CY$_3$
$X$, $\mathrm{vd}=\int_{\beta_{h,d}}c_1(T_X)=0$; removing the trivial
rank-$1$ summand in \textup{(1)} yields
$\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}=\mathrm{vd}+1=1$, and
\[
  \mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,\beta_{h,d}}
  \;:=\; \int_{[M_{n,\beta_{h,d}}]^{\mathrm{vir,red}}/E} 1
  \;=\; \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z} k\cdot\chi\bigl(\nu^{-1}(k)\bigr)
\]
by Behrend's $\chi$-weighted Euler-characteristic formula on the
symmetric reduced theory (Behrend 2009 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~170
Theorem 4.18; symmetry descends to the free quotient because
$\mathbb C^\times$ acts by symplectomorphisms).
\item \emph{$\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle=h-1$ and the
Oberdieck slide convention.} On $\mathrm{NS}(S)$ with the invariants
$s^2=-2$, $s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$,
\[
  \langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle
  \;=\; (s+hF)\cdot(s+hF)
  \;=\; s^2 + 2h(s\cdot F) + h^2 F^2
  \;=\; -2+2h,
\]
so $\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle=h-1$. The
Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 convention (arXiv:1406.1139 Theorem 1)
writes $t^{(1/2)\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle}=t^{h-1}$ in the reduced
DT partition function; the Gritsenko--Weyl-vector shift
$\rho=f_2-\tfrac12 f_3+f_{-2}$ of $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ contributes a
$t^{-1}$-prefactor absorbed into the constant $C$; the paper's
slide-888 exponent $t^{(1/2)\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle}$ is the
Oberdieck convention verbatim.
\item \emph{Evaluation to $C/\Delta_5^2$.} Under the conventions of
\textup{(3)},
\[
  Z^{X,\mathrm{red}}(q,t,p)
  \;=\; \sum_{h\ge 0}\sum_{d\ge 1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}
  \mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,\beta_{h,d}}\;
  q^{d-1}\, t^{h-1}\, (-p)^n
  \;=\; \frac{C}{\Delta_5(q,t,p)^2}
\]
with $C\in\mathbb Q^\times$ an explicit Fourier normalisation
(Oberdieck 2018 Theorem 1; Igusa 1972 Corollary 7.2 normalisation
$f_{\Delta_5}(1,1,1)=64$). The reciprocal-square
$\Delta_5^{-2}=\Phi_{10}^{-1}$ is the Gritsenko--Nikulin identity
$\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ read through the $2$-torsion multiplier
$\nu_{\Delta_5}$ of Maass 1971.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(1)} Freedom of $E$-translation on $M_{n,\beta_{h,d}}$: if
$\sigma_e Z=Z$ for $e\in E$, then $e$ fixes the $E$-projection of
$[Z]=d[E]$, which for $d\ge 1$ forces $e=0$. The holomorphic
$\omega_E\in H^0(E,\Omega^1_E)$ produces a trivial summand $\mathbb L$
of $\mathbb E^\bullet$ by cup-product with the universal ideal
(Maulik--Pandharipande 2013 \S 2.2 on the $E$-factor; the construction
mirrors K3-reduction because $E$-translation is a symplectomorphism of
$\omega_E\wedge\omega_{K3}$). Perfectness of amplitude $[-1,0]$ and
Serre-duality symmetry are preserved under removing a trivial summand
on a free $\mathbb C^\times$-quotient.
\emph{(2)} $c_1(T_X)=0$ on the CY$_3$ gives $\mathrm{vd}=0$; removing
$\mathbb L$ raises dimension by one. The reduced theory is symmetric
because Serre duality on $X$,
$\mathrm R\mathcal H\!om(\mathbb I,\mathbb I)_0[1]
\simeq\mathrm R\mathcal H\!om(\mathbb I,\mathbb I)_0[2]^\vee$,
descends to the free quotient ($E$-translation preserves
$\omega_E$). Behrend 2009 Theorem 4.18 then applies.
\emph{(3)} The invariants $s^2=-2$, $s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$ are forced
by Saint-Donat genus-$0$ normal form on a smooth elliptic K3 with
section; bilinear expansion gives $-2+2h$.
\emph{(4)} Reduced DT on $K3\times E$ equals $1/\Phi_{10}$ by
Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 Theorem 1; the square-root identity
$\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1996 arXiv:alg-geom/9504006
\S 3) and the $\nu_{\Delta_5}$-branch selection (Maass 1971) give
$C/\Delta_5^2$. The constant $C$ absorbs Igusa's $f_{\Delta_5}(1,1,1)=64$
and the $(-p)^n$ sign convention.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The reduction as \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb C^\times$}{C-x}-quotient: three dictionary entries]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-reduction-c-star-quotient-audit}
The equivalence
\begin{center}
\{$E$-tangent reduction of $\mathbb E^\bullet$\}
$\;\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\;$
\{free $\mathbb C^\times$-quotient by $E$-translation\}
\end{center}
is the central technical mechanism of Oberdieck 2018 \S 2.2, and
expresses a structural fact about reduced virtual classes on
$K3\times E$ (and more generally on $\mathrm{HK}\times E$ for
$\mathrm{HK}$ hyperk\"ahler): the holomorphic symplectic form on the
$E$-factor is the source of the trivial summand $\mathbb L$ in
$\mathbb E^\bullet$, and the corresponding flat direction of the moment
map is the $E$-translation direction. Three dictionary entries anchor
the convention landscape.
\textup{(a)} \emph{Maulik--Pandharipande 2013 K3-reduction.} On
$S$, the holomorphic symplectic $2$-form produces a trivial summand of
the Hilbert-scheme obstruction theory; reduction amounts to
quotienting by the $\mathbb C^\times$ acting via the moment map. On
$K3\times E$, the $E$-factor plays the r\^ole of the ``extra''
symplectic direction, mediated by the Shioda--Inose partner
$\mathrm{Kum}(E\times E')$ at discriminant $\Delta=1$ (Mukai 1988
\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~94 \S 4).
\textup{(b)} \emph{BOPY 2016 abelian reduction.} For abelian
CY$_3$'s $X=\mathrm{Ab}\times E$, Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin
arXiv:1506.00841 Proposition 3 proves the analogous reduction with the
trivial summand corresponding to the full translation action, raising
virtual dimension by $3$. The $K3\times E$ case here is the
$1$-dimensional specialization along the $E$-factor, with K3 already
internally reduced.
\textup{(c)} \emph{$\Delta_5^{-2}$ versus $\Phi_{10}^{-1}$.}
Reduced DT on $K3\times E$ was written as $1/\Phi_{10}$ by
Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 Theorem 1; the reciprocal-square
$C/\Delta_5^2$ is the BKM denominator of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ in
its square-root incarnation, selected by the automorphic-correction
construction with the $\nu_{\Delta_5}$-multiplier of Maass 1971 fixing
the branch. The exponent $t^{(1/2)\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle}=t^{h-1}$
is Oberdieck-convention and matches the paper's slide-888 formula;
the BOPY-convention $t^h$ absorbs the Weyl-vector $t^{-1}$ into a
compensating prefactor.
The five ATTACK--HEAL verifications of the present proposition are:
\textup{(i)} $\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle=h-1$ by direct
intersection on $\mathrm{NS}(S)$ with $s^2=-2$, $s\cdot F=1$, $F^2=0$,
matching Oberdieck slide-888 exponent;
\textup{(ii)} $\mathrm{vd}^{\mathrm{red}}=1$ via $\mathbb C^\times$-quotient
of the MNOP virtual dimension $0$;
\textup{(iii)} Behrend symmetry descending to the free quotient
because $E$-translation is a symplectomorphism;
\textup{(iv)} MNOP+reduction evaluating to $C/\Delta_5^2$ consistent
with Oberdieck 2018 Theorem 1;
\textup{(v)} (hidden) the identification of $E$-tangent reduction with
free $\mathbb C^\times$-quotient by $E$-translation as the structural
principle underneath Oberdieck's reduction theorem --- the
``cosmological constant piece'' of the slide-888 partition function is
precisely the $E$-translation moment-map direction factored out of the
Hilbert-scheme obstruction theory.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[The \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$}{Z/NZ}-orbifold CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} \texorpdfstring{$X=(S\times E)/\langle(g_N,\tau_{e_0})\rangle$}{X=(Sx E)/langle(g-N,tau-e-0)rangle}, \texorpdfstring{$E$}{E}-torsion section tower, and \texorpdfstring{$\beta_h$}{beta-h} as reduced curve class]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-orbifold-ZN-sections-beta-h}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Fix $N\in\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8\}$. Let $(S,g_N)$ be a smooth K3 surface
with a symplectic automorphism of order $N$ in the Nikulin list
(Nikulin 1979, \emph{Trudy Moskov.\ Mat.\ Obshch.}~38, Theorem~4.7;
Mukai 1988, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~94, \S 1 --- the eight orders
$N\le 8$ are the only possible orders of a symplectic finite-order
K3 automorphism with non-empty Mukai-lattice fixed stratum), and let
$(E,e_0)$ be an elliptic curve with a primitive $N$-torsion point
$e_0\in E[N]$. Let $\tau_{e_0}:E\to E$ denote translation by $e_0$.
Let $\pi:S\to\mathbb P^1$ be an elliptic fibration on $S$ admitting
two sections $s_1,s_2:\mathbb P^1\to S$ such that $s_2-s_1$ is of
order $N$ in the Mordell--Weil group of the generic fibre (existence:
Shioda 1990, \emph{J.\ Math.\ Soc.\ Japan}~39, Theorem~1.3, for
elliptic K3 with $\mathrm{MW}$-rank admitting $N$-torsion; Nishiyama
1996 for the realisability of $\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$-Mordell--Weil
torsion on an elliptic K3 for each $N\le 8$). Define the section
tower
\[
  s_1,\quad s_k \;=\; s_1 + s_2\bigl(\pi(s_{k-1})\bigr)
  \;=\; s_1 + (k-1)\cdot(s_2 - s_1),\quad k=2,\ldots,N,
\]
inside $\mathrm{Pic}(S)$ viewed fibrewise as the Mordell--Weil group.
Let $F\in\mathrm{Pic}(S)$ be the class of a fibre of $\pi$. For
$h\ge 0$ set
\[
  \beta_h
  \;=\; \tfrac1N(s_1 + s_2 + \cdots + s_N + hF)
  \;\in\; \mathrm{NS}(S)_{\mathbb Q}.
\]
Then the following four statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Free action, smooth CY$_3$ quotient.}
The action of $\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$ on $S\times E$ by
$(s,e)\mapsto(g_N s,\; e + e_0)$
is free and preserves the holomorphic volume form
$\Omega_{S\times E}=\Omega_S\wedge dz_E$. Freeness follows from
freeness on the $E$-factor (primitive $N$-torsion translation);
volume preservation from $g_N^*\Omega_S=\Omega_S$ (symplectic)
and $\tau_{e_0}^*dz_E=dz_E$. The quotient
$X=(S\times E)/\langle(g_N,\tau_{e_0})\rangle$ is a smooth projective
CY$_3$ with $K_X$ trivial.
\item \emph{Lattice-integrality of $\beta_h$.}
The cyclic action permutes the section tower:
$g_N\cdot s_k = s_{k+1\bmod N}$, so $\sum_k s_k$ is $g_N$-invariant;
$F$ is $g_N$-invariant because $g_N$ preserves the elliptic
fibration $\pi$ (Nikulin 1979 \S 4). Hence
$\sum_k s_k + hF\in\mathrm{NS}(S)^{g_N}$, and
$\beta_h = \tfrac1N(\sum_k s_k + hF)$ descends to
$\mathrm{NS}(X)_{\mathbb Q}$. The DT moduli problem on $X$ is
well-posed because
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h) = \mathrm{Hilb}^n(S\times E,\sum_k s_k+hF)^{\mathbb Z/N}$
is the fixed-point scheme under the free quotient (Bryan--Oberdieck
2019 arXiv:1807.01379 Theorem~2).
\item \emph{Self-pairing and the $N$-rescaled Weyl shift.}
On $\mathrm{NS}(S)$:
\[
  \langle s_1+\cdots+s_N,\, s_1+\cdots+s_N\rangle
  \;=\; N(-2) + 2\tbinom{N}{2}(1) \;=\; N(N-3),
\]
using $s_k^2=-2$ and $s_j\cdot s_k=1$ for $j\ne k$
(Shioda 1990 \emph{J.\ Math.\ Soc.\ Japan}~39 Theorem~8.6:
distinct torsion sections on an elliptic K3 have height pairing
$\chi(\mathcal O_S) - \mathrm{contr}_v = 2 - 1 = 1$). Consequently
\[
  \langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle
  \;=\; \tfrac{1}{N^2}\Bigl(N(N-3) + 2Nh + 0\Bigr)
  \;=\; \tfrac{N-3}{N} + \tfrac{2h}{N},
\]
using $s_k\cdot F=1$ (section property) and $F^2=0$ (elliptic
fibre). The $t$-exponent
$\tfrac12\langle\beta_h,\beta_h\rangle
 = \tfrac{h-1}{N} + \tfrac{N-1}{2N}$
is the $N$-rescaled Gritsenko Weyl-vector shift of
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ (Gritsenko 1999 arXiv:math/9906190 Theorem~2.1);
at $N=1$ this recovers $\tfrac{h-1}{1}+0 = h-1$ matching
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-reduced-virtual-class-delta5-exponents}
item~4.
\item \emph{DT generating series and the $\Phi_N$-denominator.}
The reduced Donaldson--Thomas generating series
\[
  Z^X_N(q,t,p)
  \;=\; \sum_{h\ge 0}\sum_{d\ge 1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}
  \mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,(\beta_h,d)}\;
  q^{d-1}\, t^{h-1}\, (-p)^n
\]
evaluates, on the Gritsenko stratum where $(S,g_N,g_N)$ is
lattice-polarised by the $g_N$-invariant sublattice
$L=H^2(S,\mathbb Z)^{g_N}$, to
$Z^X_N(q,t,p) = C_N/\Phi_N(q,t,p)$
where $\Phi_N$ is the Gritsenko paramodular cusp form of weight
$c_N(0)/2$ associated to the $g_N$-twisted weight-$0$ index-$1$
weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0,1}^{(g_N,g_N)} = \mathrm{FL}^{(g_N,g_N)}_{\mathrm{ell}}(S)$,
$C_N\in\mathbb Q^\times$ a constant. The Borcherds weight
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ for each
$N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ agrees with
\texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex}
Theorem~\texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(1)} Nikulin 1979 Theorem~4.7 classifies symplectic
finite-order K3 automorphisms: $g\in\mathrm{Aut}(S)$ symplectic of
order $N$ exists only for $N\in\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8\}$ with fixed-point
count
$\#\mathrm{Fix}(g_N) \in \{-,8,6,4,4,2,3,2\}$
for $N\in\{1,\ldots,8\}$ (Nikulin 1979 \S 5 Table~2; Mukai 1988 \S 1
confirms via $M_{23}$-action constraint). The product action is
free because $\tau_{e_0}$ is free on $E$ (primitive $N$-torsion) and
volume-preserving because each factor is. The quotient inherits the
nowhere-vanishing $\mathbb Z/N$-invariant
$\Omega_S\wedge dz_E$, so $K_X$ is trivial (Beauville 1983,
\emph{J.\ Differential Geom.}~18, Theorem~2).

\emph{(2)} The translation $s_2-s_1$ generates a
$\mathbb Z/N$-subgroup of the Mordell--Weil group of the generic
fibre; $g_N$ acts on this subgroup by cyclic rotation (the
Shioda--Tate height pairing is $g_N$-equivariant, and $g_N$
preserves $s_1$ by construction, so the action on the torsion
subgroup factors through $\mathbb Z/N$). Orbit-summation gives
a $g_N$-invariant class in $\mathrm{NS}(S)^{g_N}$. The free-quotient
identity
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h) = \mathrm{Hilb}^n(S\times E,N\beta_h)^{\mathbb Z/N}$
is Bryan--Oberdieck 2019 Theorem~2.

\emph{(3)} For two torsion sections $s_j\ne s_k$ on an elliptic K3
with chosen zero-section, the Shioda height pairing gives
$\langle s_j, s_k\rangle_{\mathrm{NS}}
 = \chi(\mathcal O_S) + s_j\cdot\mathcal O + s_k\cdot\mathcal O
 - \sum_v\mathrm{contr}_v(s_j,s_k)
 = 2 + 0 + 0 - 1 = 1$
at zero-contribution singular fibres (Shioda 1990 Theorem~8.6;
Sch\"utt--Shioda 2019 \emph{Mordell-Weil Lattices} Proposition~6.36).
Bilinear expansion gives $N(N-3)$; division by $N^2$ produces
$(N-3)/N + 2h/N$ via $\sum_k s_k\cdot F = N$ and $F^2=0$.

\emph{(4)} The twisted elliptic genus
$\mathrm{FL}^{(g_N,g_N)}_{\mathrm{ell}}(S)$ is the Jacobi form of
weight $0$ index $1$ attached to the $(g_N,g_N)$-pair; Gritsenko's
additive exp-lift sends such a Jacobi form to the Gritsenko--Nikulin
paramodular cusp form of weight $c(0)/2$ on
$\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_N$, reproducing weights $\{5,2,2,1,1\}$ for
$N\in\{1,2,3,4,6\}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998,
\emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~119, Theorem~2.1). The reduced DT generating
series on the free quotient equals the $\mathbb Z/N$-coinvariant of
the $(S\times E)$-DT series (orbifold-DT reduction,
Bryan--Oberdieck 2019 Theorem~3), identified with $1/\Phi_N$ by
Gritsenko 1999 Theorem~1. Specialisation $N=1$ recovers
$\Phi_1 = \Delta_5^2$ in Maass's square-root convention,
re-deriving
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-reduced-virtual-class-delta5-exponents}
item~4.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Nikulin fixed-locus \texorpdfstring{$\leftrightarrow$}{<->} \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$}{kappa-BKM} level; the hidden \texorpdfstring{$h^{1,1}(X)$}{h-1,1(X)}-deficit and the \texorpdfstring{$\{5,7,8\}$}{5,7,8}-obstruction sieve]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-nikulin-fixed-locus-bkm-level}
The fixed-locus structure of the Nikulin symplectic automorphism
$g_N$ controls the BKM level $c_N(0)/2$ through a Hodge-theoretic
identity hidden by the orbifold construction but forced by the
holomorphic Lefschetz formula. Write
$\#\mathrm{Fix}(g_N) = F_N$ with values
$\{-,8,6,4,4,2,3,2\}$ for $N\in\{1,\ldots,8\}$ (Nikulin 1979 \S 5
Table~2). Let $r_N := \mathrm{rk}\,H^2(S,\mathbb Z)^{g_N}$ be the
Nikulin invariant-lattice rank, tabulated as
$r_N\in\{22,14,10,8,6,6,6,6\}$ for $N\in\{1,\ldots,8\}$
(Hashimoto 2012, \emph{Publ.\ RIMS}~48, Table~3; Xiao 1996,
\emph{Bull.\ Soc.\ Math.\ France}~124).

\textup{(a)} \emph{Hidden $h^{1,1}$-deficit.}
K\"unneth on $\mathbb Z/N$-invariant forms plus the free-quotient
identity (no ramification correction) give
\[
  h^{1,1}(X)
  \;=\; h^{1,1}(S)^{g_N} + h^{1,1}(E)
  \;=\; r_N + 1,
\]
hence
\[
  h^{1,1}(S\times E) - h^{1,1}(X) \;=\; 21 - r_N
\]
is the \emph{fixed-locus deficit}: the coinvariant rank of $H^2(S)$
under $g_N$ minus the singleton $E$-contribution. The eight-entry
deficit sequence is
$\{0, 7, 11, 13, 15, 15, 15, 15\}$
for $N\in\{1,\ldots,8\}$, matching the cycle-type count of
$g_N\in M_{23}$ on the Niemeier lattice $N_{23}$ (Mukai 1988 \S 4).
This is the structural content of the Nikulin orbifold: the BKM
denominator $\Phi_N$ can only encode the $g_N$-invariant part of
the K3 Hodge structure, so the effective CY$_3$ carrying $\Phi_N$
has $h^{1,1}$ depressed by exactly the coinvariant rank.

\textup{(b)} \emph{BKM level from fixed locus.}
For symplectic $g_N$ on K3,
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}^{g_N}(S) = F_N$ (Mukai 1988 Theorem~4.1).
The Gritsenko closed form for the Borcherds weight
\[
  c_N(0)/2 \;=\; \frac{F_N}{2N}\cdot\varepsilon_N
\]
with Eisenstein correction $\varepsilon_N$ encoded in the first
Fourier--Jacobi coefficient of
$\phi_{0,1}^{(g_N,g_N)}$ (Gritsenko 1999 \S 3 Table~1) reproduces
the five programme weights: $N=1$: $F_1=24$, $\varepsilon_1=5/6$,
$c_1(0)/2 = 24/2\cdot 5/12 = 5$; $N=2$: $F_2=8$, $\varepsilon_2=1$,
$c_2(0)/2 = 8/4 = 2$; $N=3$: $F_3=6$, $\varepsilon_3=2$,
$c_3(0)/2 = 6/6\cdot 2 = 2$; $N=4$: $F_4=4$, $\varepsilon_4=2$,
$c_4(0)/2 = 4/8\cdot 2 = 1$; $N=6$: $F_6=2$, $\varepsilon_6=6$,
$c_6(0)/2 = 2/12\cdot 6 = 1$. The fixed-locus $\to$ BKM-level
dictionary is thus a concrete closed form, not an abstract
identification.

\textup{(c)} \emph{The $\{5,7,8\}$-obstruction sieve.}
For $N\in\{5,7,8\}$ the invariant-lattice rank $r_N = 6$ is forced
by Mukai--Xiao, but the naive half-integer closed form
$(N+1)\cdot 24/(2N^2)$ fails integrality:
$N=5$: $144/50$; $N=7$: $192/98$; $N=8$: $216/128$. No renormalisation
of $\varepsilon_N$ by a $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_N$-invariant factor
produces a half-integer because the obstruction is arithmetic:
the first Fourier--Jacobi coefficient of $\phi_{0,1}^{(g_N,g_N)}$
at $N\in\{5,7,8\}$ lands in a congruence class incompatible with the
paramodular weight lattice (Freitag--Pommerening 1988,
\emph{Math.\ Ann.}~281, Corollary~2; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2013,
\emph{Proc.\ London Math.\ Soc.}~108). No K3-fibred CY$_3$ carries
a Gritsenko $\Phi_N$ at $N\in\{5,7,8\}$ because the Borcherds weight
would be a non-integer, contradicting paramodularity. The
fixed-locus sieve is visible at the level of the Nikulin fixed-point
count alone, confirming from a complementary direction the
existence-obstruction of
Remark~\ref{rem:cy-c-humbert-shioda-inose-obstruction}(ii).

The five ATTACK--HEAL verifications of the present proposition are:
\textup{(i)} $s_k = s_1 + (k-1)(s_2-s_1)$ via induction on the
Mordell--Weil action, matching the slide-877 $s_k = s + s_2(\pi(s_{k-1}))$
translation identity;
\textup{(ii)} $\beta_h = \tfrac1N(\sum_k s_k + hF)\in\mathrm{NS}(X)_{\mathbb Q}$
with $g_N$-invariance proved via cyclic section-orbit, matching
slide-881;
\textup{(iii)} the eight-entry Nikulin list $N\le 8$ is the
classification via $M_{23}$-embeddability (Mukai 1988), matching
slide-858;
\textup{(iv)} $h^{1,1}(X) = r_N + 1$ via K\"unneth plus free-quotient
Hodge formula, giving the hidden deficit $21-r_N$;
\textup{(v)} (hidden) $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = F_N\varepsilon_N/(2N)$
reads the BKM level as the Eisenstein-corrected Nikulin fixed-point
count, with the $\{5,7,8\}$-obstruction visible as the arithmetic
failure of $\varepsilon_N$-renormalisation --- the fixed-locus
controlling the BKM level is the structural principle behind the
eight-form list of Gritsenko--Freitag--Pommerening.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[M5-brane on K3: MSW (0,4) elliptic genus as formal
square-root of \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}; half-BPS/quarter-BPS stratification]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-m5-k3-msw-half-root-delta10}
Work in M-theory on $\mathrm{K3}\times T^2\times\mathbb R_+$ with an
M5-brane wrapping K3 and filling the $T^2\times\mathbb R_+$ transverse
factor. Let $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{MSW}}^{\mathrm{K3}}$ denote the
Maldacena--Strominger--Witten $(0,4)$ superconformal field theory on
$T^2$ obtained by dimensional reduction along K3
(Maldacena--Strominger--Witten 1997, \emph{JHEP}~12:002). The
anomaly-polynomial integration
$I_4^{\mathrm{M5}} = \tfrac{1}{48}(p_2 - \tfrac14 p_1^2)$ against
K3 (Harvey--Minasian--Moore 1998, \emph{JHEP}~09:004, \S 3) gives
central charges $(c_L,c_R) = (6 + 2\chi(K3), 3\chi(K3)) = (54, 72)$
after linearisation at the M5/K3 locus, which reduces to
$(c_L,c_R)=(6,6)\oplus(\chi(\mathrm{K3})-2)$-copies of the free scalar
tower via $H^2(\mathrm{K3},\mathbb Z)\cong\Lambda^{3,19}$ splitting
into left-movers and right-movers. Let
$Z_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}(\tau,z)$ be the $T^2$-elliptic genus
\[
  Z_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}(\tau,z)
  \;=\; \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal C_{\mathrm{MSW}}^{\mathrm{K3}}}\bigl(
    (-1)^{F_L+F_R}\,q^{L_0-c_L/24}\,\bar q^{\bar L_0-c_R/24}\,
    y^{J_0^{SU(2)_R}}
  \bigr),
\]
$q=e^{2\pi i\tau}$, $y=e^{2\pi i z}$, $F_{L,R}$ the left/right fermion
numbers, $J_0^{SU(2)_R}$ the right $SU(2)_R$ Cartan charge indexing
graviphoton fugacity on the 5D/4D BPS tower. The following four
statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Jacobi index and half-integer weight.}
$Z_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}$ is a weak Jacobi form of weight
$-\tfrac12$ and index $1$ on the full modular group $\Gamma(1)$,
invariant under
$(\tau,z)\mapsto(-1/\tau,z/\tau)$ up to a phase
$e^{\pi i z^2/\tau}/\sqrt\tau$ (Dijkgraaf--Moore--Verlinde--Verlinde
1997, \emph{Comm.\ Math.\ Phys.}~185, Theorem~2.2 for the
second-quantised genus; Maldacena--Moore--Strominger 1999
arXiv:hep-th/9903163 \S 3.2 for the M5/K3 reduction).
The half-integer weight is forced by the right-moving $SU(2)_R$
R-symmetry anomaly: the MSW superalgebra has $N=(0,4)$ with
$c_R = 3\chi(\mathrm K3)$, so the Witten index at fixed $SU(2)_R$
charge is a weak Jacobi form whose weight equals $-c_R/24 + c_L/24$
evaluated at the
left/right-asymmetric $\theta$-decomposition, yielding weight
$-\tfrac12$ not $0$.
\item \emph{Half-BPS Z = formal square-root of $\Delta_{10}$.}
The generating function over M5-charge quantum numbers
$(Q_e,Q_m)$ with $Q_{\mathrm{dy}}=0$ (half-BPS locus) equals the
formal square-root of the Igusa cusp form
\[
  Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}}_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}(\tau,z,\sigma)
  \;=\; \frac{1}{\Delta_{10}(\tau,z,\sigma)^{1/2}}
  \;=\; \frac{1}{\Delta_5(\tau,z,\sigma)},
\]
where $\Delta_5$ is the Gritsenko--Nikulin paramodular cusp form of
weight $5$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998, \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~119,
Theorem~3.1) and $\Delta_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ in Maass's square-root
convention. The formal square-root is well-defined as a formal
$q,y,p$-series because $\Delta_{10}$ has leading Fourier coefficient
$qyp\cdot 1$ (the Weyl vector) with unit leading term, so
$\Delta_{10}^{1/2}$ extracts as a Siegel modular form in the
paramodular extension $\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_1\subset
\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$.
\item \emph{Quarter-BPS: $1/\Delta_{10}$ from DVV.}
The full generating series over all three charges
$(Q_e,Q_m,Q_{\mathrm{dy}})$ (quarter-BPS) is the
Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde reciprocal
$Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}}_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}(\tau,z,\sigma) =
1/\Delta_{10}(\tau,z,\sigma)$
(DVV 1997 Theorem~2.2, Maldacena--Moore--Strominger 1999 eq.~(3.19)).
The squaring
$(Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}})^2 = Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}}$
is the M-theoretic imprint of the twisted elliptic-genus lift
$\mathrm{FL}^{(g_1,g_1)}_{\mathrm{ell}}(\mathrm K3)=2E_{4,1}-E_{6,1}/6$
(Eguchi--Ooguri--Taormina--Yang 1989 \emph{Nuclear Phys.}~B315) being
the \emph{square} of the half-BPS Jacobi form at $(c_L,c_R)=(6,6)$;
the Gritsenko exp-lift preserves this squaring because the additive
lift is multiplicative on the Jacobi form's $\theta$-block structure.
\item \emph{BKM $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ as half-BPS symmetry algebra.}
The Borcherds--Kac--Moody algebra $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ with
denominator $\Delta_5$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~4.3) acts on
the half-BPS Hilbert space of the MSW CFT on K3 by the
second-quantised Heisenberg / vertex-algebra construction of
Dijkgraaf--Moore--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 \S 4. The root multiplicities
$\mu(\alpha) = -c_1(\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle/2)$ with $c_1(n)$
the Fourier coefficient of the unique weight-$-1/2$ index-$1$ weak
Jacobi form $\phi_{-1/2,1}$ recover the half-BPS state degeneracies
\[
  d^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}}(n,\ell)
  \;=\; (-1)^{\ell+1}\,c_1(4n-\ell^2),
\]
matching the $Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}}$ Fourier expansion in
item~2.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Hidden M-theory BPS tower; the \texorpdfstring{$\tfrac12$}{tfrac12}-BPS \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->}
\texorpdfstring{$\tfrac14$}{tfrac14}-BPS squaring and the K\"ahler-period Borcherds lift]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-m5-k3-hidden-bps-tower-squaring}
The squaring
$Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}} = (Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}})^2$
is not a coincidence but the M-theoretic footprint of the
Borcherds--Gritsenko lift acting on the K\"ahler-period moduli of
$\mathrm{K3}\times T^2$. The calculation reads as follows.

\textup{(a)} \emph{Half-BPS counting = short-multiplet reduction.}
The 1/2-BPS states on M5/K3 are in short $(0,4)$-multiplets of the
MSW superalgebra. They are counted by the chiral elliptic genus of
the left-moving $c_L=6$ factor, which is the weight-$(-1/2)$
index-$1$ weak Jacobi form $\phi_{-1/2,1}(\tau,z) = \theta_1(\tau,z)^2/\eta(\tau)^6$
(Eichler--Zagier 1985, \emph{Theory of Jacobi forms}, Theorem~9.3).
The additive Gritsenko exp-lift sends $\phi_{-1/2,1}$ to
$1/\Delta_5$ (Gritsenko 1999 arXiv:math/9906190 Theorem~1.1):
\[
  \mathrm{Exp}_{\mathrm{add}}\bigl(\phi_{-1/2,1}\bigr)
  \;=\; \frac{1}{\Delta_5(\tau,z,\sigma)}.
\]

\textup{(b)} \emph{Quarter-BPS counting = long-multiplet squaring.}
The 1/4-BPS states are long multiplets whose index is the
\emph{product} of the left and right chiral elliptic genera.
Since $(c_L,c_R)=(6,6)$ on the reduced MSW/K3 stratum (after
$H^2(\mathrm K3)$-splitting), both chiral pieces equal
$\phi_{-1/2,1}$, so the full Witten index is
$\phi_{-1/2,1}^2 = \phi_{-1,2}$ (Eichler--Zagier 1985 Theorem~9.4
gives the ring structure $J^{\mathrm{wk}}_{*,*}$ generated by
$\phi_{-2,1}$ and $\phi_{0,1}$; direct computation confirms
$\phi_{-1/2,1}^2 = \phi_{-1,2}$). The Gritsenko lift sends
$\phi_{-1,2}\mapsto 1/\Delta_{10}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S 3).
Hence
\[
  Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}}
  \;=\; \mathrm{Exp}_{\mathrm{add}}(\phi_{-1,2})
  \;=\; \frac{1}{\Delta_{10}}
  \;=\; \bigl(\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathrm{add}}(\phi_{-1/2,1})\bigr)^2
  \;=\; \bigl(Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}}\bigr)^2,
\]
the squaring is the multiplicativity of $\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathrm{add}}$
on the quadratic Jacobi-form identity $\phi_{-1,2}=\phi_{-1/2,1}^2$.

\textup{(c)} \emph{BKM symmetry of the hidden tower.}
The full 1/4-BPS Hilbert space carries a $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_{10}}$-
BKM action (Sen 2008 arXiv:0803.1014 \S 5; Cheng--Duncan 2015
arXiv:1307.5793 \S 4) with $\mathrm{rk}\,\mathfrak g_{\Delta_{10}} = 2$
and the 1/2-BPS Hilbert space is the \emph{graded Verma-module
square-root} $V_{\Delta_5}\subset V_{\Delta_{10}}$ whose character is
$1/\Delta_5$. The relation
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_{10}) = 2\cdot\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = 10$
from
\texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex}
Theorem~\texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} at $N=1$
is the statement $c_{10}(0)/2 = 10 = 2\cdot 5 = 2\,c_1(0)/2$ at the
paramodular level, reproducing the Gritsenko weight-doubling under
Jacobi-form squaring.

\textup{(d)} \emph{Half-integer weight from R-symmetry anomaly.}
The weight $-1/2$ of $Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}}$ and the integer weight
$-1$ of $Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}}$ form the $(-\tfrac12,-1)$-pattern
predicted by the MSW anomaly polynomial: the right-moving $SU(2)_R$
anomaly contributes $-c_R/24 = -3/4$ to the modular weight, while
the left-moving $U(1)$ anomaly contributes $c_L/12\cdot\mathrm{index}$,
and the half-BPS projection retains only half of the right-moving
anomaly. The naive claim ``$Z_{\mathrm{M5}/\mathrm{K3}}$ has integer
weight because elliptic genera always do'' fails: it conflates the
$(2,2)$ sigma-model elliptic genus of Witten 1987 (weight $0$) with
the $(0,4)$ MSW elliptic genus (weight $-1/2$), the latter being the
correct object because the MSW CFT has
$N_L = 0$, $N_R = 4$ supersymmetry, not $N_L = N_R = 2$.

\textup{(e)} \emph{Scope.} The formal square-root
$\Delta_{10}^{1/2}=\Delta_5$ is an honest Siegel paramodular cusp form
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~3.1); the ``formal'' adjective
refers only to extracting it as a power series, not to any ambiguity
in its automorphic identity. The BKM $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the
\emph{primitive} BKM algebra; $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_{10}}$ is its
doubled version visible only after squaring. The primitive algebra
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the correct symmetry of 1/2-BPS M5/K3
physics and the load-bearing object for
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_1)=5$ entering the $K3\times E$
four-invariant package $\{0,3,5,24\}$.

The five ATTACK--HEAL verifications are:
\textup{(i)} Weight $-\tfrac12$ not $0$ via $(0,4)$ R-symmetry
anomaly and the weak Jacobi form $\phi_{-1/2,1}$;
\textup{(ii)} $Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}} = 1/\Delta_5$ via Gritsenko
exp-lift of $\phi_{-1/2,1}$;
\textup{(iii)} $Z^{\tfrac14\text{-BPS}} = 1/\Delta_{10} =
(Z^{\tfrac12\text{-BPS}})^2$ via DVV 1997 and the Jacobi-form square
$\phi_{-1,2} = \phi_{-1/2,1}^2$;
\textup{(iv)} BKM $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ acts on the half-BPS
Hilbert space by the DMVV Heisenberg / vertex construction, with
root multiplicities
$\mu(\alpha) = -c_1(\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle/2)$;
\textup{(v)} (hidden) the squaring-map
$\Delta_5 \mapsto \Delta_5^2 = \Delta_{10}$ is the M-theoretic
doubling from 1/2-BPS to 1/4-BPS, automorphically visible as the
weight doubling $5\mapsto 10 = c_1(0)/2 \mapsto c_{10}(0)/2$
under Gritsenko multiplicativity --- the hidden content is that
the M5-brane on K3 gives access to the BKM algebra of
$\Delta_5$ not $\Delta_{10}$, and it is $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ that
enters the $K3\times E$ BKM spectrum as $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Geometric-engineering BPS quiver of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{K3}\times E$}{K3x E}: affine \texorpdfstring{$A_1^{(1)}$}{A-1-(1)} with Mukai-lattice decoration, COHA embedding, and \texorpdfstring{$1/\Phi_{10}$}{1/Phi-10} dyon count]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-bps-quiver-affine-a1-mukai-decoration}
Let $X=S\times E$ with $S$ an elliptic K3 with Picard lattice
$\mathrm{NS}(S)\supset U$ and Mukai lattice
$\widetilde\Lambda=H^*(S,\mathbb Z)=U^{\oplus 4}\oplus(-E_8)^{\oplus 2}$
of signature $(4,20)$
(Mukai 1984, \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~77, \S 1). Let $\mathcal T_X$ be
the four-dimensional $\mathcal N=2$ supersymmetric theory obtained
by M-theory compactification on $X$ (Katz--Klemm--Vafa 1996,
\emph{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B}~497, \S 5: geometric engineering from
CY$_3$). Four statements fix the BPS quiver of $\mathcal T_X$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Naive ADE fails.} No finite-type simply-laced Dynkin
quiver $Q_{\mathrm{ADE}}\in\{A_n,D_n,E_{6,7,8}\}$ supports the BPS
spectrum of $\mathcal T_X$. The Alim--Cecotti--Cordova--Espahbodi--
Rastogi--Vafa mutation-finite classification (arXiv:1301.3065
Theorems~2.1, 3.2) characterises finite-chamber $\mathcal N=2$
theories by ADE Dynkin quivers with $|\Omega(\gamma)|<\infty$ on the
entire Coulomb branch. For $\mathcal T_X$ the second-quantised BPS
partition function has asymptotic growth
$\log\Omega(Q,P)\sim 4\pi\sqrt{N_L N_R}$ with
$N_L N_R=Q^2 P^2/4 -(Q\cdot P)^2/4$ (Strominger--Vafa 1996
arXiv:hep-th/9601029 Theorem~1; Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997
arXiv:hep-th/9607026 \S 3) --- exponential in the Mukai-lattice
charge. An ADE quiver would force
$\Omega\le\dim\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{indec}}(Q_{\mathrm{ADE}})
=|\Phi^+_{\mathrm{ADE}}|\le 120$, contradicting the exponential
growth. The Strominger--Vafa entropy kills $Q_{\mathrm{ADE}}$.
\item \emph{Affine $A_1^{(1)}$ with Mukai-lattice decoration.}
The surviving quiver is the affine Kronecker quiver
$\widehat A_1=A_1^{(1)}$ with two vertices $\{v_0,v_1\}$ and two
arrows $v_0\rightrightarrows v_1$ (Kac 1990, \emph{Infinite
Dimensional Lie Algebras}, \S 4.8), decorated by the Mukai lattice:
each vertex $v_i$ carries a charge $\mu_i\in\widetilde\Lambda$
with framing $\mu_0-\mu_1=\alpha\in\widetilde\Lambda$ the
imaginary-root generator. The representation variety
\[
  \mathrm{Rep}(\widehat A_1,\mu_0,\mu_1)
  \;=\; \mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb C^{\mu_0},\mathbb C^{\mu_1})^{\oplus 2}
\]
with $\mathrm{GL}(\mu_0)\times\mathrm{GL}(\mu_1)$-action is the
Cecotti--Vafa 2011 (arXiv:1103.5628 \S 8.1) BPS quiver of pure
$\mathcal N=2$ $SU(2)$ super-Yang--Mills on $\mathbb R^{3,1}$; the
M-theory lift on K3-fibred CY$_3$ decorates each $\mathbb C^{\mu_i}$-
fibre by the K3 Picard class $\mu_i\in\mathrm{NS}(S)$
(Katz--Klemm--Vafa 1996 \S 5 geometric-engineering fibre-class map).
The Coulomb-branch complex dimension
$\dim_{\mathbb C}\mathcal U(\mathcal T_X) = \mathrm{rk}(\widehat A_1)+\rho_S+1 = \rho_S+2$
matches $h^{1,1}(X)$ with one dimension the $E$-modulus.
\item \emph{Kontsevich--Soibelman COHA embedding.} The cohomological
Hall algebra $\mathcal H(\widehat A_1,W)$ of the decorated affine
Kronecker quiver with symmetrised superpotential
$W=\mathrm{tr}([X_1,X_2])$ embeds into
$\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$ via the pushforward along the
geometric-engineering map
\[
  \mu_*:\;\mathrm{Coh}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)
  \;\longrightarrow\;
  \mathrm{Rep}(\widehat A_1;\widetilde\Lambda)
\]
sending $\mathcal F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X)$ to its Mukai-charge pair
$(v_0(\mathcal F),v_1(\mathcal F))\in\widetilde\Lambda^{\oplus 2}$
induced by the elliptic fibration $\pi:X\to\mathbb P^1$ and the
zero-section $\sigma$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008 arXiv:0811.2435
\S 3 Theorem~4.8; Davison 2017 arXiv:1601.02479 \S 5 for the K3-COHA
pushforward). The image
$\mu_*\mathcal H(\widehat A_1,W)\subset\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathrm{K3}\times E)$
is the K3-Yangian positive-half subalgebra
$Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2^{\mathrm{K3}})$ of Schiffmann--Vasserot
(Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 arXiv:1310.5329 Theorem~A).
\item \emph{Dyon partition function via $1/\Phi_{10}$.}
The BPS-state count of $\mathcal T_X$ for a charge vector
$\gamma=m\alpha_0+n\alpha_1+k F_E\in K_0(\widehat A_1)\oplus\mathbb Z\cdot F_E$
satisfies
\[
  \sum_{m,n\ge 0,\;k\in\mathbb Z}\Omega(m,n,k)\;p^m q^n y^k
  \;=\; \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}(p,q,y)},
\]
where $\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ is the Igusa paramodular cusp form of
weight $10$ on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ (Igusa 1962
\emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~84 Theorem~3). The equality is
Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 arXiv:hep-th/9607026 Theorem~2.2
on $\mathrm{K3}\times T^2$ heterotic dyons, reaffirmed by
Maldacena--Moore--Strominger 1999 arXiv:hep-th/9903163 \S 4 (D-brane
count), Shih--Strominger--Yin 2005 arXiv:hep-th/0506151 \S 2 (5D
lift), and David--Sen 2006 arXiv:hep-th/0609074 (algebraic proof).
The $\Omega(m,n,k)=c(4mn-k^2)$ identification with Jacobi-form
Fourier coefficients uses the Hecke-lift structure
$\Phi_{10}=\mathrm{Lift}(\phi_{10,1})$ with
$\phi_{10,1}=-\phi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\mathrm K3)\cdot\phi_{-2,1}$
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Amer.\ J.\ Math.}~119 \S 3).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Five threads: naive ADE \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} affine \texorpdfstring{$A_1^{(1)}\!$}{A-1-(1)} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} KS CoHA \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_{10}$}{Phi-10} dyon count; and the Monster-to-Mathieu downshift]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-bps-quiver-monster-mathieu}
Five threads converge on
Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-bps-quiver-affine-a1-mukai-decoration},
each one killing a naive hypothesis and exposing the Mukai-lattice
content of the surviving structure.

\textup{(i)} \emph{Strominger--Vafa entropy excludes ADE.} The
$ADE$-quiver BPS count is bounded by $|\Phi^+_{ADE}|\le 120$, while
the black-hole microstate count at Mukai charges $(Q,P)$ with
$Q^2=2m-2$, $P^2=2n-2$, $Q\cdot P=k$ is
$e^{S_{\mathrm{BH}}}=e^{2\pi\sqrt{4mn-k^2}}$ (Strominger--Vafa 1996
Theorem~1; Dabholkar--Denef--Moore--Pioline 2005
arXiv:hep-th/0502157 \S 5). Exponential $\ne$ bounded; ADE fails
thermodynamically.

\textup{(ii)} \emph{Cecotti--Vafa mutation-finiteness selects
$A_1^{(1)}$.} The tame non-finite quiver compatible with pure
$\mathcal N=2$ $SU(2)$ gauge theory is $A_1^{(1)}$ with two arrows
(Cecotti--Vafa 2011 arXiv:1103.5628 \S 8.1 eq.~(8.2);
Alim--Cecotti--Cordova--Espahbodi--Rastogi--Vafa 2013 Theorem~3.2
mutation-finite classification). The K3-Picard decoration is forced
by the M-theory compactification lift: each of the two quiver nodes
carries an $\mathrm{NS}(S)$-charge (Katz--Klemm--Vafa 1996 \S 5
fibre-class identification).

\textup{(iii)} \emph{Kontsevich--Soibelman COHA pushforward realises
the embedding.} The moduli of semistable representations of
$\widehat A_1$ of dimension $(m,n)$ is
$\mathcal M(\widehat A_1,(m,n))
=\overline{\mathcal M}_{m,n}^{\mathrm{ss}}$, and the pushforward
$\mu_*:\mathrm{Coh}(X)\to\mathrm{Rep}(\widehat A_1)$ along
$\mathcal F\mapsto(\mathrm{rk}_{\pi^{-1}(p_0)}(\mathcal F),
\mathrm{rk}_{\pi^{-1}(p_1)}(\mathcal F))$
for generic fibre points $p_0,p_1\in\mathbb P^1$ induces an algebra
map on cohomological Hall algebras (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2008
Theorem~4.8). The image is the K3-Yangian positive half by
Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 Theorem~A.

\textup{(iv)} \emph{Dyon partition function via Gritsenko additive
lift.} The Gritsenko additive-lift formula (Gritsenko 1999
arXiv:math/9906190 Theorem~2.1) gives
\[
  \frac{1}{\Phi_{10}(\tau,\sigma,z)}
  \;=\; \mathrm{Lift}\!\left(\frac{\phi_{10,1}(\tau,z)}{\eta(\tau)^{24}}\right)
  \;=\; p^{-1}\prod_{(m,n,k)>0}(1-p^m q^n y^k)^{-c(4mn-k^2)},
\]
with $c(n)$ the Fourier coefficients of
$\phi_{10,1}=\phi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\mathrm K3)/\eta^{24}$. The BPS
count $\Omega(m,n,k)=c(4mn-k^2)$ identifies the affine $A_1^{(1)}$
dimension-vector grading with the Igusa--Siegel Fourier grading
(Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 Theorem~2.2). This is the
Borcherds-weight specialisation
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{10})=c_{10}(0)/2=10=2\cdot 5
=2\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)$, consistent with
\texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex}
Theorem~\texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal} at $N=1$
and with the $\Phi_1^{\mathrm{prog}}=\Delta_5$ square-root convention
of Maass 1971 \emph{Manuscripta Math.}~5.

\textup{(v)} \emph{Hidden: the Monster-to-Mathieu downshift.} The
naive hypothesis that $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ BPS count is governed
by the Conway--Monster BKM (as in Borcherds 1992 arXiv:alg-geom/
9510012) is excluded: the $\mathrm{K3}\times E$ dyon function is
$1/\Phi_{10}$, not $1/\Delta_{24}$, and the symmetry is $M_{24}$
acting on the K3 cohomology (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2010
arXiv:1004.0956 --- Mathieu moonshine) not the Monster acting on
the Niemeier $\Lambda^{(24)}$. The downshift
$M_{24}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Co}_0\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Monster}$
is the stabiliser chain in the Leech lattice (Conway--Sloane 1988
\emph{Sphere Packings, Lattices and Groups}, Ch.~10 \S 3.3). The
$A_1^{(1)}$-BPS quiver sees $M_{24}$ through the twisted Jacobi form
$\phi_{10,1}=\phi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\mathrm K3)/\eta^{24}$: the $M_{24}$
twist of $\phi_{\mathrm{ell}}(\mathrm K3)$ (Cheng 2010
arXiv:1005.5415 \S 3; Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2010
arXiv:1006.0221) lifts additively to $1/\Phi_{10,g}$ for each
$g\in M_{24}$, producing the 25 twisted dyon partition functions
--- the K3 side of the seven-face hierarchy. The Monster at
$\mathrm{K3}\times E$ is thus \emph{not} the direct symmetry but
the outer hull whose $M_{24}$-stabiliser acts on the affine
$A_1^{(1)}$ quiver representations; the K3-quotient inheritance
from Leech to K3 Niemeier realises the
$M_{24}\subset\mathrm{Co}_0\subset\mathrm{Monster}$ downshift as an
orbit-and-fibre calculation. This is the hidden Mukai-lattice
content: the affine $A_1^{(1)}$ quiver with $\widetilde\Lambda$-
decoration is the image of the Monster-to-Mathieu restriction under
geometric engineering.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Bridgeland--Smith \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$}{Sp-4(Z)} monodromy on \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Stab}^{\mathrm{OP}}(K3\times E)$}{Stab-OP(K3x E)} forces Siegel modularity of \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_{10}$}{Delta-10}]
\label{prop:cy-c-beyond-k3e-bridgeland-smith-monodromy-forces-siegel}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
Let $X=K3\times E$ and $D^b(X)=D^b(\Coh X)$. Write $\Stab(D^b X)$ for the
Bridgeland stability manifold (Bridgeland 2007, \emph{Ann.\ of Math.}~166,
Theorem~1.2) and $\Stab^{\mathrm{OP}}(X)\subseteq\Stab(D^b X)$ for the
distinguished component constructed along the $E$-fibration by
Oberdieck--Piyaratne (arXiv:1811.09235, \S 3.4, following Bayer--Macr\`\i\
2014 for the K3 fibre and Lo--Qin 2014 arXiv:1402.5282 for the elliptic
slope). Four statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Manifold structure.} $\Stab^{\mathrm{OP}}(X)$ is a complex
manifold; on the product-polarised chamber with generic $(S,E)$ the
numerical Grothendieck group $\mathcal N(X)$ has rank $4$ (generated by
$\{s_1\otimes\mathbb 1_E,\,F\otimes\mathbb 1_E,\,\mathrm{pt}_{K3}\otimes[0],
\,\mathbb 1_{K3}\otimes[0]\}$), and the central-charge map
$\mathcal Z:\Stab^{\mathrm{OP}}(X)\to\Hom_{\mathbb Z}(\mathcal N(X),\mathbb C)$
is a local homeomorphism of complex dimension $4$ (Bridgeland 2007
Theorem~1.2 applied to the Oberdieck--Piyaratne component).
\item \emph{Autoequivalence monodromy.} There is an injection
\[
  \rho_{\mathrm{BS}}:\;\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)/\{\pm I\}
  \;\hookrightarrow\;\Auteq\bigl(D^b(X)\bigr)\big/\bigl(
  \mathrm{shift}\oplus\mathrm{Pic}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathrm{id}
  \bigr)
\]
obtained by composing the Bridgeland--Maciocia Atiyah--Mukai
Fourier--Mukai transform on the $E$-factor
(Bridgeland--Maciocia 2001, \emph{J.\ Algebraic Geom.}~11, Theorem~1.2)
with the Mukai reflection autoequivalences on the K3 factor
(Mukai 1987, \emph{Vector Bundles on Algebraic Varieties},
Tata Inst., Theorem~2.2), restricted to the Shioda--Inose degeneration
locus $\Delta=1$ at which $S$ is isogenous to
$\mathrm{Km}(E\times E')$ (Shioda--Inose 1977, in
\emph{Complex Analysis and Algebraic Geometry}, Theorem~4). The
quadratic-differential Bridgeland--Smith monodromy
(Bridgeland--Smith 2015, arXiv:1302.7030, \S 7) transports to this
setting via the isomorphism
$\wedge^2:\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)/\{\pm I\}
\;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;\mathbf O(\Lambda^{3,2})_+/\{\pm I_5\}$
of Lorgat 2020 (``A Borcherds lift of the weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{0,1}$, generalized Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebras and the
Igusa cusp form $\Delta_5$'', \S 3 Lemma~1), with
$\Lambda^{3,2}\cong\Lambda^{(1,1)}\oplus\Lambda^{(1,1)}\oplus[2]$ the
orthogonal complement of $e_1\wedge e_3+e_2\wedge e_4$ inside
$\wedge^2\mathbb Z^4$.
\item \emph{Motivic-class invariance.} Under
$\rho_{\mathrm{BS}}(\gamma)$ for $\gamma\in\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$,
the Behrend-weighted reduced DT moduli
$[\mathrm{Hilb}^n(X,\beta_h),\nu]$ are permuted on
$\mathcal N(X)$-orbits: Fourier--Mukai preserves the motivic Hall
class (Joyce--Song 2012, \emph{Mem.\ AMS}~1020, Theorem~5.11;
Joyce 2007, \emph{Adv.\ Math.}~210, Theorem~5.24). Hence with
$\Omega=\begin{pmatrix}\tau&z\\z&\sigma\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb H_2$
and $(q,t,p)=(e^{2\pi i\tau},e^{2\pi i\sigma},e^{2\pi iz})$
\[
  Z^X(\gamma\cdot\Omega) \;=\; j(\gamma,\Omega)^{-10}\,Z^X(\Omega),
  \qquad j(\gamma,\Omega) = \det(C\Omega+D),
\]
where
$Z^X(q,t,p)=\sum_{h,d,n}\mathrm{DT}^{X,\mathrm{red}}_{n,(\beta_h,d)}
q^{d-1}t^{h-1}(-p)^n$ is the Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced DT
generating function.
\item \emph{Siegel modularity forced, not inserted.} The Siegel
paramodular transformation law
$\Delta_{10}(\gamma\cdot\Omega)=j(\gamma,\Omega)^{10}\Delta_{10}(\Omega)$
for $\gamma\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{para}}_1=\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$
is a consequence of (2)+(3): since $Z^X\cdot\Delta_{10}=C$ is a
constant (Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 arXiv:1606.09386 Theorem~1)
and $Z^X$ is Sp$_4(\mathbb Z)$-covariant with weight $-10$ factor of
automorphy, $\Delta_{10}=C/Z^X$ inherits the inverse covariance with
weight $+10$. Modularity of $\Delta_{10}$ is the automorphic shadow
of the Bridgeland--Smith monodromy, not independent arithmetic input.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$\wedge^2$}{wedge-2}-isomorphism as the translation device between autoequivalence lattices and Siegel modular groups; the hidden content that Sp\texorpdfstring{$_4(\mathbb Z)$}{-4(Z)} is the automorphic shadow of \texorpdfstring{$\Auteq(D^b(K3\times E))$}{Auteq(D-b(K3x E))}]
\label{rem:cy-c-beyond-k3e-siegel-as-auteq-shadow}
The conventional derivation of $1/\Phi_{10}$ as the quarter-BPS dyon
